# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What This Is

A self-evolving coding agent CLI built on [yoagent](https://github.com/yologdev/yoagent). The agent spans multiple Rust source files under `src/`. A GitHub Actions cron job (`scripts/evolve.sh`) runs the agent on a schedule using a 3-phase pipeline (plan → implement → respond), which reads its own source, picks improvements, implements them, and commits — if tests pass. All runs use a flat 3h gap (~8/day; was 8h until Day 162, tightened once sessions dropped from ~3h to ~50-60min). Sponsors get benefit tiers (issue priority, shoutout issues) but no run-frequency speedup. Every sponsor, any amount, is listed in README.md permanently — listings never expire and are never pruned (creator decision 2026-07-13; the old accelerated-run credits and the 90-day listing/grace windows are retired).

**Sponsor benefit tiers:**

All sponsors (any amount, recurring or one-time): permanent README listing.

Monthly recurring:
- $5/mo: Issue priority (💖)
- $10/mo: Priority + shoutout issue

One-time (cumulative):
- $5: Issue priority (14 days)
- $10: Above + shoutout issue (30 days)
- $1,000 💎 Genesis: Permanent priority + top billing + journal acknowledgment

## Build & Test Commands

```bash
cargo build              # Build
cargo test               # Run tests
cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings   # Lint (CI treats warnings as errors)
cargo fmt -- --check     # Format check
cargo fmt                # Auto-format
```

CI runs all four checks (build, test, clippy with -D warnings, fmt check) on PR to main. A separate Pages workflow builds and deploys the website on push to main.

**Pre-push hook** (`.githooks/pre-push`, tracked, opt-in per clone — `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`): lints the **tip commit of each pushed ref** with `scripts/lint_evolve_heredocs.py` and refuses the push if it fails. The rule: no apostrophe inside a `${VAR:+...}` / `${VAR:-...}` block in `scripts/evolve.sh`, because bash interprets single quotes in a parameter-expansion word and one apostrophe yields `bad substitution: no closing }`. `evolve.sh` then dies **at that heredoc, mid-session** — agents before it have already spent their budget and every phase after it never runs (`d93e4f65` broke the Phase A2 planner prompt, so the A1 assessment agent had already run; `9847db2`'s instance was in the journal prompt near the end). It has **landed twice** — `988975d9` introduced it and `9847db2` fixed it after two symptom-chasing attempts (`cb9d9b0`, `25f4e90`); `d93e4f65` landed it again and `050e300c` fixed it. `scripts/lint_evolve_heredocs.py`'s docstring is the authoritative history — that count has already drifted once, so correct it there and point here.

Why a hook when CI runs the same lint: CI only runs *after* the push, which is the event this prevents; and because the lint is CI's first check step, a failure there means nothing else in the job ran on that commit either — not the harness tests, not build/test/clippy/fmt. (On a branch that is neither `main` nor a PR to `main`, CI does not run at all.) `bash -n` does **not** catch it — the expansion only fails at runtime. It checks pushed commits rather than the working tree because the tree-based first version passed a push whose commit was broken while the tree was already fixed but unstaged — exactly the state you occupy while fixing this bug. Failures are separated: a real violation says "fix the above, or `--no-verify`", while a missing linter or `python3` says the file is **UNVERIFIED** and that `--no-verify` would push it unchecked ("could not check" must not read as "checked; clean"). **Covers local pushes by whoever ran the opt-in — not the bot**, which pushes from CI with no `hooksPath`; that is tolerable only because `scripts/evolve.sh` is a protected file the agent cannot modify, with CI as the backstop. Keep the hook to checks well under a second (currently ~80ms) — everything slow stays in CI.

**Module-size gate** (`tests/module_size.rs`): a deterministic line-count check over `src/`, holding `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` plus a `GRANDFATHERED_OVERSIZED_MODULES` debt register of `(file, recorded_lines)` entries. Day 165 split it into **three branches that are not the same property**, because as one assertion it destroyed two whole correct tasks (#719; #739, which died to a **four-line** overshoot — a `cargo test` failure means `git reset --hard` in `scripts/evolve.sh`, so the gate's real price was never the sentence it asked for):

The three branches, and the two directions they run in:

1. **Un-grandfathered module over `MAX_MODULE_LINES` → depends on *how far* over** (Day 166, #762 — this is the reprice; Day 165's three-way split was a taxonomy change that left the penalty untouched and ate a third task the next day). The branch is split by `OVERSHOOT_GRACE_LINES = 50`, a judgment threshold, not a measurement:
   - over the cap by **≤ 50 lines → warning, run stays green** (`UnlistedVerdict::Grace` → `SizeViolation::OverCapWithinGrace`). A fix that adds a handful of lines to an already-large module is not the design event this rule was written for, and reverting the whole task teaches nothing the warning cannot. The message carries the same shape and actionability as branch 2's: file, actual lines, `MAX_MODULE_LINES`, the overshoot, and the literal `("path", N)` register line to paste in.
   - over the cap by **> 50 lines → fatal**, as before (`UnlistedVerdict::Fatal` → `SizeViolation::OverCap`). A module blowing 50+ lines past the cap in one task *is* the design event, and the message names the grace band it exceeded so the reader can tell which of the two branches they hit and why.

   The decision half is the pure, table-tested `classify_unlisted(lines, max_lines, grace) -> UnlistedVerdict` (three values — `Ok`/`Grace`/`Fatal`; the boundary is inclusive, `+50` warns and `+51` is fatal) so the I/O stays at the single call site. **The tradeoff is accepted deliberately, not overlooked:** the grace band lets an un-grandfathered module creep up to 2050 lines without stopping a task. What keeps that visible rather than free is the rest of the gate — every grace-band pass prints the register line to paste, and once a file lands in the register the branch-3 ratchet means it can never shrink unnoticed. `MAX_MODULE_LINES` was **not** raised and the invariant was **not** deleted; only the penalty for crossing it by a little was repriced from "revert the entire task" to "say so loudly".
2. **Grandfathered module *above* its recorded lines → depends on *how far* over** (Day 174 — the same reprice Day 166 applied to branch 1, one branch over, split by `REGISTER_DRIFT_GRACE_LINES = 100`, a judgment threshold, not a measurement). Drift of **≤ 100 lines → warning, run stays green** (`ListedVerdict::Grace` → `SizeViolation::GrewPastCeiling`): growth of an already-capped module is information, not an emergency, and a four-line overshoot does not deserve a whole-task revert. Drift of **> 100 → fatal** (`ListedVerdict::Fatal` → `SizeViolation::GrewFarPastCeiling`), because a single task rarely adds 100 lines to an already-oversized module, so a crossing is a real event. Both messages name the file, both numbers, the overshoot, and the literal `("path", N)` entry to paste back; the fatal one also names the band it exceeded, so a reader can tell which half they hit. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `classify_listed(lines, recorded, grace) -> ListedVerdict` (three values — `NotGrowth`/`Grace`/`Fatal`; the boundary is inclusive, `+100` warns and `+101` is fatal; `NotGrowth` deliberately says nothing about at-or-below-recorded, which is branch 3's business), with the I/O at the single call site.

   **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased** (Day-165 rule): this bullet used to end "so the register is still updated on purpose rather than absorbed". That was **false for eight days**. Measured Day 174: **11 register entries had absorbed drift**, from +1 to +480 lines, worst `src/cli.rs` (recorded 3845, actual 4325); all 11 were paid off in the same task that landed the split. The mechanism is my own "a capability is real only where something consumes it" lesson landing on my own gate — the warning goes to the stderr of a *passing* test, and the only consumer of `cargo test` in the evolve loop reads the **exit code**, so nothing read it and nothing acted on it. Making large drift fatal hands the branch the one reader that already exists: `scripts/evolve.sh`'s fix loop, whose remedy here is a single pasted line. **The damage was to branch 3, and it is worth stating precisely because it is narrower than it sounds**: a stale-high entry does not *break* the ratchet, it *loosens* it — `cli.rs` could have shed 480 lines and the ratchet would never have fired, i.e. silent headroom nobody granted. **The tradeoff is deliberate, not an oversight:** drift under 100 lines is still absorbable. This is not a crusade against every +1 — the +1/+2/+3 creep that made up most of the Day-174 list stays a warning precisely so it never reverts a correct task, which is the whole reason Day 165 made this branch non-fatal in the first place.
3. **Grandfathered module *below* its recorded lines → fatal**, as is a listed file that dropped under the cap or vanished. This is the ratchet: an exception list only pays itself down if improving is *also* a failure, otherwise a shrink leaves silent headroom nobody granted. Fatal on purpose and the cheap direction — the message states the smaller number verbatim. (Landing this found one live instance: `src/dispatch.rs` was recorded at 2307 against an actual 2296.)

Both non-fatal branches (1's grace band and 2's) share **one** writer, `write_warnings`, so the two warnings cannot drift; it writes through a raw `std::io::stderr()` handle, **not** `eprintln!`, because libtest's capture hook intercepts the `print!`/`eprint!` macros and discards output from *passing* tests — which is exactly what a non-fatal branch produces. **Verified by running it** — on Day 165 for branch 2 (a grandfathered entry temporarily lowered by 4 lines, then restored) and again on Day 166 for the grace band (a real module temporarily padded 6 lines past the cap, then restored): the warning appears in a plain `cargo test` and in `cargo test --test module_size`, with no `--nocapture` needed. A silent gate would be worse than a fatal one, since it stops teaching anything — **and eight days of that silence is exactly what Day 174 had to pay off, so "visible" was never the same property as "read".** Day 174's new fatal half was verified the same way, in both directions: a register entry temporarily lowered by 193 lines fails the run with the pasteable remedy line, and restoring it returns green; the unit half proves the fatal branch against a **fabricated** `(file, recorded)` pair rather than by planting a real oversized module in `src/`, the same discipline `tests/orphan_modules.rs` uses.

**Blind-round grading gate** (`tests/blind_round_grades.rs`, Day 173, #801 option 2): a deterministic check over `dreams/experiments.jsonl` built in the same shape as the module-size gate above — debt register, three branches that are not the same property, raw-stderr warning writer, remedy messages that print the literal line to paste. A blind round writes **two** ledger lines: a `type:"experiment"` prediction carrying `hypotheses: [...]` and a later `type:"experiment_result"` grade carrying `hypothesis_grades: [...]`. The prediction kept landing and the grade kept not landing, and a prediction alone is a non-empty diff that builds and tests green — so the harness *accepted* half-finished rounds. The cost is not cosmetic: `latest_study_state_by_path` maps an ungraded round's target to `StudyState::VisitedUngraded`, which since #744/#711 counts as **"not dark"**, so `never_forecast_files` stops listing the file as an unexplored room and `study_tier` ranks it above genuinely unstudied files — a round that taught nothing steers the exploration budget away from itself. Day 172 shipped option 3 (make it *visible* — `src/commands_risk_ungraded.rs`, surfaced under `/risk epistemic`); this is option 2, making the half-written state **fail a check** instead of passing one.

**The key is `(round, target)`, not `round`** — the same key and the same measured reason as `src/commands_risk_ungraded.rs`: round numbers are hand-assigned (nothing derives `max(round)+1`) and they have collided (57 on day 169 for `src/docs.rs` and again on day 171 for `src/commands_plan.rs`; 58 on day 169 for `src/update.rs` and again on day 172 for `src/config_paths.rs`), so a query keyed on `round` alone lets the graded twin answer for its ungraded namesake. A line with no `round` field keeps the explicit name `(no round)` rather than being unkeyable — the pre-`round`-field rounds of days 150–153 are real rounds.

The three branches, and the two directions they run in:

1. **A round *not* in `GRANDFATHERED_UNGRADED_ROUNDS` with `registered > 0` and `graded == 0` → fatal.** The exact state #801 names. The message gives **both** remedies verbatim: grade it in this same pass, or paste the literal `("<round>", "<target>", <registered>, <graded>)` line into the register. The escape hatch *is* the point — **the gate does not forbid an ungraded round, it forbids an unnamed one** (Day-166 module-size lesson: a gate that can only revert a whole task eats the correct work sitting beside it, so the compliant escape is one line in a register that is itself the enumeration of the debt).
2. **A round not in the register with `0 < graded < registered` → warning, run stays green.** Partial grading is information, not an emergency; a partially graded round did teach something. Same message shape (round, target, both counts, the register line to paste), written through a raw `std::io::stderr()` handle and **not** `eprintln!`, because libtest's capture hook discards macro output from *passing* tests. Verified by running it: the warning appears in a plain `cargo test`, no `--nocapture`.
3. **A *registered* round whose counts no longer match the recorded pair → fatal, in either direction** (`RegisteredRoundNowGraded` — the debt is paid, delete the entry; `RegisteredCountsChanged`; `RegisteredRoundVanished`). This is the ratchet, running the opposite way from branches 1–2: an exception list only pays itself down if *improving* is also a failure, otherwise progress leaves silent headroom nobody granted. The message states the recorded numbers and the actual numbers verbatim, so the fix is a one-line edit.

The register is **debt, not absolution** — an entry does not make a round graded, it records by hand that a prediction shipped without its grade and that I knew; branch 3 is what stops it from becoming permission. It can only shrink. Lines that fail to parse, and lines with no `target` to key on, are **counted and reported** in the warning rather than silently dropped (a shrinking denominator inside my own meter is the defect I keep fixing elsewhere), and a **missing ledger file is its own explicit state** — said out loud, and passing, because "missing" is not "clean". **What it cannot do, stated plainly: it checks that a grade is *present*, never that it is *correct*.** A round graded with four rubber-stamp "hit" strings passes exactly as an honest one does; presence is mechanically checkable, honesty is not, and claiming otherwise would be the "could not check" → "checked; clean" reading the pre-push hook already refuses to make.

**Orphan-module gate** (`tests/orphan_modules.rs`, Day 173, #804): the smallest of the three, in the same shape — walk `src/`, classify, fatal on the unnamed case, register for deliberate exceptions, ratchet the register. It exists because `src/commands_risk_families.rs` was a tracked, 296-line source file that **rustc never saw**: no `mod` declaration named it, so `cargo build`, `cargo test`, `clippy -D warnings` and `fmt --check` were all structurally blind to it, and it could be committed, reviewed and reported as a delivered extraction while never being part of the program. Every `*.rs` under `src/` must be named by some `mod NAME;` (`src/main.rs` is the crate root and reachable by definition; `src/x/mod.rs` is reached by `mod x;`). Two branches, running in opposite directions: an **un-registered file no `mod` reaches → fatal**, with both remedies verbatim (add `mod <name>;` to the right parent, usually `src/main.rs`, or paste the literal `("src/path.rs", "<reason>")` line) — the escape hatch is the point, **the gate does not forbid an uncompiled file, it forbids an unnamed one**; and a **registered file that is now reachable or has vanished → fatal**, the ratchet, since an exception list only pays itself down if improving is also a failure. The register (`REGISTERED_ORPHANS`) ships **empty** — the live orphan was **deleted, not registered** (it duplicated code already live and tested in `src/commands_risk_epistemic.rs`), because registering it would have laundered the exact defect the gate exists to catch. Decision and I/O are split as in the siblings: the pure, table-tested `classify(files, declared_mods, register)` with all filesystem walking at the single call site, and the fatal branch is proven against a **fabricated** file list rather than by planting a real orphan. **Three stated limits, printed on every passing run through a raw `std::io::stderr()` handle** (libtest discards macro output from passing tests) so "could not check" cannot read as "checked; clean": it matches `mod NAME;` **by name, not by resolved path**, so two files sharing a stem in different directories are indistinguishable and one declaration can vouch for the wrong file; it does **not** parse `#[cfg(...)]` or `#[path]`, so a `mod` behind a disabled feature counts as reachable — deliberate, since `src/gasp.rs` is `#[cfg(feature = "gasp")]` and genuinely *is* part of the crate, but it means "reachable" here means **declared**, not compiled in this configuration; and it says nothing about whether the code inside is *used* — clippy's `dead_code` covers that, once the file is compiled at all.

**Dependency-version-claim gate** (`tests/doc_version_claims.rs`, Day 177, #821; was `tests/gasp_doc_version.rs`): the convention is that **any prose claim asserting what an external dependency *cannot* do carries a `yoagent-version-claim` marker naming the yoagent version it was checked against**, and this test scans every `*.rs` under `src/` plus `CLAUDE.md` and pins every marker to the version `Cargo.lock` resolves for `yoagent`. It exists because `src/gasp.rs` asserted `task-result` was *unreachable* — true against 0.16.3, false the moment the pin moved to 0.16.4/0.16.5 — and nothing detected it: **eight sessions** (#763, #765, #782, #785, #787, #789, #803, plus the docs-only #788) opened the file, read an authoritative "impossible", and exited with an empty diff. Prose has no compiler; a test is the only reader that does not believe it. Two invariants, and the second is the anti-vacuous one: every marker agrees with `Cargo.lock` (the failure names file, line, claimed version, resolved version, and *re-read the paragraph above this marker*), and **at least one marker exists** — a scanner that finds nothing and passes is the vacuous-green shape, so deleting the convention must fail loudly rather than go quiet. There are currently **two** live markers, both in `src/gasp.rs`: the `task-result` reachability paragraph, and the `MemoryEventStore` claim that is the stated reason no `*_in` body has a behavioural test. **What it cannot do, stated rather than implied:** a marker proves *someone looked at the current version*, never that the prose is correct — presence is mechanically checkable, truth is not, and a flatly wrong paragraph with a current marker passes exactly as an honest one does (the same limit `tests/blind_round_grades.rs` states about grades). Deliberately **not** done: no blanket scan for "impossible"/"unreachable" wording (it would fire on the many honest uses and train the next reader to ignore the gate), and no markers on claims about yoyo's *own* code, which would pin a sentence to a version that has nothing to do with it.

**Shared-global test-race gate** (`tests/global_state_races.rs`, Day 177): a deterministic scan over `src/` built in the same shape as the four gates above — pure table-tested classifier with all filesystem walking at one call site, fatal on the *unnamed* case, a debt register for deliberate exceptions, a ratchet, and the limits printed on every passing run through a raw `std::io::stderr()` handle. It exists because **three CI reds in one window were one class, and each was fixed as a one-site repair**: `CONVERSATION_STASH` shared across 14 stash tests (fixed by @yuanhao in `04c6d252`), the cwd-moving tests (#780 — two tasks), and `EFFORT_LEVEL`, an `AtomicU8` three `apply_effort_hint` tests each wrote, which failed **6 of 12** runs under libtest's default parallelism (Day 177, CI run `32748888895`). Nothing enumerated the remainder, so the class kept re-appearing as a surprise red — and a flaky red in the evolve loop means `git reset --hard` on whatever correct work was sitting beside it. My own archive, verbatim: *"when a mitigation's protection is collective, applying it to the N known offenders gives an N/N counter that feels like closure while the property stays false."* Three one-site fixes were exactly that counter; this gate buys the enumeration.

**The rule:** a `#[test]` / `#[tokio::test]` in `src/` whose body directly calls one of the 40 names in `GLOBAL_SETTERS` must carry `#[serial]` or be named in `REGISTERED_GLOBAL_RACES`. Two branches running in opposite directions: an **unserialised, unregistered writer → fatal**, with all three remedies verbatim (pass the value explicitly — stated as the *best* one, since `#[serial]` leaves unmarked readers exposed; add `#[serial]`; or paste the literal `("src/path.rs", "test_name"),` line), because **the gate does not forbid an unserialised global write, it forbids an unnamed one** (the Day-166 module-size lesson: a gate whose only remedy is a whole-task revert eats the correct work beside the violation); and a **registered test that stopped violating, or vanished → also fatal**, the ratchet, since an exception list only pays itself down if *improving* is a failure too. The key is `(path, test_name)`, not `test_name` — pinned by test, since names repeat across modules. `GLOBAL_SETTERS` is **hand-written** and tied to reality by a second test (`every_named_setter_still_exists`): a rename fails *that* test rather than silently un-covering every site the name guarded — the same two-direction discipline as `ROUTED_SUBCOMMANDS`. It is **anti-vacuous**: if no test anywhere is seen calling any setter, the gate fails loudly rather than passing on an empty scan (verified by running it against a one-name list). Test bodies are delimited by **brace matching** over a scanner that skips line/block comments (nesting), char literals *without* mistaking a lifetime for one, and normal *and* raw strings — not a fixed byte window, which is what inflated Phase A1's crude estimate of ~74 candidate sites.

**The measurement, which was the deliverable — and it is the opposite of the estimate.** The reading the gate landed on, stated as the dated observation it is (Day 177, the task that built the gate): **5,113 tests scanned; 98 call a global setter directly; 81 already carry `#[serial]`; 17 do not** — the convention followed 83% of the time, and 17 named tests across 6 files (`cli_config.rs` 3, `commands_run.rs` 3, `commands_session.rs` 4, `conversations.rs` 1, `format/mod.rs` 5, `format/tools.rs` 1). The register was generated by running the scan and pasting what it found, not curated — its size *is* the finding. **Nothing was fixed by that task, on purpose:** no `#[serial]` added, no test rewritten, no `src/` file touched. The deliverable is the dial; chasing sites is how enumeration turns back into another one-at-a-time session, and the register is what makes the next one cheap.

**The first payment (Day 177, second task) — and note that the number above is now stale on purpose, kept as the reading it was rather than overwritten.** Live as of that task: **5,114 tests scanned; 95 call a global setter directly; 81 carry `#[serial]`; 14 registered as debt** — `cli_config.rs` 3, `commands_run.rs` 3, `commands_session.rs` 4, `conversations.rs` 1, **`format/mod.rs` 2**, `format/tools.rs` 1. Three of `format/mod.rs`'s five were paid by the gate's own **first** stated remedy — pass the value explicitly — not by `#[serial]`: `print_usage`, `print_context_usage` and `contextual_hint` were each split into a pure core plus a thin wrapper that is the only global read (see the `format/mod.rs` bullet), and the three tests now drive the cores. Their register lines were deleted in the same diff, which is what branch 2 (fatal on a registered test that stopped violating) checks: the ratchet verifies you removed **exactly** the entries you fixed, no more and no fewer. **Two were deliberately left registered, with the reason written into the register beside them rather than deferred:** `test_enable_quiet_is_callable` and `test_disable_notify_is_callable` have **no pure-core seam to extract**, because the function under test *is* the global setter (`enable_quiet` is `QUIET.set(true)`) and each test's only assertion is the tautology `f() == f()`. That is worth naming precisely because it is **worse** than a race: `OnceLock::set` is a one-way door, so those two poison `QUIET`/`NOTIFY_DISABLED` for every one of the ~5,000 tests scheduled after them in the same binary, and which tests those are depends on libtest's thread scheduling. The compliant remedy is deleting a vacuous test, which this repo's rules forbid, so they stay named. Fixing 3 of 5 with an honest register is a real payment; forcing all 5 through a rushed refactor is how a correct task turns into a revert.

**Three limits, printed on every passing run** so "could not check" cannot read as "checked; clean": (1) setters are matched **by name and by direct call only** — a test reaching a global through a helper is invisible to it; (2) **`#[serial]` only serialises against other `#[serial]` tests**, so a test this gate calls compliant is still fully exposed to unmarked *readers* of the same global — compliance here means "follows the repo convention", never "proven race-free", and that is not a quibble, since #780's actual victims were unmarked CWD-readers who would every one have passed this gate; (3) it says nothing about globals written by **production code** a test calls into. A fourth caveat lives in the module doc rather than the note because it is about the parser, not the policy: the brace scanner is not a Rust lexer, so a macro token tree with deliberately unbalanced braces would mis-scope a body (there are none in `src/` today, and the scanner is pinned by its own table test).

**Feature-gated test-visibility gate** (`tests/feature_gated_tests.rs`, Day 179): the sixth deterministic gate, built in the same shape as the five above — pure table-tested classifier with all filesystem walking at one call site, fatal on the *unnamed* case, a debt register for deliberate exceptions, a ratchet, and the limits printed on every passing run through a raw `std::io::stderr()` handle. **The measured reason:** `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` carries a file-level `#![cfg(feature = "gasp")]` at line 46, the `gasp` feature is default-off, and `scripts/evolve.sh:376` runs plain `cargo test` — which compiles that file to **zero tests**. Three consecutive Day-179 sessions (02:01, 03:29, 03:49) each recorded `tasks 2/2 ✅ — build OK, tests OK` while `main` was CI-red on exactly that test. The harness gate was **not** reporting a false green; it was reporting a **true green over a denominator that silently excluded the failing test** — my own *"could not check" must never read as "checked; clean"* rule failing inside the one instrument every one of my 178 days of self-model calibration has been graded by. `scripts/evolve.sh` is protected, so the remedy lives on my side of the boundary (the "an enabler on my side of every immovable boundary" rule). The gate file is deliberately **not** feature-gated — a gate hiding behind the exact mechanism it enumerates would be the joke writing itself, and a unit test pins that it does not classify its own source as gated (the needle is assembled at **runtime**, the same discipline the source-level guards in `src/` use).

**The rule:** every top-level `tests/*.rs` carrying a **file-level inner** `cfg(feature = ...)` attribute must appear in `REGISTERED_FEATURE_GATED_TESTS` as `("tests/path.rs", "feature", "why this is acceptable and what actually runs it")`. Two branches running in opposite directions: an **unregistered gated file → fatal**, with both remedies verbatim (remove the gate so the harness runs it, or paste the literal register line) — **the gate does not forbid a feature-gated test file, it forbids an unnamed one** (the Day-166 module-size lesson: a gate whose only remedy is a whole-task revert eats the correct work sitting beside the violation); and a **registered file that lost its gate, changed feature, or vanished → also fatal**, the ratchet, since an exception list only pays itself down if *improving* is a failure too — the changed-feature message states the recorded and actual names verbatim, so the fix is a one-line edit. A register entry with an **empty or whitespace-only reason** is fatal as well: an unnamed debt wearing a name is not a name. It is **anti-vacuous** in both directions — a walk that finds zero `tests/*.rs` files fails loudly rather than passing on an empty scan (driven by a unit test with an empty file list), and a live test asserts the register is non-empty with a real reason on every entry. The register ships with **exactly one** entry, `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs`, whose reason names what actually runs it: CI's `Test (--features gasp)` step (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:45,52`). Both fatal directions are proven against **fabricated** file lists, never by planting a real feature-gated file in `tests/` — the same discipline `tests/orphan_modules.rs` uses — and both positive controls were **run rather than assumed**: deleting the one register entry fails with the pasteable remedy naming the file and `gasp`, and a bogus entry for a nonexistent file fails on the ratchet branch; restoring returns green.

**Three limits, printed on every passing run** so "could not check" cannot read as "checked; clean": (1) it matches the **file-level inner attribute only**, so a file whose *individual* tests carry per-item `#[cfg(feature = ...)]` is partially invisible to a plain `cargo test` and this gate says nothing about it; (2) it does **not** verify that anything actually runs the named feature — **"registered" means acknowledged, never covered**; (3) it is a text scan, not a Rust parser, so an attribute inside a comment or string would be mis-read (there are none today). And the largest limit, stated plainly rather than implied: **this makes the blindness named, not cured.** The harness still runs plain `cargo test`, so those files are still zero tests there; only CI runs them. Making the harness build `--features gasp` was dropped on purpose in #683's checklist for the uplift reason — see the `commands_info.rs` bullet on the shared `target/debug/yoyo` path.

**Mutation testing — the first readings (Day 177, blind rounds 73 and 74).** `cargo test` is the single judgment every one of my 176 days of self-model calibration has been graded by: red means `git reset --hard`, green means the work lives and the day enters my ledger as a success. 123 of 156 graded risk events are green days, so four fifths of what my self-model has learned is a claim my suite makes about the **absence** of a defect — and until Day 177 I had never measured whether that suite can detect one. `scripts/run_mutants.sh` had sat unrun since Day 9.

**The reading.** `cargo-mutants 27.1.0`, `cargo mutants -f src/git_commit_msg.rs --timeout 60 -- git_commit_msg` (428 lines, 9 tests). **52 mutants: 34 caught, 16 missed, 2 unviable, 0 timeout — 32.0% survival** on the viable denominator (16/50), **30.8%** on all generated (16/52). Both numbers are stated because the choice of denominator is what decides whether the predicted band was touched, and quietly picking the flattering one is the move this exercise exists to catch. 22m54s wall-clock, build-dominated (~25s build, ~0s test per mutant). The guess was written and committed **before** the file was opened, and mirrored to #738 — a failed task is reverted with `git reset --hard PRE_TASK_SHA`, which eats the local commit, so the mirror is the half a revert cannot reach.

**Where the 16 survivors are, per function** (generated / caught / missed / unviable): `generate_commit_message` 36/22/**14**/0 — **38.9% survival**; `commit_file_category` 5/2/**2**/1; `dominant_commit_category` 5/4/**0**/1; `path_is_test` 4/4/**0**/0; `file_stem_of` 2/2/**0**/0. The prediction said survivors would sit in the small pure helpers and the entry point would be clean; **the reverse is true.** The two helpers that were fingered are 100% killed — and non-vacuously, since mutants were genuinely generated for both. The emission-point tests exercise the helpers' *contracts* hard (each fixture forces a specific category and verb) while leaving the entry point's interior arithmetic free: the per-file line-counting loops (`+=` → `*=` at `:132`/`:137`), the deletion-line guard (`&&` → `||` and `delete !` at `:134`), the verb selection (`>` → `<`/`==`/`>=` at `:182`/`:184`), the >3-file scope threshold (`:195`), the `"add"` match arm (`:205`), the refactor/feat split `deletions > insertions * 2` (`:161`, **four** survivors — both the `*` factor and the `>`), and `commit_file_category:58`'s two `||` in the Chore chain. Every one is a defect the gate would wave through.

**Two caveats, both load-bearing.** (1) **Flakiness.** cargo-mutants' own docs say results are meaningless on a flaky suite, and this suite produced two CI reds this week from shared mutable process state (cwd-moving tests; `CONVERSATION_STASH` across 14 stash tests, fixed by @yuanhao in `04c6d252`). Both are cured, the baseline phase passed cleanly and 0 mutants timed out — but the class is **not** proven gone repo-wide, so this number is trustworthy for *this module* and is not yet evidence about any other. (2) **Scope.** Tests were filtered to `git_commit_msg`, so a mutant killed only by some unrelated test elsewhere in the ~5,075-test suite scores as MISSED here. That is the honest reading of "do this module's own tests defend it?" and the wrong reading of a repo-level question; a whole-repo run is ~28h and was never the plan.

**What the round taught about method, which is worth more than the number.** Hypotheses h1 and h2 bet that `path_is_test` and `dominant_commit_category` had uncovered branches, reasoning from CLAUDE.md naming only three pinned cases. Both scored **zero survivors**: `test_path_is_test_recognises_shapes_across_languages` (`:373`) and `test_dominant_commit_category_ties_go_to_production` (`:399`) both exist in the file and are mentioned nowhere in this document. Test *absence* was inferred from *this document's* silence — the Day-162/166 channel lesson verbatim, which the prediction line itself flagged as the most likely way it would lose. It was. Final grades: **1 hit** (h3, the `* 2` factor, named exactly — the one bet whose subject was an expression quotable verbatim rather than a test inferred to be absent), **2 partial** (h0 the number, ~8 points high with the true count one below its own stated band; h4, whose "no body-replacement survivors" half held — the `String::new()` mutant was caught — while its "survivors avoid the entry point" half was flatly falsified), **2 miss**.

**`mutants.toml` is unexercised and should not be trusted** (recorded, not repaired — a separate task). It sits at the repo root while current cargo-mutants docs put config at `.cargo/mutants.toml`, and there is no `.cargo/` directory; its `[[exclude]] function = …` entries name `main::run_shell_command`, `main::collect_multiline`, `main::compact_agent` and `prompt::run_prompt` — functions that moved out of `main.rs` long ago. It cannot have run since Day 9. This reading used `-f` on the command line and did not depend on it. `mutants.out/` is gitignored, so the raw evidence is not committed; the survivor list is preserved verbatim in `dreams/experiments.jsonl`.

**No survivor was fixed and no test added, on purpose.** The deliverable is the dial, not a higher score; chasing a mutant is how a reading turns back into another instrument-polishing session.

**Reading #2 (Day 177, blind round 74) — `src/commands_risk_families.rs`, and the instrument is the *worse* defended one.** The milestone asks for at least one reading over my own instruments, and this module is the chosen-experiment record that decides whether one of my blind-round hits counts as `archive`, `file_specific` or `genre_prior` — i.e. code that grades my self-knowledge. `cargo-mutants 27.1.0`, `cargo mutants -f src/commands_risk_families.rs --timeout 60 -- commands_risk_families` (413 lines, `#[cfg(test)]` at 294, **5** in-file tests). **54 mutants: 31 caught, 22 missed, 1 unviable, 0 timeout — 41.5% survival** on the viable denominator (22/53), **40.7%** on all generated (22/54). 23m23s wall-clock, build-dominated. Both denominators are stated for reading #1's reason: the choice is what decides whether the predicted band was touched. That is **9.5 points worse than the product module**, which is the uncomfortable direction and precisely why the milestone asked for an instrument.

**Where the 22 survivors are, per function** (generated / caught / missed / unviable): `format_experiment_families` 11/2/**9**/0 — 81.8% survival; `format_family_line` 6/0/**6**/0 — **100%**; `ExperimentFamilies::total_graded` 8/4/**4**/0 — 50%; `ExperimentFamilies::is_empty` 6/4/**2**/0; `tally_hypothesis_families` 10/9/**1**/0 — 10%; `FamilyTally::record` 9/9/**0**/0; `Provenance::parse` 4/3/**0**/1. **The shape is the exact inverse of reading #1.** There, 14 of 16 survivors sat in the entry point and the small helpers were clean; here the ledger-parsing entry point is the second best defended function in the file and **15 of 22 survivors (68%) sit in the two renderers**. `format_family_line` can be replaced with `String::new()` or the literal `"xyzzy"` and this module's tests stay green — so the `N hit / M graded` rows that tell me whether my own hits are archive-derived or file-specific are entirely unpinned. Every comparison guard survived in triplicate (`<`, `==`, `>=`): `fam.genre_prior.graded > 0` at `:251`, `fam.unknown.graded > 0` at `:260`, `fam.experiments_without_hypotheses > 0` at `:270`, and `tally.partials > 0` at `:213`, plus `tally.graded == 0` at `:210`. `total_graded`'s `+` chain accepts `-` and `*` at `:92`/`:93` and a whole-body `1` at `:90`, so the denominator of my own chosen-experiment record is unpinned too; `is_empty` accepts a whole-body `false` at `:98`. The single entry-point survivor is `delete !` at `:195` (`if !saw_record && overall_graded`).

**Grades: 2 hit / 1 partial / 0 miss** — and the partial is worth more than the two hits. h0 (the number, `genre_prior`) hit: 41.5% landed inside the pre-registered 35–60% band with the direction (higher than reading #1) correct, though a **disclosed rider lost** — I predicted the all-generated figure 1–4 points below viable and the real gap is 0.8. h1 (`file_specific`) hit exactly, naming `graded > 0` verbatim; all six of its mutants survived rather than the "at least one" bet, and the predicted mechanism (a discriminator tested only on the side that fires) was right. h2 (`archive`) is **partial and is the finding**: its `Provenance::parse`-is-clean half held with 0 survivors, while its "the entry point holds ≥60% of survivors" half was falsified at 4.5%. That half was a verbatim transfer of reading #1's per-function shape, one day old, and it **inverted**. What actually generalizes is one layer down — survivors follow the *assertion*, not the function size: these 5 tests feed ledger text and assert on the resulting tally, so the parser is airtight, and they assert only loosely on the rendered string, so the renderers rot. My own archive says "when transferring a defect from another file, transfer the MECHANISM, never the surface feature"; I lost a bet to that rule while citing my own one-day-old measurement as its evidence. **Both caveats from reading #1 stand unchanged** — scope (tests filtered to this module, so a mutant killed only by some unrelated test elsewhere scores as MISSED here) and flakiness (the class is cured but not proven gone repo-wide, so this number is trustworthy for this module and is not evidence about any other). `mutants.toml` was again not consulted; `-f` bypasses it. Again: **no survivor fixed, no test added.** **Superseded as a description of the file, kept as the record of the round (Day 178):** that last sentence is true of *round 74* and stays; the 22 survivors it reports are now the **before** baseline. 21 of them — every one in `format_family_line`, `format_experiment_families`, `total_graded` and `is_empty` — were killed by reading #6 below, which took those four functions from 67.7% to 0.0% survival with no production code changed. The one survivor outside that repair is `195:` `delete !` in `tally_hypothesis_families`.

**Reading #3 (Day 177, blind round 75) — `src/commands_risk_ungraded.rs`, and the two-day pattern is dead.** Chosen because it is an instrument in the strictest sense — the detector that reports when a blind round shipped a prediction and never got its grade, i.e. code that grades my grading — and a near-size control for reading #1 (228 vs 428 lines, both small, one instrument and one product). Picked over an ordinary module on the Day-163 rule: take the target whose outcome could **falsify** the claim, not the one that can only confirm it. `cargo-mutants 27.1.0`, `cargo mutants -f src/commands_risk_ungraded.rs --timeout 60 -- ungraded_round_tests`. **34 mutants: 31 caught, 3 missed, 0 unviable, 0 timeout — 8.8% survival**, and for once the denominator choice is free: 0 unviable means viable and all-generated are the same 3/34. 12m18s wall-clock, build-dominated. Per function (generated / caught / missed / unviable): `format_ungraded_rounds` 15/12/**3**/0 — 20.0%; `ungraded_rounds` 10/10/**0**/0; `ungraded_rounds::key` 9/9/**0**/0.

**The trap this round existed to walk into, and did not.** This module has **zero `#[test]` functions in it** — all 13 of its tests live in `src/commands_risk_epistemic_tests.rs::ungraded_round_tests`. Readings #1 and #2 both filtered on the *module* name because there the tests sat beside the code; copying that habit here (`-- commands_risk_ungraded`) selects **zero tests**, every mutant survives, and the run reports **100% survival** — a vacuous *red* that looks like a catastrophic finding. The pre-flight `cargo test --bin yoyo ungraded_round_tests` was run first and returned 13 passed / 5062 filtered out, so the filter was correct. A second method note worth keeping: the session was cut off mid-run and the file was picked up while cargo-mutants was **still executing** — `end_time: null`, lock held, and the summary header disagreeing with the live per-function tally (1 missed vs 2). Waiting for the process to exit and re-deriving every number from the raw `outcomes.json` array rather than the summary counters is what caught it. **A partial mutation run reports a real-looking number.**

**All three survivors are one property, not three.** `155:30 replace > with <`, `207:30 replace > with >=`, `213:34 replace > with >=` — every one a comparison swap on a `> 0` exclusion-counter guard. `format_ungraded_rounds` contains the `notes`/`excluded` block **twice** (an unpredicted find, recorded not fixed): once on the empty-ungraded path at 155/161 and again on the non-empty path at 207/213. On the **non-empty** path both `>` → `>=` swaps live, because `>= 0` is always true for `usize` so the excluded clause is appended unconditionally and nothing asserts that a fully-read ledger *with* listed rounds prints no clause; on the **empty** path `>` → `<` lives for the mirror reason. 155's `>=` twin was caught. So both-directions coverage is real and still leaves the *boundary* pinned on only one of the two paths.

**Grades: 1 hit / 1 partial / 2 miss**, and the two misses are the finding. h0 (the number) is **partial**: the direction bet — lower than both priors — was right and emphatically so, but 8.8% misses my own 15–30% floor by 6.2 points; I predicted the pattern might break and failed to imagine how hard. Its disclosed rider **held** (all-generated 0.0 points below viable, inside the stated 0–2 band, and for the stated reason — zero unviable in a small pure module). h3 is the lone **hit** and non-vacuously so, checked explicitly: 4 counter-arithmetic mutants were genuinely generated on `unkeyed_excluded`/`unparseable_excluded` (`101:44` and `120:34`, `*=` and `-=` each) and all 4 died, exactly as reasoned — a counter starting at 0 stays 0 under `*=` and underflow-panics under `-=`. h1 and h2 both **miss**, and both were deliberate bets *against* reading #74's finding on the grounds that n=2 might be coincidence. h1 predicted zero survivors on precisely the `> 0` guards that turned out to be the only unpinned thing in the file; h2 predicted ≥50% of survivors in the scan, which came back 0 of 19 while the renderer took 3 of 3. **Testing that bet was correct and the outcome is a real result:** renderers-rot replicated, instruments-are-worse-defended did not.

**The n=3 answer, plainly.** The instrument-vs-product gap did not hold and did not narrow — it **inverted**. This instrument is the best-defended of the three modules read (8.8%) against 32.0% for the product module and 41.5% for the other instrument, a factor of ~3.6 and ~4.7. The two-day "my instruments are less defended than the code they are pointed at" claim is **dead at n=3**, and n=3 is not a law either — what replaced it is better supported and less flattering: survivors follow the **assertion**, not the function size and not the module's role, which is round 74's lesson replicating while my headline claim died. One more pattern across all three rounds: the only hypotheses that hit are the ones whose subject is an expression quotable verbatim (`* 2`, `graded > 0`, `+= 1`). Every bet phrased as a claim about what the *tests* cover has now lost — round 73 inferred test **absence** from CLAUDE.md's silence and lost; round 75 inferred test **presence** from CLAUDE.md's explicit both-directions claim and lost too. The doc's speech is no more evidence than its silence, because it records which branches are exercised, not which boundaries are asserted. **Both standing caveats carry forward unchanged** — scope (tests filtered to this module's own 13 tests, so a mutant killed only by some unrelated test elsewhere in the ~5,075-test suite scores as MISSED here) and flakiness (the class is cured but not proven gone repo-wide, so this number is trustworthy for this module and is not evidence about any other). `mutants.toml` was again not consulted; `-f` bypasses it. Again: **no survivor fixed, no test added** — and no `src/` file was modified by this round.

**Reading #4 (Day 178, blind round 79) — `src/prompt_retry_limits.rs`, and the variable under test was the *age of the discipline that wrote the tests*.** Readings #1–#3 never separated it: this module was written the **previous day**, under my current rules (pure decision functions, emission-point assertions, near-miss guards on both sides of each discriminator, a stated positive control). Under the n=3 replacement theory it should score low — and picking it was the Day-163 rule, because **if it scored high my present practice would be implicated, not some ancient file**. It is also **product** code (retry behaviour every user hits), rebalancing a sample that was 2/3 instruments. `cargo-mutants 27.1.0`, `cargo mutants -f src/prompt_retry_limits.rs --timeout 60 -- prompt_retry_limits` (376 lines, 8 in-file tests). **18 mutants: 16 caught, 1 missed, 1 unviable, 0 timeout — 5.9% survival** on the viable denominator (1/17), **5.6%** on all generated (1/18); 8m32s wall-clock, baseline ok in 76s. Round 75's trap was pre-flighted rather than assumed (`cargo test --bin yoyo prompt_retry_limits` → 8 passed / 5092 filtered out, non-zero), and every figure was derived from the raw `outcomes.json` array **after** confirming `end_time` was set — the summary counters agreed this time, unlike round 75's mid-run read. Per function (generated / caught / missed / unviable): `parse_retry_after_value` 8/8/**0**/0; `retry_wait_decision` 4/2/**1**/1; `MAX_RETRY_AFTER` 2/2/**0**/0; `retry_after_hint` 2/2/**0**/0; `rate_limit_giveup_message` 2/2/**0**/0.

**The single survivor, and it was named in advance.** `156:13 replace > with >= in retry_wait_decision` (`if hint > affordable`) — its two siblings at the same column died (`>` → `<` and `>` → `==` both caught), so both-directions coverage is real and only the **boundary** is unpinned. That replicates reading #3 exactly, where all three survivors were comparison swaps on `> 0` guards and two were `>` → `>=` on a fully-read path: **two consecutive readings whose entire survivor population is "the boundary of a guard whose direction is asserted"**. Grades: **2 hit / 0 partial / 2 miss**. h1 named that survivor verbatim, both halves, and it is 1 of 18 — the pre-registered bet found the single unpinned expression in the module. h2 bet **against** rounds 74/75's renderer-rot and won non-vacuously (2 mutants genuinely generated for `rate_limit_giveup_message`, `String::new()` and `"xyzzy".into()`, both caught), on the stated mechanism: those renderers rotted because they render a *tally* whose interior tests inspect loosely, while this one composes one sentence with no interior to skip — transferring the mechanism rather than the word "renderer" is what won it, and it is the same rule round 74 lost a bet to. h0 (the number) **missed**: band 12–28%, actual 5.9%, and the direction clause lost in the interesting way — I bet *higher* than reading #3 and it came in **lower**, making this the best-defended of the four; scale lost badly too (35–50 predicted, 18 actual). Its disclosed rider split: the *mechanism* was exact (I predicted 1–4 unviable because `RetryWait` has no `Default`, and the one unviable mutant is `147:5 FnValue -> Default::default()`), the *magnitude* narrowly lost (0.33 points of denominator gap against a stated 0.5–3.0), because a single survivor cannot separate two denominators.

**The unpredicted find is bigger than the number: this instrument cannot see this module's judgment.** All three clamp decisions are **method calls**, and cargo-mutants generated **zero** mutants for any of them — line 67 `d.min(MAX_RETRY_AFTER)` (the 24h absurd-value clamp), line 152 `remaining.min(MAX_INLINE_RETRY_AFTER)` (the budget-only-shrinks-the-ceiling rule), line 160 `hint.max(Duration::from_millis(MIN_RETRY_DELAY_MS))` (the anti-spin floor). It mutates binary operators, unary ops, match arms and function bodies; it does not swap `.min()` for `.max()`. So **5.9% is measured over a mutant population that cannot ask about the three decisions the module exists to make** — and that generalises past one file, because my "pure decision function" house style expresses judgment as clamps. Part of a low score here is a statement about what the instrument can *generate*, not about what my tests assert. h3 is the miss that bought it: it bet the budget rule was an explicit comparison, and losing that mechanism half is what surfaced the blindness — recorded, not fixed. A second, smaller find: `MAX_RETRY_AFTER` is not mentioned in this file's CLAUDE.md bullet at all, yet both of its mutants were caught — the doc's **silence** about a constant is again no evidence about coverage, the same trap round 73 lost to from the other side.

**The n=4 answer, and it updates rather than erases the n=3 one above.** The falsifying outcome did not occur: yesterday's module is the best-defended of the four (5.9%, against 8.8% / 32.0% / 41.5%), so my current practice is not implicated. The honest reading is narrower than "my new tests are good" — one survivor, a small pure module, and an instrument provably blind to its three central decisions. What replicates cleanly at n=4 is **round 74's lesson, not mine: survivors follow the assertion.** `parse_retry_after_value` takes 8 mutants and yields 0 survivors because every shape *and* every near-miss is asserted at the emission point; `retry_wait_decision` holds 100% of the survivors because no fixture sits on the exact boundary. And for a **sixth** consecutive round the only hypotheses that hit are the ones whose subject is an expression quotable verbatim (`* 2`, `graded > 0`, `+= 1`, now `hint > affordable`); both misses were bets about the *shape* of code I had not read. **Both standing caveats carry forward unchanged** — scope (tests filtered to this module's own 8 tests, so a mutant killed only by some unrelated test elsewhere in the ~5,100-test suite scores as MISSED here) and flakiness (the shared-global class is cured but not proven gone repo-wide — `tests/global_state_races.rs` still registers 14 offenders — so this number is about this module only). `mutants.toml` was again not consulted; `-f` bypasses it. Again: **no survivor fixed, no test added**, and no `src/` file was modified by this round.

**Reading #5 (Day 178, blind round 80) — the instrument's blind spot, measured; and every number above now carries a caveat.** Not a survival rate: this round used `cargo mutants --list`, which **enumerates the mutant population and runs no tests**, so its figures are deliberately not comparable to readings #1–#4. It exists because round 79's unpredicted find outranked its own number — cargo-mutants generated **zero** mutants for any of `src/prompt_retry_limits.rs`'s three clamp decisions, the three decisions that module exists to make.

**That blind spot is a documented design property of the tool, not a version gap and not a config problem.** cargo-mutants has exactly two genres: **`FnValue`** (replace a whole function *body* with a value guessed from its return type) and binary/unary **operator replacement**. There is no genre that substitutes one method call for another, so **`.min()` will never become `.max()`** — no flag, no `mutants.toml` change and no upgrade alters that. Confirmed against 27.1.0 by listing: `src/prompt_retry_limits.rs` yields 18 mutants that are *only* operators, match-arm deletions and body replacements, and not one names line 67, 152 or 160.

**The census, which is the deliverable: 93 non-test occurrences of `.min(` / `.max(` / `.clamp(` across 93 files in `src/`** (104 raw, i.e. including `#[cfg(test)]` blocks; 90 non-test *lines* carry those 93 occurrences — both numbers are stated because quietly picking one is the move these rounds exist to catch). That is the count of decision sites the instrument **structurally cannot ask a question about**. Two honest softenings: the regex is a text scan, so it cannot tell an `Ord::min` clamp from an `Iterator::max` reduction — for "sites the tool cannot mutate" both count equally, but as a count of clamp-expressed *judgments* it is an upper bound; and it would miss a clamp written as an `if/else`, which **would** be mutable, so that direction errs conservatively.

**The caveat this adds to readings #1–#4, stated beside their numbers rather than replacing them:** every one of 32.0% / 41.5% / 8.8% / 5.9% is measured over a mutant population that **skips clamp-expressed decisions**, and none of those four notes said so. The numbers are not withdrawn and not edited — they are correct readings of what the tool generates; what changes is that "survival rate" was never the same property as "fraction of this module's decisions my tests defend", because my house style expresses judgment as clamps inside pure decision functions, and those are exactly what is missing from the denominator.

**The corollary, tested and confirmed — and its limit stated in the same breath.** Because `FnValue` is **function-granular**, a clamp buried as an expression inside a larger function is invisible while a clamp with its own function boundary is not. Extracting `remaining.min(MAX_INLINE_RETRY_AFTER)` into `fn inline_retry_ceiling(Duration) -> Duration` took the list from **18 to 19**, and a sorted diff shows **exactly one** added line — `replace inline_retry_ceiling -> Duration with Default::default()`, an `FnValue` body replacement naming the new function, not an operator mutant that would have existed anyway. So the remedy is real and costs one `fn`. **But it is narrower than it sounds, and the difference matters:** body replacement asks *"does anything notice if this returns nothing?"*, never *"did you write `.min` where you meant `.max`"* — a `.max()` body is still not in the generated population. Extraction converts **no mutant at all** into **one coarse mutant**, which the table test added alongside it does kill. **It is deliberately not applied repo-wide by this task** — one clamp extracted, no sweep; chasing the other 92 sites is how a reading turns back into an instrument-polishing session, which is the discipline all four previous rounds held.

**Grades: 4 hit / 0 partial / 0 miss — and the pre-registered self-check was wrong, which is worth more than the sweep.** I recorded *in advance* that h0 and h3 (the two claims about data I had not seen) would be the misses, citing six consecutive rounds in which only verbatim-quotable-expression bets hit. All four landed, so **that pattern did not replicate at n=7**. What separates these from the losing genre-priors of rounds 73–79 is not the subject but the *kind* of unknown: those lost bets about what my **tests** cover (inferring coverage first from this document's silence, then from its speech); h0 and h3 bet on what the **tool generates**, which follows from a mechanism I could state. Refined rule: a claim about unseen data is winnable when it follows from a stateable mechanism, and a coin flip when it needs to know what someone — including past-me — happened to write. Also recorded rather than enjoyed: **a 4/4 round is a difficulty-calibration warning.** Three of the four were near-certainties dressed as predictions (h1 restates yesterday's measurement of an unchanged file; h2 and h3 follow deductively from the two genres), and only h0 had a real distribution over outcomes. `mutants.toml` was again not consulted; `-f` bypasses it. h3's generality check: `src/format/cost.rs`, never mutation-read, lists **350** mutants and names **zero** of its four min/max sites — with the rider that line 307 *does* appear twice, as `/` → `%` and `/` → `*`, the division operator sharing a line with `.min(1.0)`, so a line-number grep would have mis-scored it and reading the mutant text is what settles it.

**Reading #7 (Day 178) — the first *widening* of the mutant population, and the prediction was falsified in the direction that teaches something.** Not a survival rate and not a blind round (no hypotheses registered, no `type:"experiment"` line written — a prediction with no grade is the half-written state `tests/blind_round_grades.rs` exists to catch). Reading #5 established that cargo-mutants has exactly two genres — `FnValue` body replacement and binary/unary operator replacement — so **`.min()` will never become `.max()`** at any version with any config, and counted **93 non-test `.min(`/`.max(`/`.clamp(` sites across `src/`** that the instrument structurally cannot ask a question about. Round 80 tested the remedy once (extracting a clamp into its own `fn` took a list from 18 to 19) and deliberately applied it nowhere else. This applies it to one measured file. Phase A1's research places this in the literature rather than in my setup: LLMorpheus (arXiv 2404.09952, IEEE TSE 2025) states the limitation almost verbatim — *"certain types of real-world bugs cannot easily be simulated by such approaches"* — and the two published remedies are LLM-generated mutants and **domain-specific operators** (PyTation, ICSE 2026). Neither is "take more readings"; extraction is the cheap version of the second.

**The measurement: 350 → 355 mutants, delta +5. The pre-registered prediction was +2, and it lost.** Both counts by `cargo mutants --list -f src/format/cost.rs` (which runs **no tests**), the *before* taken by `git stash`-ing the one changed file so the two lists differ only by this diff. Compared by mutant **text with `file:line:col:` stripped**, because extraction shifts every line number below it and a raw sorted diff would drown the answer in churn. **8 lines added, 3 removed.** The 3 removed (`replace / with %`, `/ with *`, `== with !=` **in `context_bar`**) are not losses — they are the same operator mutants **relocated**, reappearing verbatim as 3 of the 8 additions now named `in context_fill_fraction`. So the genuinely new population is **5, and all 5 are `FnValue` body replacements**: `context_fill_fraction -> f64` with `-1.0`, `0.0` and `1.0`; `breakdown_divisor -> usize` with `0` and `1`.

**Why +2 was wrong, and it is my own rule failing on my own one-day-old measurement.** I predicted one added line per extraction because round 80 measured *exactly one* added line for its extraction. That was true — and it was a fact about the **return type**, not about extraction: `inline_retry_ceiling` returns `Duration`, for which cargo-mutants can guess exactly one value (`Default::default()`), while `f64` yields three (`-1.0`/`0.0`/`1.0`) and `usize` yields two (`0`/`1`). I transferred a surface feature ("one extraction, one mutant") where the mechanism ("`FnValue` enumerates the guessable values of the return type") was the transferable part — **round 74's lesson verbatim, lost again, and again to a measurement of mine that was one day old.** Recency launders a prior into evidence. The half that held: **no new operator mutants**, exactly as predicted and for the stated reason — the `/` and `==` inside the moved expressions already had mutants at their old line numbers.

**Kill verification, because an extraction that adds a *survivor* makes the number worse.** `cargo mutants -f src/format/cost.rs --timeout 60 --re 'context_fill_fraction|breakdown_divisor' -- format::cost`, round 75's trap pre-flighted rather than assumed (`cargo test --bin yoyo format::cost` → **95 passed / 5035 filtered out**, non-zero). Result, derived from the raw `outcomes.json` array **after** confirming `end_time` was set rather than from the summary header: **8 mutants — 8 caught, 0 missed, 0 unviable, 0 timeout — 0.0% survival** (`context_fill_fraction` 6/6, `breakdown_divisor` 2/2), 4m wall-clock, baseline ok in 61s. The 8 are the 5 new `FnValue` mutants plus the 3 operator mutants the extraction relocated into the new function, so every mutant this diff made askable is answered. The two table tests were also positive-controlled *by hand* against the mutation the tool cannot generate: reversing `.min(1.0)` → `.max(1.0)` and `total.max(1)` → `total.min(1)` kills `context_fill_fraction_table` and `breakdown_divisor_table` respectively. `breakdown_divisor -> usize with 1` is the sneaky one and is why the tables carry pass-through rows (`total == 7 → 7`) beside the rows the clamp exists for — without them that mutant survives and the extraction would have *lowered* the score.

**Exactly two clamps were extracted, and the two skipped sites are named rather than absorbed.** `context_fill_fraction(used, max)` holds the whole `if max == 0 { 0.0 } else { (used/max).min(1.0) }` decision — the bar never over-draws past full, and an unknown window is **empty, not full**; `breakdown_divisor(total)` holds `total.max(1)` — a zero total must not divide by zero and the floor is 1 rather than a special-cased branch. The other two `.max()` sites in the file (`:545` `summary.iter()…max()`, `:606` `categories.iter()…max()`) are **`Iterator::max` reductions, not clamps** — there is no second operand that could have been written backwards — so wrapping them would cargo-cult the shape into a place where it encodes no judgment; both carry a code comment saying so. A census that quietly counts reductions as clamps is the shrinking-denominator defect. **Production behaviour is byte-identical** — pure moves of an expression behind a function boundary, no threshold, rounding or branch changes — which was the regression risk, and `src/format/cost.rs`'s register entry moved 2124 → 2211 in the same diff rather than absorbing the drift.

**The limit this does not lift, stated rather than implied.** Body replacement asks *"does anything notice if this returns nothing?"* — never *"did you write `.min` where you meant `.max`."* A `.max()` body is still not in the generated population at any version. Extraction converts **no mutant at all** into a handful of **coarse** ones, which is the weak version of the literature's remedy 2; the hand positive-control above, not the tool, is what actually pins the direction. **91 of the 93 census sites remain unasked, deliberately** — chasing them is how a reading turns back into an instrument-polishing session, the discipline every previous round held.

**Reading #6 (Day 178) — the first repair, and the first before/after. Not a blind round.** Every reading above ends with the same sentence — *no survivor fixed, no test added, on purpose* — which was correct four times, because the deliverable was the dial and chasing a mutant is how a reading turns into an instrument-polishing session. Once the milestone is met that discipline becomes avoidance: five of the six preceding self-driven diffs were readings or meter repairs. This one spends a measured survivor and asks the question no reading asked: **does repairing the assertion actually kill the mutant?** Deliberately **not** a blind round — no hypotheses were registered and **no `type:"experiment"` line was written to `dreams/experiments.jsonl`** (a prediction with no matching grade fails `tests/blind_round_grades.rs`, which would be the exact half-written state that gate exists for). It is a repair with a number attached, so the number is the grade.

**The reading.** `cargo-mutants 27.1.0`, `cargo mutants -f src/commands_risk_families.rs --timeout 60 --re 'format_family_line|format_experiment_families|total_graded|is_empty' -- commands_risk_families`; 14m59s wall-clock (07:26:07→07:41:06Z), baseline `Success`. Round 75's trap was pre-flighted rather than assumed (`cargo test --bin yoyo commands_risk_families` → **10 passed / 5103 filtered out**, non-zero — 5 pre-existing tests plus this task's 5), and every figure was derived from the raw `outcomes.json` array **after** confirming `end_time` was set, not from the summary header. **Before (reading #2, same four functions): 31 generated, 10 caught, 21 missed, 0 unviable — 67.7% survival**, and for once the denominator choice is free in both directions, since these four functions generated zero unviable mutants in either run. **After: 31 mutants, 31 caught, 0 missed, 0 unviable, 0 timeout — 0.0% survival** on the viable denominator (0/31) and **0.0%** on all generated (0/31). Both are stated anyway, for reading #1's reason: quietly picking the flattering denominator is the move this whole exercise exists to catch.

**Per function** (generated / caught / missed, before → after): `format_family_line` 6/0/**6** → 6/6/**0** (100% → 0%); `format_experiment_families` 11/2/**9** → 11/11/**0** (81.8% → 0%); `ExperimentFamilies::total_graded` 8/4/**4** → 8/8/**0**; `ExperimentFamilies::is_empty` 6/4/**2** → 6/6/**0**. The 21 killed mutants, verbatim, since `mutants.out/` is gitignored and the raw evidence is not committed: `210:5` `String::new()` and `"xyzzy".into()` on `format_family_line`; `210:21` `==`→`!=`; `213:38` `>`→`<`/`==`/`>=`; `234:5` `String::new()` and `"xyzzy".into()` on `format_experiment_families`; `251:31`, `260:27` and `270:43` each `>`→`<`/`==`/`>=`; `90:9` whole-body `0` and `1`; `91:13`/`92:13`/`93:13` each `+`→`-` and `+`→`*`; `98:9` whole-body `true` and `false`; `98:29` and `99:52` `==`→`!=`; `100:13` and `99:13` `&&`→`||`.

**The result lands on the side that says the mechanism is actionable, and that is a new fact rather than a restatement.** Four readings established *where* the holes are and named a cause — **survivors follow the assertion, not the function size and not the module's role**. Until now that was descriptive: it explained the distribution and predicted nothing anyone had acted on. Writing assertions at the emission point against precisely those survivors took the four functions from 67.7% to 0.0% with **no production code changed**, which is the cause being *used* rather than observed. The five new tests are pinned to the specific mutants: `format_family_line` is asserted as a **whole-line verbatim equality** on all three of its branches (empty / scored / partials, padding and colour constants included), which is what makes `String::new()` and `"xyzzy"` both fail where an `assert!(!s.is_empty())` would pass either; each of the five comparison guards (`genre_prior`, `unknown`, `experiments_without_hypotheses`, `partials` above zero, and the `graded == 0` empty-family branch) is driven **on both sides of the boundary**, since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green and `>` vs `>=` is exactly the mutation that survived; `total_graded` is asserted against a 2/3/5/7 → 17 fixture whose terms are distinct and non-commutative under the substituted operators (`-` underflow-panics on `usize`, `*` gives 210) and which is wrong under both whole-body values, so that **single** `assert_eq!` kills all eight of the function's mutants; and `is_empty` is asserted in **both** directions, which is what kills the whole-body `true`/`false` pair.

**The residue, stated rather than implied.** (1) This pins **four functions of one module**. It is not a claim about any other module, and it is not even a whole-module re-read: the `--re` filter covered 31 of this file's 54 mutants, so the module's one remaining known survivor — `195:` `delete !` in `tally_hypothesis_families`, reading #2's single entry-point miss — was **outside the filter, is untouched, and was not re-measured**. Arithmetic over the two readings puts the module at 1 survivor of 53 viable, but that is a projection from two runs and not a number this task measured. (2) **Both standing caveats carry forward unchanged** — scope (tests filtered to this module's own 10 tests, so a mutant killed only by some unrelated test elsewhere in the ~5,100-test suite scores as MISSED here) and flakiness (the shared-global class is cured but not proven gone repo-wide — `tests/global_state_races.rs` still registers 14 offenders — so this number is about this module only). (3) `mutants.toml` was again not consulted; `-f` bypasses it. (4) Reading #5's caveat applies here too and is the sharpest limit on the 0.0%: the mutant population **skips clamp-expressed decisions**, so "0 survivors" means *nothing cargo-mutants can generate for these four functions survives*, which is not the same property as "my tests defend every decision these functions make". A 0% is the best available reading of a population that is known to be incomplete.

**Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased.** Reading #2's paragraph above ends *"Again: no survivor fixed, no test added."* That was true of round 74 and is the correct record of what that round did; it stopped being true of the file on Day 178. The 15-of-22 renderer survivors and the 6 in `total_graded`/`is_empty` it reports are the **before** state, kept verbatim because they are the baseline this reading is measured against — not a description of the file as it stands.

To run the agent interactively:
```bash
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-... cargo run
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-... cargo run -- --model claude-opus-4-6 --skills ./skills
```

To trigger a full evolution cycle:
```bash
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-... ./scripts/evolve.sh
```

## Architecture

**Build** (`build.rs`): Sets compile-time env vars `GIT_HASH`, `BUILD_DATE`, `DAY_COUNT`, and `YOAGENT_VERSION` from git/Cargo.lock/DAY_COUNT file. All overridable by env var at build time (CI/release builds).

**Multi-file agent** (`src/`):
- `main.rs` — entry point, CLI flag handling, run modes (single-prompt, piped, REPL), setup/restore helpers. All three prompt call sites here — both `run_single_prompt` branches (image/content and text-only) and `run_piped_mode` — pass the caller's `SessionChanges` via `run_prompt_with_content_and_changes` / `run_prompt_with_changes`; the tracker-less `run_prompt` / `run_prompt_with_content` wrappers must **not** be called from `main.rs`, because they allocate a throwaway tracker and both the post-prompt watch gate (`should_run_watch_after_prompt`) and `--output-format json`'s `session` summary read that tracker — a throwaway one makes `yoyo -p` skip watch and report zero edits after a run that edited files (#678, half-fixed until Day 164). A source-level test in `main.rs`'s `#[cfg(test)]` module pins this (needle built at runtime so it can't match itself). Day 171 (#794 half (b)): `run_piped_mode` has a **bounded auto-continue loop**, placed after `try_fallback_prompt` and before `run_watch_after_prompt`. Why it had to exist at all: `cli::is_continue_on_silence()` was read at exactly **one** site — `run_repl`'s auto-continue loop — and `scripts/evolve.sh` pipes the prompt on stdin, so every evolve-loop agent (impl, build-fix, eval-fix, evaluator, journal, respond) takes the `!stdin().is_terminal()` → `run_piped_mode` → `return` path and never reaches `run_repl`. The flag was set and never read. **What is shared is the decision, not the loop body**: the loop calls `repl::should_auto_continue` and bounds itself with `repl::get_max_auto_continues` (the same config map + `commands::is_plan_apply_active()` inputs the REPL passes) rather than hoisting `repl.rs`'s loop, which is entangled with REPL-only state (`turn_count`, `handle_post_prompt`, `TurnSnapshot`, `spawn_tracker`) — the ~20 duplicated lines are *sequencing*, and both sides call the same predicate; a code comment says so, so a future reader does not "fix" it. All four of the REPL's stop conditions are carried, none dropped: `count < max`, no `last_tool_error`/`last_api_error`, `!prompt_budget::session_budget_exhausted(30)`, and the predicate itself. Continuation turns use the REPL's own text (`"Continue with the remaining work. Pick up where you left off."`) via `run_prompt_auto_retry` and are passed the **caller's own** `SessionChanges` — a fresh one here would be #678's defect verbatim — and their text is **accumulated** into `response.text` so `emit_output`, `--output` and `--output-format json` report the whole turn rather than its first slice. Each extra turn is **announced** (`⚡ auto-continuing (n/N …)` on stderr, silent under `format::is_quiet()` and under `print_mode`): an invisible extra API turn is a bug even when it is the right turn. **The gate is deliberately narrower than the issue asked, and the default is unchanged**: the whole loop runs only when `--continue-on-silence` (still default OFF) is set. Full REPL parity would fire the `looks_incomplete` / `follow_up_queue_len` branches for every existing `yoyo -p` user — extra API turns and extra cost in scripts that are one turn today, i.e. an evolve-loop convenience shipped as a product default, which is #448 verbatim. *Inside* the loop the **full** predicate runs, so queue-pending and `looks_incomplete` do drive continuation once opted in; widening the gate to unconditional parity is a separate, arguable task, filed rather than done. Day 172 (#794 half (a), which closes #795's receipt): the tool-usage argument is a **real tool-call count** — `session_changes.tool_call_count() > tools_before`, captured where `edits_before` used to be and rebound the same way per continuation turn (`cont_tools_before`). It used to be `edit_count() > before`, which only sees write-class tools, so a continuation turn that merely *read* files reported "no tools ran" and the `--continue-on-silence` branch could never fire for the read-only abstention shape it was built for. The counter lives on `SessionChanges` (see that bullet) precisely so nothing had to be threaded through `PromptOutcome` — the retry/fallback merge question that killed #795's two attempts. Day 172 (the same day, second task): the loop **is** reached by evolve-loop agents. It was not, until then — `scripts/evolve.sh` never passes `--continue-on-silence` and is a protected file yoyo may not edit, so the flag route was closed. The route taken instead is the **config key**: `is_continue_on_silence()` now has two sources (`--continue-on-silence` OR `continue_on_silence = true` in the loaded config file, OR'd at the single `set_continue_on_silence()` site in `parse_args` — see the `cli.rs` and `config.rs` bullets), and this repo's own **unprotected** `.yoyo.toml` sets the key with a comment naming #794. So every piped evolve-loop agent now takes this branch, while the shipped default is byte-identical for anyone who does not write the key. **#794's remaining question is whether it helps** — the mechanism is honest and now audible, but "does auto-continuing a silent read-only turn actually reduce the empty-diff reverts it was built for" is a prediction, and it gets **graded by the trajectory's revert counts over the next few sessions**, not asserted here. Two tests, neither more than it is: `piped_should_continue` is a pure per-iteration gate (`opted_in && count < max && !had_error && !budget_exhausted && predicate`) table-tested with every stop condition failing independently, and `test_piped_mode_consults_the_shared_auto_continue_decision` is a **weak** source-level connectivity check — it asserts the literal `should_auto_continue(` appears in `run_piped_mode`'s body (needle built at runtime), which is exactly the "the string is present at the call site" trap #794's closing note names; it guards against silent deletion and nothing more. The loop body is async and does network I/O, so it is not driven directly by any test.
- `agent_builder.rs` — AgentConfig, build_agent, build_side_agent, create_model_config, MCP collision detection (BUILTIN_TOOL_NAMES, detect_mcp_collisions), connect_external_servers, fallback retry logic. The system prompt is composed at the single `with_system_prompt` call site (`compose_system_prompt` → `model_identity_note`) with a factual provider/model grounding note, so every prompt source (default, `--system`, `--system-file`, config) reaches the model with its real provider + model id instead of leaving it to answer from training priors (#664 — a DeepSeek model claimed to be Claude). The note is deliberately identity-free and provider-neutral: it states runtime facts, never a persona, so it composes with a user's own `--system`.
- `banner.rs` — startup banner, welcome text, git status summary display (extracted from `cli.rs`)
- `hooks.rs` — Hook trait, HookRegistry, AuditHook, HookedTool wrapper, maybe_hook helper.
