Day 166 (#751): `.yoyo/audit.jsonl` has exactly **one** writer per completed tool call — the prompt event-stream tool-end handlers in `src/prompt.rs` (`PromptEventState::handle_tool_execution_end` for the display path, `handle_stream_json_events` for `--output-format json`), each keyed by `tool_call_id` and each recording the **real** elapsed duration and the real `!is_error`. `AuditHook` stays registered but is deliberately observe-only and writes nothing: `post_execute` receives only the tool name, params and output string, so the second entry it used to write had to fabricate `duration_ms: 0` and `success: true` — doubling every count mined off the log and reporting failed tool calls as successes (pinned by the source-level test `test_hooks_never_writes_to_the_audit_log` in `hooks.rs`).
- `tools.rs` — StreamingBashTool, RenameSymbolTool, AskUserTool, TodoTool, WebSearchTool, tool builders, SharedState wiring for sub-agents. Day 170 (#783): `RenameSymbolTool::execute` puts the paths it actually wrote (`RenameResult.written`) on the tool result's `details` under `"files_written"` — the tool has no `SessionChanges` handle, so the written set is *reported* here and recorded by `prompt::record_rename_tool_writes`; see the `commands_rename.rs` bullet for why the recording cannot happen off the tool's arguments.
- `smart_edit.rs` — SmartEditTool: fuzzy matching for edit_file errors, whitespace-only auto-fix retry, nearest-match line-number hints
- `tool_wrappers.rs` — Tool decorator types (GuardedTool, TruncatingTool, ConfirmTool, ArcGuardedTool, AutoCheckTool, RecoveryHintTool — short-circuits on the `REFUSAL_STEM_*` markers (`is_deterministic_refusal`): a deterministic refusal (read/plan mode, session cap, denied path) is returned verbatim with no `ToolFailureTracker` bump and no recovery hint, so a deliberate guard is neither treated as flakiness nor talked around (#710), ToolFailureTracker, LiteDescriptionTool, SessionCapTool — session-wide 200-call circuit breaker on `web_search`/`sub_agent`, honest error past the cap; the counter is per-wrapper-instance (per agent build), so the budget resets on `/clear` because `/clear` rebuilds the agent — pinned by `test_session_cap_fresh_wrapper_resets_budget`, do not convert to a process-wide static; ReadModeGuardTool — mechanical `/read` AND `/plan` mode enforcement at the tool layer: checks `is_read_mode()` and `commands_plan::is_plan_mode()` at call time, refuses write-class tools (`write_file`, `edit_file`, `rename_symbol`) with an honest error, and blocks bash commands flagged by the destructive-pattern classifier in `safety.rs` OR the write-command detector (`safety::detect_write_command` — write verbs at token boundaries: `touch`, `mkdir`, `mv`, `cp`, `tee`, `truncate`, `chmod`, `sed -i`, `dd of=`, `install`, `ln`, `rsync` (non-dry-run), plus `>`/`>>` redirection outside quoted regions; the refusal names what matched, read-only commands pass); the plan-mode block is a transparent pass-through while `/plan apply` is executing (`is_plan_apply_active()` — apply needs write access to carry out the plan); transparent pass-through when neither mode is on) and helper wrappers
- `rtk.rs` — RTK (Rust Token Killer) detection, proxy integration, output compression. Day 169 (blind round 56, a never-forecast file — this was an **unpredicted find while reading for h2**, not a graded bet, and the round's ledger says so): the one-time detection notice in `maybe_prefix_rtk` now goes through the pure `rtk_announcement(quiet, plain) -> Option<&'static str>` — `None` under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. It was the only stderr notice in the codebase that consulted neither, so `--quiet` still printed it and `--screen-reader` still got the `📦`; the two conventions ~15 other sites follow were skipped here because the announcement is buried inside a command-rewriting function rather than in a display module. The `RTK_ANNOUNCED` swap still happens first, so the notice stays once-per-process in every mode. Pinned by `test_rtk_announcement_respects_quiet_and_plain_output`, which asserts the literal string the call site prints in all three states (the `eprintln!` itself takes no other input) — the earlier round-56 grade line claimed this fix had landed when it had not, and the correction line in `dreams/experiments.jsonl` records that rather than quietly replacing it.
- `update.rs` — version comparison (`version_is_newer`) and update checking (`check_for_update`) against GitHub releases
- `safety.rs` — bash command safety analysis, destructive pattern detection, and (Day 165) the secret-redaction primitives `REDACTED`/`SECRET_PATTERNS`/`redact_secrets` — moved here verbatim from `gasp.rs` so they are compiled and tested by the **default** `cargo test` run. Two consumers: `prompt_budget::write_audit_entry` (always compiled) masks every string leaf of the tool **arguments** it writes to `.yoyo/audit.jsonl`, which `scripts/evolve.sh` pushes to the public `audit-log` branch; and, under `--features gasp`, the GASP recorder's summarizer. It is a mask, not a guarantee — a novel secret shape passes through, and tool **output** is not written to `audit.jsonl` today, so this is not a claim that the audit log is secret-free.
- `cli.rs` — CLI argument parsing, subcommands, configuration (delegates `--help` text to `help.rs`). Value-taking flags are validated by `check_flag_values` (pure, returns `Vec<FlagIssue>`; printing/exiting stays in `parse_args`): one left-to-right scan that skips the token it just consumed as a value, so **every** occurrence of a repeatable flag is checked, not just the first. Day 153: the old `position()`-per-flag loop validated only the first `--allow`/`--deny`/`--mcp`/… while `collect_repeatable_flag` consumes all of them, so a later occurrence could silently swallow the next flag as its value — the eaten flag never fired and `warn_unknown_flags` could not warn about it (silent wrong-op + silent no-op). The same scan also stops a flag *name* used as a *value* (`yoyo -p '--model'`) from being mistaken for a flag missing its value. Day 166 (#748) added the **project-config trust boundary** at `parse_mcp_and_openapi_config`, the single seam where the three MCP sources merge: the pure `gate_mcp_sources(cli_servers, config_servers, config_server_configs, project_local, trusted)` drops **only** the two file-config sources (`mcp = [...]` and `[mcp_servers.*]`) and **only** when `config::loaded_config_is_project_local()` is true and `--trust-project` was not passed — `--mcp` flags typed on the command line and home/XDG config entries are byte-identical to before, and when nothing is dropped the merge order is unchanged. A repo's `.yoyo.toml` could previously name an arbitrary local command and yoyo would start that process during normal startup with no prompt and no display of what was about to run; `--safe-mode` mitigated it but was opt-in, i.e. the default was wrong. Refusals are announced, not silent: `project_mcp_refusal_message` (pure) prints one stderr block naming the **resolved command string of every refused server** plus the two escape hatches (`--trust-project` to start them this run, `--safe-mode` to disable all project customizations), silent under `is_quiet()` and glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. `--trust-project` is in `KNOWN_FLAGS`, takes no value (so it is deliberately *not* in the value-taking list), and its state lives beside `set_continue_on_silence`/`is_continue_on_silence` in this file. Day 172 (#794): `set_continue_on_silence()` is still called from exactly **one** site in `parse_args`, but that site now has **two** sources OR'd together — the `--continue-on-silence` flag on the command line, **or** `config::parse_continue_on_silence_from_config(&file_config)` reading the `continue_on_silence` key of the loaded config file. Neither present → off, exactly as before, so the product default is unchanged; there is deliberately **no** disable flag, because an OR is the whole rule and a fourth precedence value was not needed. The site sits beside the existing config-driven flags (`parse_quiet_from_config`, `parse_no_bell_from_config`) and runs after `load_config_file` and before any agent build. The key is **not** part of the project-config trust boundary above and must not be added to it: it grants no privilege — the worst case is extra API turns in a repo you already chose to run yoyo in — unlike an MCP command or a `permissions.allow` entry. Day 166 (#749 item 3) extended the same boundary to the **privilege-granting half of `[permissions]`**, which was the more urgent gap: a stranger's repo shipping `allow = ["curl *"]` silently auto-approved those bash commands for the whole session, and unlike an MCP entry the effect is invisible until a command runs without asking. The pure `gate_project_permissions(permissions, from_cli_flags, project_local, trusted) -> PermissionGateOutcome` moves `permissions.allow` into `refused_allow` **only** when the config is project-local, `--trust-project` was not passed, and the permissions did not come from CLI flags (a `--allow` the user typed is the user's own word); every other input is a byte-identical pass-through. The gate is **deliberately asymmetric, and that asymmetry is the point**: fields are sorted by which direction they move privilege, and only the granting one is refused — `permissions.deny` is kept verbatim, and `dir_restrictions.allow`/`deny` are not touched at all, because the file-tool default is *unrestricted* so an allow-list can only narrow. A naive mirror of `gate_mcp_sources` would leave yoyo **less** confined than the repo asked for: a regression dressed as a security fix. A repo may always restrict yoyo further; it may not loosen it. Refusal is announced by the pure `project_permission_refusal_message` (names every refused pattern verbatim, both escape hatches, and states that deny/dir restrictions are still in force; silent under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`). **Both** entry points are wired, because a per-token pass is not a per-entry-point pass: `parse_permission_and_dir_config` in this file, and the `"permissions"` subcommand arm in `dispatch_sub.rs`, which parses the raw config independently to display effective permissions — a display showing refused patterns as active would be a lie in the one place a user goes to check (that arm also reads `--trust-project` straight off `args`, since `try_dispatch_subcommand` runs before `set_trust_project`). Day 175 (#820) closed the **fourth door on the same boundary**: `parse_args`' shell-hook parse (`crate::hooks::parse_hooks_from_config`) sat two lines below the `gate_project_permissions` call and read none of the provenance answer already computed there, while `hooks.rs` hands each configured string to `Command::new("sh").arg("-c")` on the first matching tool call — so cloning a stranger's repo whose `.yoyo.toml` carried `hooks.pre.bash = "…"` executed it during a normal session with no prompt and no display. The pure `gate_project_hooks(hooks, project_local, trusted) -> HookGateOutcome` refuses **only** when the config is project-local and `--trust-project` was not passed; home/XDG configs, trusted runs and repos with no hooks are byte-identical pass-throughs. **Both phases are refused, `pre` and `post` alike, and that is the direction rule applied correctly rather than a lapse in it** — #820's own body (which I wrote) argued a blocking `hooks.pre.*` *restricts* yoyo and so should survive like `permissions.deny`; that does not survive contact with the mechanism, because a pre-hook produces its blocking effect **by running arbitrary shell first**, so the narrowing cannot be had without the execution. The Day-166 asymmetry sorts *declarative* values yoyo interprets (a deny pattern, a path list — kept verbatim, since a repo may always confine yoyo further); a hook's entire content is **executable code**, so direction is decided by what the entry **is**, not by what it does after it runs. Refusal is announced by the pure `project_hook_refusal_message`, which names every refused hook in the shape it has in the file (`pre.bash = <command>`, so the user can grep for it) with the command echoed verbatim — capped at `REFUSAL_HOOK_CMD_MAX_BYTES = 400` on a **char** boundary with the cut marked in band — plus both escape hatches and the outright claim that **nothing was executed**; silent under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. The second door needed **no** code: `/hooks` (`commands_config::handle_hooks`) is reached only from `dispatch.rs:829` passing `ctx.agent_config.shell_hooks`, which `main.rs:943` builds from `config.shell_hooks` — the gated vec — so it (and `/config show`'s `hook_count`) inherits the gate; traced rather than assumed, and duplicating a gate that already flows through would be ceremony, not coverage. Day 178 (#749 item 1) gave that whole boundary its **second door: a persisted per-directory trust store**, because `--trust-project` applied to one run and a capability reachable only by retyping a flag is the shape that trains people to alias it away — i.e. back to the unsafe default, by habit (#749 says so out loud, and Claude Code / Cursor / VS Code all ask once per folder and remember). `set_trust_project()` is still called from exactly **one** site in `parse_args`, which now has **four** sources (the fourth landed the same day, see below): `--trust-project` (unchanged, one run, byte-identical), the new `--trust-project-always` (this run **and** `config_paths::remember_trusted_dir(cwd)`), or — with neither flag — `config_paths::dir_is_trusted(cwd)`. It is the same two-source-OR shape as `continue_on_silence` (#794) and `wait_for_reset` (Day 178), and the flag is in `KNOWN_FLAGS` taking no value (so deliberately *not* in the value-taking list `check_flag_values` scans). **A stored decision grants the full boundary** — project MCP servers, `[permissions].allow`, shell hooks (#820) and `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` (#761) all consult `is_trust_project()`, so recording a directory turns all four on there for every later session. **Both events are announced, because a silent trust grant is a bug even when it is the right grant** (the `⚡ auto-continuing` rule): the pure `trust_remembered_message` names the directory recorded, the store file's full path and that removing the line revokes it, and the pure `trusted_by_store_message` — the important one — says a run is live because of a `--trust-project-always` typed weeks ago and names the file, so a user can never discover that state by accident; both are silent under `is_quiet()` and glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. **The default is unchanged and pinned:** neither flag and no store file → the trust path is unreachable and output is byte-identical. There is deliberately **no un-trust flag** — revoking is deleting a line from a plain-text file, and both messages name its path, so a fourth switch would be ceremony. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #749 item 2):** this bullet used to end "**Deliberately not covered (follow-up #749 item 2): there is still no interactive 'do you trust this folder?' prompt**, so trust is only ever granted by a flag the user types — which is also why a non-interactive run always takes the safe answer." That was true for one task. The **fourth source** is now the prompt itself, and it is consulted **last**, only in the `else` branch where neither flag was passed and the store did not answer: yoyo **asks**, once, and honours the answer. The clause worth keeping from the superseded sentence is the second half, which is still exactly true and is now the guard rather than a consequence — **a non-interactive run always takes the safe answer**, because `should_prompt_for_trust` requires `stdin().is_terminal() && stderr().is_terminal()`, so the evolve loop (stdin piped), `yoyo -p` (piped) and CI (neither) all skip the question in silence and proceed untrusted exactly as before. The decision half is four pure functions in `config_paths.rs` (see that bullet); `cli.rs` gains only the tty check, one `read_line`, and the three-arm match — `Always` trusts this run **and** calls `remember_trusted_dir`, reusing the existing `trust_remembered_message` rather than writing a second one; `Once` trusts this run and prints nothing extra (the user just said yes, they know); `No` prints `trust_declined_message`, which **is** informational and so *is* silent under `is_quiet()`. **Placement is load-bearing and was verified, not assumed:** the block sits at ~`cli.rs:1436`, ahead of `gate_project_permissions` (~1758), `gate_mcp_sources` (~1792) and `gate_project_hooks` (~1805), so a `y` is honoured *this run* instead of arriving after the refusals it was meant to lift. **What did not change:** trust still grants exactly what it granted — all four gates are untouched and still decide that; the store format is unchanged; there is still no un-trust flag and still **no config key**, because trust must never be grantable by a file the repo itself ships, which is the whole reason the store is user-level (a project-local `.yoyo.toml` that could answer its own trust question would defeat the boundary it is being asked about). The prompt fires at most once per process, and only when the config is **project-local** and `project_trust_grants` is **non-empty** — so a home/XDG config, or a project `.yoyo.toml` that grants nothing (this repo's own, which sets `continue_on_silence`), is byte-identical to before.
- `cli_config.rs` — CLI constants (VERSION, thresholds, SYSTEM_PROMPT — now structured as named sectioned behavioral defaults: role, evidence/anti-fabrication, search craft, change discipline, bounded verification), Config struct, ContextStrategy/OutputFormat enums, effective context token state (extracted from `cli.rs`, re-exported by it)
- `commands.rs` — slash command dispatch, grouped /help, custom command discovery (loads user-defined `.md` files from `.yoyo/commands/` and `~/.yoyo/commands/`)
- `dispatch.rs` — REPL `/command` routing (`dispatch_command`), `CommandResult`, `DispatchContext`
- `dispatch_sub.rs` — CLI subcommand routing (`try_dispatch_subcommand` for `yoyo <subcmd>`), `flag_value`, `FlagValueCheck`, `require_flag_value`. Routes `yoyo risk [snapshot|validate|history|predict|accuracy|effectiveness|--all]` to `commands_risk::handle_risk`, making the risk subsystem callable from any non-interactive context (the prerequisite for the harness or a human to record daily risk snapshots where the DREAM.md measurement data actually accumulates). Day 165: the **bare-word path carries a near-miss guard** — before Day 165 a single unrouted word (`yoyo tokens`, `yoyo statsu`) fell straight through to the single-prompt path and was executed as an **LLM prompt** with write-capable tools attached, silently spending API tokens to "answer" a mistyped command; the REPL side had `commands::suggest_command`/`closest_match` and the sibling CLI entry path had no guard at all (the Day-162 "a policy's reach is set by the wrapper it was written for" shape). `bare_word_arg` (pure) admits only the unambiguous shape — exactly `yoyo <word>`, `args.len() == 2`, no leading `-`, no whitespace — and `bare_word_near_miss` (pure: no I/O, no exit) returns the message or `None`. Candidates are **derived, never hand-listed**: `ROUTED_SUBCOMMANDS` (37 verbs — count corrected Day 177 blind round 77; it read 36 while `security` sat uncounted in the const — a second copy of the dispatcher's match arms) plus every `commands::KNOWN_COMMANDS` entry the dispatcher does *not* route (`repl_only_commands`), and the distance budget reuses `commands::closest_match` at the REPL's own edit-distance threshold (`near_miss_threshold` — distance ≤2 for words of ≤5 chars, ≤3 for longer; measured over chars where `suggest_command` measures bytes) rather than hand-rolling a second distance function. It mirrors that threshold only — `suggest_command`'s unique-prefix shortcut is not reproduced, so a short prefix that is far in edit distance stays a prompt. Two drift guards run in **opposite directions** over the dispatcher's source (`test_routed_subcommands_matches_the_dispatcher`): a verb routed but missing from the table would make the guard fire on a word that works, and a verb left in the table after being unrouted would make the guard stay silent on a word that is now a paid prompt. The one call site is in `main.rs`, immediately after `apply_cli_flags` and **before** `parse_args`/any agent build, so a fired guard prints to stderr and exits 2 having spent zero tokens. **What it deliberately does not do:** it does **not** add `tokens`/`cost`/`context` (or any other REPL-only verb) as real subcommands — an exact REPL-only name is told where the command lives (`/tokens`) and given the `yoyo -p "tokens"` escape hatch, and wiring those verbs as subcommands is separate work; a single word that resembles **nothing** (`zzqqx`) is still sent as a prompt, unchanged; and multi-word args (`yoyo do the thing`), flagged args, and `-p` are untouched byte-for-byte, because bare positional args as a prompt is a real feature. Day 166 (#754): the `"goal"` arm refuses `check` **before** dispatch — the pure `goal_args_need_session` mirrors `handle_goal`'s own parse (the exact-equality `check` arm reached via `input.strip_prefix("/goal").trim()`, `src/commands_goal.rs:157`) and returns early, so a non-interactive invocation whose only output is a refusal does **not** execute the project-local verify command from `.yoyo/goal_verify.md`; previously `handle_goal` ran that command while building its prompt and the refusal ("requires an interactive session") printed *after* the side effect it appeared to be declining, so the message's implied "nothing ran" is now true and stated outright. The post-dispatch `SendToAgent` check is kept as a belt-and-braces fallback for a future handler change. The **trust gate** for `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` — the same project-authored-file question as #748/#749 — was filed forward from here on #754 and **landed on Day 167 as #761**, in `commands_goal.rs` (`gate_goal_verify` / `goal_verify_refusal_message` / the `verify_command_if_permitted` seam — see that bullet); this pre-dispatch refusal is kept beside it as the earlier and cheaper stop for the non-interactive door, since it returns before `handle_goal` is ever entered and so needs no gate consultation at all. Day 169: `"ast"` and `"def"` arms were added (34 → 36 verbs), both delegating to the existing `commands_ast_grep::handle_ast_grep` / `commands_search::handle_def` with args re-joined verbatim by the pre-existing `join_args_as_command` — the near-miss guard only inspects the two-token `yoyo <word>` shape, so `yoyo ast '$X.unwrap()' src/` and `yoyo def handle_goal` were falling through to the single-prompt path and spending API tokens; four of the six search commands (`find`/`grep`/`index`/`outline`) already had doors, making this the third "two doors, one works" instance in four days (#745, #767).
- `help.rs` — canonical source for all help content: `cli_help_text()` (`--help` output), `/help` REPL help, per-command detailed help (re-exports data from `help_data.rs`)
- `help_data.rs` — static command help text and short descriptions: `command_help()`, `command_short_description()` (pure data, extracted from `help.rs`). **Data only** — the drift-guard test suite that used to live at the bottom of this file was moved verbatim to `src/help_data_guards.rs` (Day 166), because the file was 1995 lines against a fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` and the next fix that has to land here (#757, `/pr` number-first vs verb-first) costs more than the 5 lines of headroom that were left.
- `help_data_guards.rs` — `#[cfg(test)]`-only module (declared `#[cfg(test)] mod help_data_guards;` in `main.rs`, so it never reaches a release build) holding the subcommand-drift guard suite moved out of `help_data.rs` on Day 166 — a **pure move**: same tests, same names, same fixture, the only edit being `use super::*;` → `use crate::help_data::*;`. Test paths are now `help_data_guards::tests::…`. Its concern was always different from the data half's: these guards read *other modules'* source text off disk to compare three copies of the subcommand vocabulary. Two subcommand-drift guards run over the same `subcommand_tables()` fixture (20 `(cmd, prefix, table)` triples), in **opposite directions**: `test_every_subcommand_table_is_documented_in_usage_form` compares the completion table against the prose help (copy 2 vs copy 3), and Day 164's `test_every_subcommand_table_token_appears_in_its_dispatcher` compares it against the **dispatcher** (copy 2 vs copy 1) — the direction both real bugs lived in (`/todo list`, #702; `/map --depth`, Day 164), and the direction a prose check is structurally blind to, because the help entry documents the phantom too. It reads each command's dispatcher module (`dispatcher_source`, a total match — a new entry in `subcommand_tables()` without one panics rather than being skipped) via `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, truncates at the `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` marker (built at runtime so it can't match itself; a bare `#[cfg(test)]` split would blank `src/git.rs`, which carries such guards inside real functions), strips `*_SUBCOMMANDS` table declarations (13 of the 20 tables live in the same file as their dispatcher — without this the token would be found in the table itself and the test would be vacuously green), and asserts each non-flag token appears as a quoted literal `"token"`. **What it does not catch, plainly:** it is a *presence* check, not a match-arm parse — a literal appearing anywhere in the non-test half of the module counts as present, even in a printed hint, an error string, or an arm for a different command. Day 164 (#730) added `/checkpoint` and `/context` (18 → 20 triples), and `/checkpoint` is the sharpest live instance of that weakness: `handle_checkpoint` and `handle_fork` share `src/commands_fork.rs`, and `list`/`delete` are arms in *both*, so those two tokens would read as present even if `handle_checkpoint` dropped them — the `"checkpoint"` arm of `dispatcher_source` says so in a comment rather than implying coverage it doesn't have. Verbs routed on a *compound* literal are listed in `KNOWN_INDIRECT`, each with the `file:line` that handles it (currently `/config show|edit|set|get` and `/lint fix`, all routed in `src/dispatch.rs` on the full command string); a bare entry is how a deleted feature keeps breathing.
- `config.rs` — permission config, directory restrictions, MCP server config, TOML parsing helpers. Day 177 (blind round 76, the #1 dark room at 39 snapshots unobserved and never studied — this was an **unpredicted find while falsifying h2**, not a graded bet, and the round's ledger says so): `resolve_path` **expands a leading `~`** before doing anything else. It didn't, and `fs::canonicalize` doesn't either (the shell does that, and a config file never goes through a shell), so a `~/…` string was treated as *relative* and joined onto the cwd — `deny = ["~/.ssh"]` resolved to `$CWD/~/.ssh`, a directory that cannot exist, so the entry could never match. That exact line is the worked example in `docs/src/configuration/permissions.md:111`, which makes this the dangerous direction: a user copies the documented way to fence off their SSH keys, believes it worked, and the file tools reach `~/.ssh` unimpeded. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `expand_tilde_with(path, home)` with the `HOME` lookup in the `expand_tilde` wrapper, and it is **deliberately narrow** — only a bare `~` and a `~/` prefix expand; `~bob/.ssh` is returned verbatim (resolving another account's home needs a passwd lookup this crate does not do, and guessing `<home_parent>/bob` would invent a fence the user never wrote), and a missing/empty `HOME` leaves the path unchanged rather than rewriting it into something that resolves elsewhere. Pinned at the **emission point** — the `Result` a caller of `check_path` receives, never `resolve_path` one layer below — with the near-miss guard beside it (`~/.ssh` must block `$HOME/.ssh/id_rsa` **and** must not fence off the rest of `$HOME`), plus the pure table for the branches that must *not* expand; both emission-point tests were verified to fail against the old body first, and they read the real `HOME` instead of setting it, so no process-global env var is mutated and they need no `#[serial]`. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #823):** this bullet used to say the two-matcher asymmetry "**still stands — `[directories]` has no glob support and this task did not add any**", and ended "**Deliberately not done: globbing was not added to `check_path`.** That is a behaviour change to a security control with asymmetric risk — a glob in `deny` that suddenly starts matching is an improvement, but a glob in `allow` that suddenly starts matching *widens a fence the user is currently living inside*; a test pins that a wildcard entry still matches nothing, so a future 'fix' that quietly adds globbing has to face that assertion." Both sentences were true for one day and are **false as of Day 178**: `[directories]` entries now glob. The Day-177 half of the story is kept below because it is the record of what that day shipped — a *warning*, which my own archive says is the strictly weaker of "make it visible" and "make it work" and must not close the issue — and because the handshake it left behind was **answered on purpose, not bypassed**: the test that pinned the old behaviour (`wildcard_entry_still_matches_nothing`) was **replaced deliberately, not deleted quietly**, and its successor (`wildcard_allow_entry_now_matches_instead_of_denying_everything`) carries the answer in its own doc comment. **What changed, and how narrow it is:** `DirectoryRestrictions::check_path` routes every entry — in **both** the deny loop and the allow `any()` — through the pure, table-tested `directory_entry_matches(entry, entry_resolved, candidate_resolved) -> bool`, with all path resolution left at the existing call site. Two branches, split by the **same** wildcard predicate the startup note uses (`entry_has_glob_meta`, extracted so "what counts as a wildcard" has one statement and the matcher and the note cannot drift apart): an entry with **no** `*`/`?` falls through to the unchanged `path_is_under` and is **byte-identical to before** — essentially every existing user, and the whole regression risk, pinned in both lists by `literal_entries_behave_exactly_as_before`; an entry **with** a wildcard is `glob_match`ed against the resolved candidate **and each of its ancestors**, so `deny = ["*/secrets"]` fences the files *inside* every matching directory (a directory entry already covers everything beneath it, which is what a user writing a path pattern means; `*` also spans `/`, so `secrets/*` reaches deep paths directly and the ancestor walk is for patterns whose wildcard is not in the last component). **Only entries that previously matched nothing change meaning.** No third matcher was written — both halves are the existing `glob_match` and `path_is_under`; `glob_match` itself was **not touched** (every `[permissions]` pattern in the world depends on it) and `[permissions]` is untouched, since it already globbed. **Why widening `allow` is safe here although widening a fence generally is not**, stated in `directory_entry_matches`'s doc comment and not only here: the Day-177 argument is true in general and false in this case, because a wildcard `allow` entry matched **nothing**, so nobody was living inside that fence — they were locked out of every file and the tool was unusable. Going from "refuses everything" to "allows exactly what the user wrote" is not the loosening of a working restriction; it is the difference between broken and working. Pinned at the **emission point** — the `Result` a caller of `check_path` receives, never the helper one layer below: `deny = ["secrets/*"]` blocks `secrets/api/key.txt` (the fails-open hole, closed) **and** does not block `src/main.rs` (the near-miss guard, since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green), `allow = ["src/*"]` permits what it matches and still refuses `/etc/passwd`, deny still overrides a matching allow across the wildcard branch, plus the pure table for the branch decision itself; the positive control was run — with the wildcard branch removed the two glob tests fail. `docs/src/configuration/permissions.md` gained a `Wildcards in [directories]` section stating support with one worked example per direction, using the shapes those tests pin. **The Day-177 warning was corrected, not dropped, and the choice was deliberate** (the two options were "reduced note" or "delete"): the confusing behaviour it described is gone, so a note claiming entries match nothing would itself be the false sentence — but the two blocks still glob *different subjects*, and one genuine trap survives, so one line is still worth printing. It now confirms the entry was read as a **path pattern**, names the list and consequence per entry verbatim, says `*` spans `/`, and names the remaining trap: `glob_match` implements `*` and **not** `?`, so a `?` stays a literal character in either block. What is unchanged from Day 177 and still true: the decision half is the pure `wildcard_directory_entries(&DirectoryRestrictions) -> Vec<(DirectoryList, String)>` (scans `allow` then `deny`, returning offending entries verbatim) and `directory_wildcard_warning(entries, plain) -> Option<String>` — **`None` for an empty slice, the byte-identical common path**, so every user who writes no wildcard sees output unchanged; glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`, silent under `is_quiet()`, mirroring `project_permission_refusal_message`; detection is `*` and `?` only (`*` is what `glob_match` honours, `?` is over-reported on purpose because a user who writes it meant a wildcard and will otherwise get a silent literal match; `[` is deliberately **not** detected, being a plausible literal); and **both** `parse_directories_from_config` call sites stay wired — `parse_permission_and_dir_config` in `cli.rs` (the startup door) and the `"permissions"` subcommand arm in `dispatch_sub.rs` (the door a user walks through *specifically to check what is in force*) — because a per-token pass is not a per-entry-point pass. Day 166 (#748) added **config provenance**: `load_config_file`'s first-wins chain (`./.yoyo.toml` → `~/.yoyo.toml` → XDG) records the winning path in a `OnceLock` at each of its three return rungs (`record_loaded_config_path`), and `loaded_config_is_project_local()` answers "did the *project* supply this config?" from that recorded winner — there is exactly one statement of the search order, never a second precedence ladder to drift against it. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `config_path_is_project_local(path, cwd, home)`: the file must be one of `CONFIG_FILE_NAMES`, resolve (relative paths against `cwd`) to a file whose parent *is* `cwd`, and — pinned explicitly, not left accidental — `cwd == home` returns **false**, because `~/.yoyo.toml` reached as `./.yoyo.toml` is still the user's own config. The `OnceLock` is deliberately write-once: a later `cd` must not retroactively change the provenance the session already acted on. Provenance is only *reported* here; the gate that consumes it lives in `cli.rs`. Day 172 (#794): `parse_continue_on_silence_from_config` joins the `config_flag`-shaped family of boolean readers (`auto_watch`, `quiet`, `no_bell`, …) — **default `false`, opt-in**, so a user who never writes the key gets byte-identical behaviour; three-state unit test (absent → `false`, `= true` → `true`, `= false` → `false`). `"continue_on_silence"` was added to `SETTABLE_KEYS` with its matching `validate_config_value` arm in the same diff, so `/config set continue_on_silence true` validates and `/config get continue_on_silence` reads it back — a key nothing can set through the normal door is half a feature. Its consumer is the single `set_continue_on_silence()` site in `cli.rs`, and this repo's own `.yoyo.toml` sets it (see the `main.rs` bullet for why the config key rather than a flag). Day 178 (self-discovered; transferred class — Claude Code v2.1.246 shipped a *warning* for the same shape): **a `permissions.allow` wildcard sitting before a literal swallowed option tokens.** `glob_match` only requires prefix / suffix / in-order-middle matching, so `allow = ["git * main"]` — which a user writes meaning "the `*` is the subcommand slot" — also matched `git -c core.sshCommand=<arbitrary command> push main`, auto-approving an arbitrary command **for the whole session** from a pattern that looks narrow. **Verified before writing any fix rather than reasoned about**, because the task's own step 0 was to stop if the reading was wrong: a standalone transcription of `glob_match` compiled with `rustc` returned `true` for `git * main` vs `git -c core.sshCommand=curl_evil.sh push main`, and the test added in this diff failed at exactly that row (`left: Some(true), right: None`) before the fix landed. Project-local configs are gated by the trust boundary (#748/#749), so the exposed path is the user's own `~/.yoyo.toml`, which is deliberately ungated — a real path, not a hypothetical one. **`glob_match` was not touched**: every `[permissions]` pattern in the world depends on it and, since #823, `[directories]` consumes it too — changing it would be a semantic change to a security control with two consumers. The fix is the pure `allow_wildcard_swallows_options(pattern, command)`, consulted **only** on the allow branch of `PermissionConfig::check`, and its precision is the whole task since a broader rule breaks legitimate configs: (1) it applies **only** to a **non-trailing** `*` — a non-empty literal segment must follow some `*`, so `cargo *`, `npm run *` and `git commit -m *` are honest "anything goes" patterns and stay **byte-identical to before**, which is essentially every existing user and the whole regression risk; (2) when it applies, the match is rejected if the command carries any whitespace-separated token starting with `-` that does not appear verbatim as a token in the pattern, so `git * --force` still auto-approves `git push --force`. **Rejection means "not auto-approved", never "refused"** — the command falls through to the normal confirmation prompt, which is graceful degradation by design and the reason no warning or refusal message was added; the doc comment says so outright so a later reader does not helpfully add one. **`deny` keeps plain `glob_match`, verbatim, and this asymmetry is written into the code rather than only here:** narrowing an `allow` removes privilege (safe), while narrowing a `deny` makes a fence stop matching — i.e. it **fails open**, so a naive mirror onto the deny branch would be a regression dressed as a security fix. Same asymmetry `cli::gate_project_permissions` already encodes (#749 item 3); `[directories]` is untouched. Pinned at the **emission point** — the `Option<bool>` a caller of `check` receives, never the helper one layer below — with all four near-miss guards beside the two hole rows (trailing wildcard still auto-approves; an option token written verbatim in the pattern still auto-approves; a wildcard-free pattern is untouched; a `deny` pattern still blocks the option-carrying command), since a discriminator tested only on the side that blocks is vacuous green. **Positive control run rather than assumed**: neutering the helper to always return `false` fails the hole row and restoring it returns green. **The limit, stated rather than implied: this narrows `allow` matching only, and it is not a claim that `permissions.allow` is now safe against every option-injection shape** — it closes the wildcard-swallows-options class and nothing wider.
- `context.rs` — project context loading (reads YOYO.md, CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, .cursorrules, .github/copilot-instructions.md), file listing, git status, recently changed files, project-type convention hints
- `conversations.rs` — side, quick, and extended conversation handlers (extracted from `repl.rs`): `build_add_content_blocks`, `handle_side`, `handle_quick`, `handle_extended`
- `providers.rs` — provider constants (KNOWN_PROVIDERS), API key env vars, default/known models per provider
- `format/mod.rs` — Color, constants, utility functions, re-exports, contextual command hints (`HintContext`, `contextual_hint`); context-budget warnings are split into a pure core (`budget_warning_threshold` — the 60/80/90/95 ladder, `max == 0` → `None`; `budget_warning_message` — the four strings) plus `context_budget_warning_with(&AtomicU32, ..)` which takes its escalation state as a parameter, so tests drive local state instead of the process-wide `LAST_WARNED_THRESHOLD`; `context_budget_warning`/`reset_context_budget_warning` keep their exact public behavior and one `#[serial]` test pins that the public pair is still wired to the global. Day 177 (the first payment against `tests/global_state_races.rs`'s debt register) applied that same seam to the file's other three racing tests, in the gate's own stated order of preference — **pass the value explicitly, never `#[serial]`**, which only serialises against other `#[serial]` tests and leaves unmarked *readers* of the same global exposed: `usage_print_line(quiet, verbose, ..) -> Option<String>` (the decision half of `print_usage`; **both** globals it used to read are parameters, and `None` means "print nothing"), `context_usage_line(quiet, used, max) -> Option<String>` (the decision half of `print_context_usage`, with both suppression branches — quiet, and an unknown context window `max == 0` — in one place), and `contextual_hint_with(&mut HashSet<&'static str>, ctx)` (the decision half of `contextual_hint`, taking the shown-hints set as a parameter exactly as `context_budget_warning_with` takes its escalation state). **Production behaviour is byte-identical** — each wrapper is the only global read and preserves the original read order, including `print_usage`'s `!quiet && is_verbose()` short-circuit — and the three tests now drive the pure cores and assert **both** directions instead of the previous "must not panic" no-ops. The wrappers are pinned by a deliberately **weak** source-level test that reads this file's own source (needle built at runtime so it cannot match itself) and asserts each wrapper still calls its global reader; it proves the call is **present**, not that its result is used, and it was verified to fail against a broken wrapper before landing. Two `format/mod.rs` tests stay in the register with the reason written beside them — `test_enable_quiet_is_callable` and `test_disable_notify_is_callable` have no core to extract, because the function under test *is* the setter and the assertion is `f() == f()`; see the shared-global gate paragraph above.
- `format/diff.rs` — LCS-based line diff algorithm, colored unified diff rendering
- `format/output.rs` — tool output compression, filtering, truncation, batch summary, indentation. Day 164: `filter_test_output` collapses pass lines only when the same output also carries a runner **summary** line (`test result:`, pytest `=== N passed ===`, jest `Tests:`, go `ok pkg 0.12s`) or a cargo `running N tests` header — a `✓` glyph is a shape, a runner summary is provenance, and collapsing on shape alone was deleting non-test bash output (npm/docker/eslint/yoyo's own `/doctor`, and observed live eating `/risk accuracy`'s `✓ src/…` hit lists) while labelling it "N passing tests omitted"
- `format/highlight.rs` — syntax highlighting for code, JSON, YAML, TOML. Day 169 (#759): under `norm == "rust"` **only**, a `'` no longer unconditionally opens a string/char literal. Rust lifetimes (`&'a str`, `fn f<'a>(…)`, `'static`, `'_`) never close their tick, so from that character on the *rest of the line* rendered in string colour — every Rust source line containing a lifetime displayed wrong, anywhere yoyo prints highlighted Rust (file display, `/find`/`/grep` context, tool-output rendering). The discrimination is **not** a second implementation: `is_rust_char_literal` delegates to `commands_refactor::char_literal_len`, the same helper `significant_braces` uses, made `pub(crate)` with this consumer in the same diff — so the char-literal-vs-lifetime rule has one statement and one table test, not a third quote scanner drifting from the other two. That reuse also closed a gap the highlighter's own deleted copy had: `'\u{1F600}'` now highlights as a char literal instead of rendering unstyled. **Named classes covered** (this is not a claim to lex Rust): `'x'`, `'\n'`, `'\''`, `'\u{7d}'` char literals highlight as before; a `'` that opens no *closed* literal is emitted as plain text, so the lifetime tick is inert and the code after it keeps its normal colours. **Deliberately unchanged:** every non-Rust language — Python, JS, shell and the rest still treat `'…'` as a genuine string, byte-identical to before, because the guard is gated on the language. **Cross-line state (Day 173) — the gap to its sibling is now closed for every delimiter class either of them models, and what is left out is named rather than implied:** this highlighter used to be **line-based with no cross-line state at all**, so a `/* … */` block comment and a string literal spanning lines were both mis-coloured from the second line on, while `significant_braces` carried both facts in a `BraceScanState` (#771 items 1+2, Day 171 — but that issue was about the *scanner that rewrites files* and touched nothing on this display path). Three Day-173 tasks carried those facts over here into `HighlightState`, which `highlight_code_line_with(lang, line, &mut state)` threads across lines: (a) `block_comment_depth`, a **depth** not a bool, so Rust's nested `/* /* */ */` is one comment while the other C-family languages close on the first `*/`, and a stray `*/` at depth 0 is ordinary code rather than an underflow; (b) `open_string`, a plain `"…"` left open at end of line, set **only** where such a literal really spans lines (`multiline_strings` — Rust today), whose content is emitted string-coloured so `/*`, `//` and keywords inside it are inert and ordinary highlighting resumes just past the closer. (c) Day 173 (#806) carried the remaining two delimiter classes — Rust **raw** strings (`r"…"` / `r#"…"#` at any hash count, `b` prefix allowed) and **backtick** literals — which turned that field from a `bool` into `Option<StringDelim>`, so the delimiter *type* is carried rather than merely the fact that something is open. It is **not** a third scanner: `commands_refactor::raw_string_open` and `close_open_string` were made `pub(crate)` with this consumer landing in the same diff, the highlighter's own private single-purpose `close_open_string` copy was **deleted**, and `StringDelim` grew one variant (`Backtick { escapes }`) whose match arm was added at the same time — the same one-rule-one-table discipline #759 used for `char_literal_len`. Backticks are gated per language by `backtick_strings(norm) -> Option<bool>`: `js` → spans lines and honours `` \` ``, `go` → spans lines with **no** escapes (a `\` before the closing backtick does not protect it, because Go's backtick literal is raw), everything else `None`, so a backtick in rust/c/python stays inert and those languages are byte-identical. **Still not carried, and this is the whole residue:** `${…}` interpolation inside a JS template literal is treated as string content, not code, so an interpolated expression is not highlighted — this colours a line, it does not lex one. `highlight_code_line(lang, line)` is kept as the stateless wrapper and **is** the byte-identity baseline the regression tests assert against: languages with no block comments (python, shell, yaml, toml, json) and every non-Rust language reset the carried flags, and a line with no carried state and no block comment takes a single-segment fast path, so their output is byte-identical to the pre-state behaviour. Pinned at the **emission point**: tests call `highlight_code_line` / `highlight_code_line_with` and assert the string a caller receives (lifetime case, `'x'`/`'\n'` pass-through, unchanged python/js/shell single-quoted strings, and — for the cross-line half — line *sequences* driven through one carried state: a raw string's second line, an `r##"` hash count, a JS template literal, a Go raw string, the js-vs-go escape-rule difference, a backtick left inert in rust/c, plus a byte-identity check of the stateless path for the single-line forms of all of them).
- `format/highlight_lang.rs` — the eight per-language predicates and tables the highlighter reads (`normalize_lang`, `lang_keywords`, `lang_types`, `comment_prefix`, `supports_block_comments`, `block_comments_nest`, `multiline_strings`, `backtick_strings`), all `pub(super)` and each answering only *what is true of language X?* — none of them touches `HighlightState`, a `&mut` scanner, or an output string, which is the seam the split was taken at. A **pure Day-174 move** out of `format/highlight.rs` (2048 → 1682 lines) to clear the fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` gate, which the file was sitting 48 past inside the 50-line grace band: no body was rewritten, no test moved (all 95 stay in `highlight.rs` and were not edited), and nothing about highlighting behaviour changed. The names above are still referenced by the `format/highlight.rs` bullet — they simply live one file over.
- `format/cost.rs` — pricing, cost display, token formatting; fleet-model pricing (claude-fable-5, opus-5, opus-4-8, sonnet-5, haiku-4-5) is read at runtime from yoagent 0.9's preset `ModelConfig.cost` via `agent_builder::anthropic_preset` (preset is the source of truth), all other models use the local pricing table. That "preset is the source of truth" rule is what `test_estimate_cost_sonnet_5_preset` derives its expected value from rather than hardcoding a literal — yoagent 0.16.6 corrected the sonnet-5 preset from $3/$15 to $2/$10 (it had been carrying Sonnet 4.6 pricing) and main was red for 31 hours reporting an upstream *fix* as a yoyo regression. Day 177 corrected a **false mechanism** in that test's comment and recorded the superseded claim rather than erasing it: it read "because `Cargo.lock` is gitignored", which was true when written and went stale on 2026-08-24 at `0577bfe7` (which tracked the lockfile and added `--locked` to every CI invocation that resolves dependencies). The route the comment described is therefore closed; the reason to derive from the preset is not. Verified at correction time: `git ls-files --error-unmatch Cargo.lock` succeeds and `git check-ignore -v Cargo.lock` finds nothing. This is the `src/gasp.rs` class exactly — a wrong mechanism in a comment, read as authoritative by the next agent who opens the file, which cost eight sessions on #683 — and a dated measurement of my own repo decays just like one of a dependency, so the present tense was what made it read as a standing fact. Day 178 (reading #7, recorded in full above): the file's **two judgment clamps were extracted behind `fn` boundaries** — `context_fill_fraction(used, max)` (the bar never over-draws past full; an unknown window is empty, not full) and `breakdown_divisor(total)` (`total.max(1)`, so a zero total cannot divide by zero and the floor is 1 rather than a special-cased branch) — **purely to widen the mutant population**, since cargo-mutants' `FnValue` genre is function-granular and so a clamp is invisible to the dial until it owns a `fn`. Production behaviour is **byte-identical** (pure moves of an expression, no threshold/rounding/branch change), the count went **350 → 355**, and each has a table test covering both the ordinary pass-through row *and* the case the clamp exists for (`used > max` → exactly `1.0`, `max == 0` → `0.0`, `total == 0` → `1`) — a clamp tested only where it does not fire is vacuous green, and the pass-through rows are specifically what kill the `FnValue with 1` mutant. The file's other two `.max()` sites (`:545`, `:606`) are **`Iterator::max` reductions, not clamps**, and were deliberately left alone with a comment saying why.
- `format/markdown.rs` — MarkdownRenderer for streaming markdown output
- `format/tools.rs` — Spinner, ToolProgressTimer, ActiveToolState, ThinkBlockFilter; plain-output (screen reader) switch: `set_plain_output`/`is_plain_output` (AtomicBool, set by `--screen-reader` in `cli.rs`, which also enables the existing no-color path) — when on, Spinner prints one static "running <tool>…" line and ToolProgressTimer never repaints (no `\r`, no ANSI cursor escapes); default off, byte-identical to prior behavior. Day 174 (blind round 63, the ranking's darkest file at 203 snapshots unobserved): that parenthesised promise was **false at teardown**, and this doc line was itself the assertion the code broke — `ToolProgressTimer::stop` and `ToolProgressTimer::drop` gated the erase on `stderr_is_terminal()` **alone**, so `--screen-reader` printed its one linear line via `plain_tool_progress_line` and then wrote `\r\x1b[K` straight into a screen reader's stream. The twin was the tell: `Spinner::stop`/`drop` were already correct (`stderr_is_terminal() && !is_plain_output()`), so one struct obeyed plain output at teardown and its sibling did not — the paint path was swept, the teardown path was not. All **four** teardown sites now route through one writer (`write_teardown_clear`) over the pure, table-tested `teardown_clear_sequence(is_terminal, plain) -> Option<&'static str>`, which returns the literal erase only for a non-plain terminal; `animations_enabled` was deleted because its only two callers were two of those four sites, so the rule has one statement rather than three. **Non-plain users are byte-identical** — the predicate the Spinner already used is the one now shared, and only the plain branch changes. Pinned at the emission point (`write_teardown_clear` writes exactly the returned string and nothing else, so the tested string is the string a caller receives): the four-state table, a plain-mode assertion that the emitted text contains neither `\r` nor `\x1b` and is empty, and a source-level guard that all four sites still share the one writer (needle built at runtime so it cannot match itself). **What the round did not find, stated rather than implied:** `ThinkBlockFilter::flush` (called at `prompt.rs:685`) takes the buffer but never clears `in_block` — harmless today because `PromptEventState::new()` builds a fresh filter per prompt, so nothing reuses one; and neither struct consults `is_quiet()`, which is deliberate, not an oversight — `--quiet`'s documented scope is "informational stderr output (config/context loading messages)" (`help.rs:175`) and a self-erasing animation is neither.
- `gasp.rs` — GASP recorder wiring, **recording** (#683 steps 1–2). A **default-off `gasp` cargo feature** (`Cargo.toml`: `default = []`, `gasp = ["yoagent/gasp"]` — a plain `cargo build`/`cargo test`, which is what yoyo runs while modifying itself, never compiles `yoagent-state`) gates the whole module, which is declared `#[cfg(feature = "gasp")]` in `main.rs`; nothing changes in a default build. An **env-gated open** (`open_recorder_from_env` → `GaspRecorder::open(root, "yoyo", "yoyo-inproc", GoalRef::Existing(id))`; the worker id is deliberately distinct from the retiring sidecar's, because mixed-writer history is what that field is for) yields a recorder that `main.rs` hands to `gasp::install`, a process-global `OnceLock` (a session opens at most one store — a second install is a bug, not a reconfiguration, so it is ignored rather than swapping stores mid-session). **Run and tool events are now recorded**: `tee_prompt` / `tee_prompt_messages` call `GaspRecorder::recording_sender(task, Some(forward_tx))` and `Agent::prompt_with_sender(...)` / `prompt_messages_with_sender(...)`, returning the *forwarded* stream so the caller sees exactly what `agent.prompt*` would have given it, and `None` when no recorder is installed. `src/prompt.rs` has **exactly four** agent-start call sites (`run_prompt_once`, `run_prompt_once_with_messages`, `run_prompt_stream_json`, `run_prompt_stream_json_with_content`) and **all four** route through one local seam pair (`start_prompt` / `start_prompt_messages`) which tees when a recorder is installed and falls back to `agent.prompt*` otherwise — one seam, so recording is on for all four or none; a partial sweep reads as done. The recording `JoinHandle` is deliberately detached: the task ends when yoagent drops the sender at loop end. **Redaction ships in the same diff, on purpose** — recorded summaries land in a *shareable* git repo, which is a one-way door, so the recorder is opened with `.with_summarizer(crate::safety::redact_secrets)` and every persisted tool-arg/output summary passes through the pure `redact_secrets` first (which lives in `src/safety.rs` since Day 165, so it and its tests **are** exercised by the default test run) (masks `sk-…`/`sk-ant-…`, `ghp_`/`gho_`/`ghu_`/`ghs_`/`ghr_`, `github_pat_`, `AKIA…`, and `KEY=`/`TOKEN=`/`SECRET=`/`PASSWORD=` style assignments with `[redacted]`, keeping the variable name so a redacted log still says *which* credential appeared). It is a mask, not a guarantee: a novel secret shape passes through. The decision half is pure and testable: `plan_from_env_values(root, goal)` reads the two env values as `Option<&str>` and returns three explicit states — `Disabled` (no `YOYO_GASP_STATE_DIR`; the normal case on every machine that isn't the evolve runner — silent, warns nothing, creates nothing, needs no credentials), `Misconfigured` (dir set but `YOYO_GASP_GOAL_ID` unset — the operator asked for recording and won't get it, so this one says so), and `Open`. A configured-but-nonexistent directory degrades to `None` silently; an open failure returns `None` after **one** honest stderr line naming what failed. It never propagates, never panics, never aborts startup — a session must not break because instrumentation did. Day 167 (#683 item 5) ported the **named half** of `tools/gasp-emit`'s session-graph emission into this module: **`session-start` (`session_start`) and `task` (`task_planned`)**, plus the `ensure_goal` helper they share and the `DEFAULT_GOAL` const value, all verbatim. Day 168 added the third arm, **`session-end` (`session_end`)**: it resolves the goal through the existing `session_goal`, names the outcome default (`DEFAULT_SESSION_OUTCOME = "done"`, via `session_outcome`, so absence has a name instead of being absorbed by an inline `unwrap_or`), parses the sidecar's comma-separated `--extra` projection list with `parse_extra_paths` (**the same three operations in the same order** — `split(',')` → `map(str::trim)` → `filter(!is_empty)` — because a differently-parsed path list writes a *different boundary commit*), then `record_run_finished` → `commit_run` → `release_lease` and **returns** the boundary sha rather than printing it (the sidecar printed because it was a CLI; in-process the caller decides). Unlike its two siblings it has **no** store-generic `*_in` body, and that is a constraint rather than an oversight: `commit_run`/`release_lease` are `GitEventStore` *inherent* methods, not `EventStore` trait methods, so it cannot be written against a generic store and cannot be driven against a scratch one — the two pure helpers are table-tested, `session_end` itself is compile-tested only, and its doc comment says so. **`task-result` was ported on Day 177** — all four sidecar arms now live here. It had been unported-but-unblocked since Day 172; the port finally landed as a creator-authored patch after **eight** revert receipts (#763, #765, #782, #785, #787, #789, #803) recorded sessions that opened `src/gasp.rs`, read the then-authoritative "unreachable" claim, and exited with an empty diff. The blocker was never upstream: `ProjectRef`/`ArtifactRef` (0.16.4) and `PatchStatus` (0.16.5) are re-exported from `yoagent::gasp`, verified again at port time. Like its three siblings it shipped **dormant** at port time (`#[allow(dead_code)]`, consumer named as #683 items (3)+(7)) — no longer true as of #827/#831; see the correction below. The stale claim that stopped it read as follows — recorded rather than erased, because it is the whole mechanism: the module doc in `src/gasp.rs` asserted it was *unreachable* because it names `ProjectRef`, `ArtifactRef` and `PatchStatus` (`patch.base_project_ref`, `patch.artifacts`, `update_patch_status(…, PatchStatus::Promoted, …)`, `tools/gasp-emit/src/main.rs:166-268`), and all three are reachable as of yoagent **0.16.5**: `ProjectRef`/`ArtifactRef` landed in 0.16.4 (#115) and `PatchStatus` in 0.16.5 (#117), so `task-result` is **portable — no upstream blocker remains**. Day 172: that stale sentence was the *mechanism* behind five empty-diff reverts on #683 item (5) (#765, #782, #785, #787, #789) plus the docs-only #788 — every one of those agents opened `src/gasp.rs`, read an authoritative "unreachable" that had been true against 0.16.3 and went stale when the pin moved, and exited without a diff; `CLAUDE.md` said the opposite, but the half an agent opens to do the work is the half that said impossible. Both the module doc and the inline comment above the session-graph section now state the correction *and* record the superseded claim (a correction that erases the wrong claim teaches nothing about why five sessions died), and the corrected claim carries a machine-readable `yoagent-version-claim` marker pinned to `Cargo.lock`'s resolved `yoagent` version by `tests/doc_version_claims.rs` (see the dependency-version-claim gate above) — an integration test, because this module is behind the default-off `gasp` feature and a `#[cfg(test)]` guard inside it would never run in a plain `cargo test`. Day 177 (#821) widened that test from one hardcoded file to a scan and **retired its old exactly-once assertion**: once the marker is a convention rather than a single instance, "exactly one in the repo" forbids the second consumer, so the invariant that survives is *every marker agrees with `Cargo.lock`* plus *at least one marker exists*. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased:** this bullet then read "that task corrected the claim and nothing else: `task-result` remains unported — unblocked is not ported". True from Day 172 until Day 177, when the port landed; the distinction it drew (unblocked is not ported) is still the right one and is why the gap stayed visible for five days instead of reading as closed. Compile-verified Day 171 against the command's *complete* type surface (12 types, extracted mechanically from `tools/gasp-emit/src/main.rs:166-268` rather than read): clean. That mechanical extraction matters — the operator's earlier "it builds" claim on #683 was **wrong** because the probe covered only the types named in the first ~60 lines and missed `update_patch_status`; the contradiction between that claim and this file's then-correct note is what sent Day 170 into a prove-the-blocker task that was reverted (#785). yoyo's skepticism was right both times. Shipping a `task-result` that silently dropped `base_project_ref`/`artifacts`/`update_patch_status` would be exactly the "half that reads as complete" the creator forbade. Naming the half is the deliverable, not a caveat: a partially-ported tier that names its parts is fine, one that reads as complete is not, so the module doc comment and this bullet both name all four arms, state that every one of them is dormant with zero wired consumers, and state that no `*_in` body is behaviourally tested. The sidecar mixes decisions with I/O and that shape was **not** copied — the decisions are plain non-async functions with table tests (`goal_for_kind` — the `--kind product` reroute to `goal_product_value`, every other kind keeps the goal it was given; `standing_goal_title` / `standing_goal_summary` — **byte-identical** to `tools/gasp-emit/src/main.rs:66-79`, because a goal id and its node text are *identities in a shared store*, so a reworded constant is a different graph rather than a nicer string; `task_node_id` — the `task_{run_id}_{num}` shape; `default_session_task_label`), and the three entry points that *can* be written generically (`session_start` / `task_planned` / `task_result`) delegate to a store-generic `*_in` body — `session_end` cannot, for the inherent-method reason above. **That generic split does not buy a behavioural test, and the docs no longer claim it does:** this bullet and two doc comments in `src/gasp.rs` used to say the `*_in` bodies are "testable against a scratch store"; they are not, because `MemoryEventStore` lives in `yoagent-state`, which `Cargo.toml` deliberately does not take as a direct dependency, and `yoagent::gasp` does not re-export it (probed at port time: the name does not resolve). **Zero tests drive any `*_in` body.** That sentence is itself a claim about what an external dependency cannot do, so it carries the second live `yoagent-version-claim` marker (`src/gasp.rs`, in `task_result`'s doc) — if upstream ever re-exports the store it goes stale silently and the `*_in` bodies stay untested for no reason. What is tested is the pure decision half of each arm (`goal_for_kind`, `standing_goal_title`, `task_node_id`, `eval_command_or_default`, `parse_extra_paths`) and the node-id shapes; the split is kept because it is the right shape and becomes testable the moment upstream re-exports the store. They take a `&GraphSession` — a **directly-opened** `GitEventStore` plus `YoAgentState::load`, built by `open_graph_session(plan, resume, worker)` (the third parameter since Day 179, #828 item 2 — see the end of this bullet) — and read `session.state()` / `session.store()` / `session.actor()` / `session.goal()`. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #831).** This sentence used to read: *"They take the `&GaspRecorder` and read `recorder.state()` / `recorder.actor()` / `recorder.goal()` — a second `GitEventStore` on the same root is **never** opened, because a GASP repo is single-writer behind a 600s lease and a second store collides with the recorder's rather than cooperating (measured Day 165; it is the whole reason the sidecar cannot run alongside)."* The lease fact it cites is still true and is **not** what was wrong; the conclusion drawn from it was, because it answered a question nobody was asking. Two *concurrent* stores do collide — but `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` emits **one event per process**, four short-lived `yoyo gasp` invocations per session, which are **sequential and never concurrent**, so there is no second live store to collide with. What there is instead is the opposite hazard: yoagent 0.16.6's `GaspRecorder::with_store` closes any run a *previous* process left open as `"interrupted"` on **every** open, so call 2 interrupted the run call 1 opened. Measured in #831: call 3 emitted `run.finished`, `task.created` landed **into a finished run**, and `session-end` failed with `cannot finish <run>: no run is open` — **no boundary commit**, so the session's state was never pushed, **with every call still exiting 0**. That last clause is why it survived the door landing and why the test pins *ordering*, not success. The fix is what `tools/gasp-emit` always did and is confined to how the arms acquire their handle: open the store directly, and `resume_open_run()` on every arm **except** `session-start` (`gasp_cli::needs_open_run`, table-tested), so an open run survives the process boundary instead of being closed by its own next event. **`GaspRecorder` remains the correct handle for the in-process run/model/tool tier (#683 item (3)) and that path is byte-identical** — `open_recorder`, `open_recorder_from_env`, `install`, `tee_prompt` / `tee_prompt_messages` and `prompt.rs`'s `start_prompt` seam were not touched: there it is one process and one open, so interrupt-on-open is the right rule. Pinned by `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs`, which spawns **four real `CARGO_BIN_EXE_yoyo` processes** (the defect does not exist inside one process — the interrupt happens *at open* — so a single-process test calling all four arms against one handle passes on the broken code) against a scratch git repo and asserts **position**: `run.finished` is the **last** domain event, there is **exactly one** of them (two means a run was reaped mid-session), `run.started < task.created < run.finished`, and `session-end` reports a boundary commit. Ordering rather than presence is load-bearing — both columns of #831's measured table emit all the same event kinds, so an exit-code or presence-set check **passes on the broken behaviour**; the presence set is kept only as the anti-vacuous guard, so a log that recorded nothing cannot satisfy "`run.finished` is last" by having nothing to order. Verified against the defect and not only in the passing direction: reintroducing `GaspRecorder::with_store` into `open_graph_session` produced `[goal.created, run.started, run.finished, task.created, …]` with `session-end` failing and committing no boundary. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #827 — and it went stale silently, which is the point).** This bullet used to read: *"All four **ship dormant and that is the correct outcome**: they compile, they unit-test, they record nothing, because their only consumers are #683 items (3)+(7) (the operator-lane env bridge) and wiring the env vars here would destroy the sidecar's session record — every path still lands in `RecorderPlan::Disabled`. `session_start`, `task_planned`, `session_end` and `task_result` are therefore the only items carrying `#[allow(dead_code)]`, each narrowly scoped with a comment naming (3)+(7) as the pending consumer."* True until `0aec6cf5` (Day 178, 21:24), when #827 gave all four a **CLI door** — `yoyo gasp <arm>`, parsed by `gasp_cli::parse_gasp_args` and run by `run_gasp_command`, routed from `dispatch_sub.rs:747` — and **that task did not update CLAUDE.md**, so this file asserted "dormant, records nothing" for a day about arms that had a live door. Checked at correction time rather than assumed: `grep -n 'allow(dead_code)' src/gasp.rs` returns **nothing** (the three remaining ones are in `gasp_cli.rs`, on the plain-build path), and `run_gasp_command` builds its plan from the `--state-dir` flag via `plan_from_env_values`, so it lands in `RecorderPlan::Open`, not `Disabled`. What is **still** true and is the part worth keeping: `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` still invokes the `tools/gasp-emit` sidecar, so the **operator lane has not been swapped** — that atomic swap is #683 item (7) and the env bridge is item (3), both still pending, and both were blocked behind #831 rather than behind anything upstream. The pure helpers are genuinely called by `ensure_goal` / `session_start_in` / `task_planned_in` / `session_end`, so they need no allow. **Two things remain untrue and should not be overstated:** the **default** `cargo test` run exercises nothing in this module — the feature is off, so it compiles into nothing, and `cargo build --features gasp` / `cargo test --features gasp` / `cargo clippy --all-targets --features gasp -- -D warnings` have to be run **by hand** locally (the redaction half is the one exception, and only because it no longer lives here); CI *does* run the featured test and the featured clippy (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:45,52`, since Day 165 `9dcccf48`), so a featured clippy warning is a red CI rather than a nit — the older claim that "CI does not build `--features gasp`" was true when written and has been false since that commit. And recording failures are logged by **yoagent** via `tracing` while the event stream keeps flowing — yoyo's UI does not surface them. **Day 179 (#828 item 2) — `--worker` is honoured, for the graph tier only, and the flag that was parsed-announced-and-ignored is gone.** `gasp_cli::parse_gasp_args` accepted `--worker` and then printed a stderr line saying it had **no effect**, because the graph-tier open hardcoded its id — *a capability with a description and no consumer*, which is the same shape as the "unreachable" claim that cost eight sessions. It matters on the one axis the #683 ordering constraint is about: a GASP repo is single-writer behind a **600s lease**, and `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` passes `--worker evolve-shim-$$`, so the pid suffix is what gives **overlapping sessions distinct lease identities** — a property the sidecar had and the port did not, and the operator cannot swap them until the door can honour it. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `gasp_cli::graph_worker_id(Option<&str>) -> String`, deliberately in `gasp_cli.rs` because that file compiles on the **plain** (non-`gasp`) build path, so `cargo test` actually reaches it — a decision behind the default-off feature would be untested by the run that gates every task. `None` → `DEFAULT_GRAPH_WORKER_ID`, and an empty or whitespace-only value → the same default, because **an empty worker id is not an identity**: refusing to open a store under one beats inventing a name. That fallback is announced by `worker_fallback_note` (a flag was typed and dropped, so say so — silent under nothing, since this is the operator lane), and the note is `None` when no flag was passed or the flag was real; both directions are pinned. **Passing no flag is byte-identical to before** — `DEFAULT_GRAPH_WORKER_ID` is the same literal `"yoyo-inproc"` the open hardcoded — which is the whole regression risk, and `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` is its check: those four real processes pass no `--worker`, so they exercise the default path unchanged. **`gasp::WORKER_ID` is a second, separate constant carrying the same literal, and the two must not be merged** — a code comment at the site says so: it is the **in-process** run/model/tool tier (#683 item (3), `open_recorder` / `open_recorder_from_env`), which is deliberately fixed, because a caller-supplied `evolve-shim-$$` there would make an in-process event indistinguishable from a sidecar-written one, and mixed-writer history is exactly what that field is for. Only the graph tier takes the flag. **Stated plainly: this does not complete #683.** Item (3) (the env bridge) and item (7) (the atomic sidecar retirement) remain **operator-lane and unshipped** — (7) touches `scripts/gasp_shim.sh` and `tools/gasp-emit/`, both on the harness's protected list, so yoyo cannot land it and an attempt reverts the whole task. What this removes is the last blocker inside yoyo's own lane: the door can now be handed the shim's argv without losing a property the shim depends on.
- `git_commit_msg.rs` — the deterministic, token-free commit-message generator behind `/commit` (`generate_commit_message`, re-exported by `git.rs` so every call site still says `git::generate_commit_message`). Split out of `src/git.rs` on Day 174 as a **pure move** — the parent was at 1997 lines against the fatal 2000-line gate and the #811 fix plus its tests is ~260 lines; no body was rewritten by the move itself. Day 174 (#811) fixed three compounding defects that produced the real commit `c46d8453` — a 283-line production fix (`src/prompt.rs` +171, `src/repl.rs` +110) committed as `test(6 files): update code`, i.e. a message asserting the opposite of what the diff did. **Kind: product** — every yoyo user's `/commit` history carries this string, so the fix is language-agnostic, not "Rust files under `src/`". (1) The test rule was `path.contains("test")` with `any()`, so one fixture out of six re-typed the whole commit and any path merely carrying the letters (`src/commands_latest.rs`, `contest.rs`, `src/protest_handler.rs`) counted. It is now the pure `path_is_test(path)`, matching by **path shape**: a path component equal to `tests`/`test`/`spec`/`__tests__`, a file stem ending `_test`/`_tests`, or a filename containing `.test.`/`.spec.` — covering Rust, Go, Python, Ruby and JS/TS conventions in one predicate. The two cases that decide it are pinned by test in both directions: `src/commands_latest.rs` must **not** match, `src/main_tests.rs` must. (2) The prefix ladder was a first-match `if/else if` chain, so the type was decided by the *presence* of one file rather than by where the change is. `commit_file_category(path) -> CommitFileCategory` (`Test`/`Docs`/`Chore`/`Code`) plus `dominant_commit_category(&weights)` now pick the **largest total**, weighted by per-file changed lines parsed out of the diff the function was already walking — deliberately **no new git invocation**; a file with no counted lines (rename, mode change) still weighs 1. **Ties go to production code**: a mixed commit is neither a test commit nor a chore. Among the other three, ties fall back to the legacy ladder order (test, docs, chore) so nothing that used to be decided one way flips for free, and an empty diff defaults to `Code` rather than inventing a type. The prefix vocabulary is unchanged (`test`/`docs`/`chore`/`refactor`/`feat`) — this changed *how the winner is chosen*, not the set of types, and `refactor` vs `feat` still turns on the same `deletions > insertions * 2` test, now only once `Code` has won. (3) The summary said the least exactly when the commit was biggest: over 3 files the scope collapses to `"N files"` and any mixed diff read `update code`. Once the scope can no longer name the files, the summary spends itself on a fact the diff already knows — `{verb} {stem of the file with the most changed lines} (+N more)` — and deliberately **invents no description of intent**, which this function cannot know. Under 4 files the strings are byte-identical to before (`add changes` / `remove code` / `update code`), and the scope half is untouched at every size. Tested at the **emission point** (the string a caller receives, never a helper one layer below): the #811 file list now yields verbatim `feat(6 files): update prompt (+5 more)`; a lone `src/commands_latest.rs` does not type as `test`; a lone `tests/module_size.rs` still does (the near-miss guard, so the fix cannot regress the case the old rule got right). All four pre-existing tests were kept — `test_generate_commit_message_multiple_files` asserted only `msg.contains("4 files")`, which pinned the degradation rather than questioning it, so its scope assertion stands unchanged and two assertions were **added** beside a comment saying what it used to pin and why. **The AI-generated message path is untouched** — this is the deterministic fallback used when that path returns nothing usable (`commands_git.rs:1663`) and directly at `repl.rs:831` and `commands_git.rs:1380/1408`; nothing downstream sanity-checks its output, which is why the defect reached history silently. Day 178 (self-discovered) fixed a fourth defect on the same function, and it is the one the #811 work walked past: **every whole-file deletion was dropped from `files_changed` entirely.** git renders a delete as `--- a/<path>` / `+++ /dev/null`, and the walker only recorded a path on `+++ b/`, so the `+++ /dev/null` branch set `current = None` and recorded nothing — the deleted path appears *only* on the `---` line, which nothing read. All four downstream decisions were then computed over a list missing that file: the scope (a lone deletion gave `files_changed.len() == 0`, so `join` on an empty vec rendered the literal `()`), the category weights (a commit deleting only test files could not type as `test`), the `> 3 files` threshold (undercounted, so a 5-file deletion took the small-commit path), and the large-commit summary focus (picked from `files_changed`, so it could never name a deleted file). The visible symptom is the real commit **`26defce9`** in this repo's own history: `refactor(): remove code`, empty parens, for a diff deleting `mutants.toml`.
