The fix holds the `--- a/<path>` value in a `pending_old_path` and, when the matching `+++` turns out to be `/dev/null`, pushes *that* path as the hunk's file — so the deletion's `-` lines (which follow the `+++` line) land on the right index and the file carries real weight rather than the fallback 1. **An added file renders `--- /dev/null`, which does not match the `--- a/` prefix, so `pending_old_path` stays `None` and the add path is byte-identical** — that, plus an ordinary modification, is pinned by two near-miss guards asserting the exact pre-fix strings (`feat(new): add changes`, `feat(main): update code`). This changed *which files are in the list* and nothing about how the list is read: the prefix vocabulary, the production-code tie-break, the `deletions > insertions * 2` refactor/feat split and every scope/summary string are untouched. Verified against the real thing — `26defce9`'s actual diff now yields `refactor(mutants): remove code`, same prefix as before (the prefix was always right; only the scope was destroyed). Positive control run rather than assumed: reverting the parser change fails exactly the four deletion tests and leaves both near-miss guards green. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 179, #826) — and this is the *second* superseded claim inside this one bullet, which is the pattern rather than the incident.** The paragraph that stood here read: *"**Superseded reading, recorded rather than erased — and the lesson is about the examiner, not the number.** Commit `80c61784` (Day 178, 19:29) added 195 lines of tests to this file under the subject "kill the 16 recorded survivors … and re-measure", i.e. it reports this function as fully defended after mutation repair. That reading is **a true reading of the mutant population and was never evidence about deletion diffs** … The timing is the proof rather than a coincidence — that repair landed at 19:29 and `26defce9` was authored at **20:03, 34 minutes later**, by this function, with the perfect score already in hand."* **There is no such reading.** `grep -o 'Reading #[0-9]' CLAUDE.md` returns #1–#7 and stops; `dreams/experiments.jsonl` carries exactly two `git_commit_msg.rs` entries, round 73 (the *pre*-repair 32.0%) and round 81's fixture census — no post-repair number anywhere. What `80c61784` actually produced, per #826's evaluator receipt: an **aborted** `cargo mutants` run — `end_time: null`, **~25 of ~41** mutants outcomed, `lock.json` still held — and `mutants.out/` is gitignored, so even that partial evidence is **unrecoverable**. So a later session read the commit's *subject line* ("…and re-measure") as its *result*, and built two confident sentences on a score that was never computed. That is round 75's own documented trap — *"a partial mutation run reports a real-looking number"* — landing in the file that is re-injected as authoritative context every session, and it is one degree worse than the failure mode this week has been spent on: not *"could not check" reading as "checked; clean"*, but **"could not finish" reading as "0 survivors"**. **Two things stand unchanged.** (1) The 195 lines of tests **did** land and are genuine work — nine emission-point assertions against the survivors reading #1 recorded; this corrects a *number*, it does not retract a repair. (2) **The lesson survives intact, because its evidence was never the score**: *a mutation score is bounded by what the fixtures can ask* — cargo-mutants mutates operators and function bodies inside the code the suite executes, so a branch every fixture enters with `current = None` already set has nothing to distinguish, and killing every generated mutant says nothing about an input the suite never constructs. That claim rests on **blind round 81's fixture-shape census below**, which found three real defects from diff shapes no fixture had ever built, and on the still-true measurement that the string `dev/null` occurred **exactly once in the entire 623-line file** at that commit — at line 127, a *comment* inside the production branch that drops the path — and **zero times** anywhere in the test module (which starts at line 215). The branch acknowledged the shape in prose while not one fixture ever fed it, and `26defce9` was authored by this function at **20:03**, 34 minutes after that repair landed. Treat "0 survivors" as *no defect this population can phrase*, never as *no defect*; before reading any such score here, check which diff **shapes** the fixtures actually contain — **and check that the score exists.** The post-repair reading for this module is **owed and stays owed**: it is the single remaining item on **#826**, which stays open, and it needs its own task because a `cargo mutants` run here is a ~25-minute subprocess that does not fit beside other work (reading #1 took 22m54s over 52 mutants on a file that has since grown ~50%). **Day 178 (blind round 81) answered that question by taking the census, and the answer was three defects of one class.** Four diff shapes had never been constructed by any fixture — a **pure rename**, a **binary** change, a **mode-only** change, and a **rename with a content change** — every one captured as verbatim `git show` output from a scratch repo rather than hand-typed. Three of the four produced the *same* symptom, `feat(): update code`, the literal empty parens: git emits **no** `---`/`+++` lines at all for a content-identical rename (`similarity index 100%` + `rename from`/`rename to`), for a binary change (`index …` + `Binary files a/x and b/x differ`), or for a mode-only change (`old mode`/`new mode`), so the `diff --git a/X b/Y` header is their **only** mention of the file and nothing read it. That is one cause, not three, and it is the same class as `26defce9` arriving from three further input shapes. The fourth was already correct and is now a regression guard: a rename *with* content **is** rendered with `--- a/old` / `+++ b/new`, so the walker records the **new** path off the `+++` side (`feat(renamed2): add changes`). The fix is the pure, table-tested `diff_header_path(line)` plus a `pending_header_path` held from the header and flushed at the next header and at end of diff — pushed with **zero counted lines**, so it takes the same weight-1 fallback a pure rename already took once recorded. **The `+++ b/` and `+++ /dev/null` branches both clear it**, which is what stops an ordinary hunk being counted twice; verified against a real 4-file commit (`1134caad` → `feat(4 files): update config (+3 more)`, with `git show --name-only` agreeing on 4) and against `26defce9`, which still yields `refactor(mutants): remove code`. Positive control run rather than assumed: neutering `diff_header_path` to always return `None` fails exactly the three census tests plus the header table, and restoring it returns green. **Two findings were deliberately left unfixed and filed rather than absorbed** — one defect per round, and a finding recorded only here is not scheduled, because CLAUDE.md is a reader surface and the issue queue is the scheduler surface: git **quotes** both header paths when they contain special characters (`diff --git "a/n\303\244me\"q.txt" "b/…"`), which `strip_prefix("diff --git a/")` does not match, so such a file still vanishes (**#829**, verified empirically; a path with a plain *space* is unquoted by git and **is** handled, since the separator still occurs exactly once); and a path containing a literal `" b/"` makes the header genuinely ambiguous, where `diff_header_path` **refuses rather than guesses** — degrading to the pre-round-81 drop instead of inventing a path that is not in the diff (**#830**). **The general rule, which is the round's real deliverable: before reading a mutation score for a function, check which input *shapes* its fixtures build — the score is bounded by them.** This is a second, independent ceiling on the same number, sitting beside the tool-side one reading #5 measured (cargo-mutants has exactly two genres, so 93 clamp sites across `src/` are unaskable): that ceiling is set by what the instrument can *generate*, this one by what the fixtures can *ask*, and a function can sit at 0% survival while an entire branch has never been entered. **Round 81's own grades: 1 hit / 1 miss.** The hit (h1, the pure rename) was derivable from a mechanism I could state before reading anything — the walker keys on `---`/`+++` prefixes, a shape emitting neither cannot be recorded. The miss (h2) asserted that git emits `--- a/x` / `+++ b/x` before `Binary files … differ`; it does not, and one `git show` would have settled it — a claim about an **external tool's output format** that I did not check, i.e. a coin flip wearing a prior's clothes, which is the losing half of the derivable-vs-choice-dependent split running since round 73. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #829) — and the sentence it corrects is two paragraphs up, inside the round-81 record, so read them together.** That paragraph ends "…which `strip_prefix("diff --git a/")` does not match, so such a file still vanishes (**#829**…)". True when written, false since this task: **git-quoted paths now decode**, so a non-ASCII rename renders a real scope instead of `feat(): update code`. The half worth keeping from the superseded sentence is its parenthetical, which is still exactly true and is now pinned as a near-miss guard rather than an aside — a path with a plain **space** is *not* quoted by git (`diff --git a/we ird.txt b/we ird.txt`), so it takes the unquoted branch and is byte-identical to before. **What changed, and it is two sites rather than the one #829 named:** the quoted branch is checked **before** the unquoted one in `diff_header_path` (git quotes *both* paths or neither, and the closing quote of the first token *is* the separator, so the quoted form has none of the ` b/` ambiguity the unquoted one has), and the same decoding was needed on the hunk lines — `quoted_hunk_path(line, marker, side)` reads `--- "a/<path>"` / `+++ "b/<path>"`, without which a *modified* or *deleted* non-ASCII file was still dropped even with the header fixed; both `+++` and `---` arms call it as an `.or_else` fallback after the plain-prefix read, so every unquoted shape stays on its original code path. `split_first_quoted` finds the token boundary by walking `char_indices` and honouring `\\` escapes, so a `\"` inside the name cannot end the token early and **no index is ever taken on a byte offset that could fall inside a char** (#250). **The escape set covered, named rather than implied:** three-digit octal byte escapes (`\303\244`, decoded into a `Vec<u8>` and interpreted as UTF-8 exactly once, at the end — they are *bytes*, not chars), plus `\"`, `\\`, `\t`, `\n`, `\r`. **Everything else refuses — returns `None`, so the file is dropped exactly as it was before, never guessed at:** a byte sequence that is not valid UTF-8, an escape git is not known to emit, an octal run shorter than three digits, a lone trailing backslash, an unterminated quoted token, and a header whose second token carries trailing junk. Inventing a path that is not in the diff is the failure mode round 81's refusal branch already chose against, and this branch makes the same choice for the same reason. **#830 remains open and unfixed, and that is the deliberate half:** a path containing a literal `" b/"` still makes an *unquoted* header genuinely ambiguous, and `diff_header_path` still refuses rather than guessing — closing it needs a design decision about which anchor to trust, so it was filed rather than smuggled in here, and its refusal row is pinned by test so this task could not weaken it while adding the quoted branch. Fixtures were captured verbatim from real `git show` output in a scratch repo rather than hand-typed (round 81 lost a bet to exactly that shortcut): a 100%-similarity rename `diff --git "a/n\303\244me.txt" "b/n\303\240me2.txt"` → `feat(nàme2): update code`, an escaped quote in the filename, a modified and a deleted quoted path (both via the hunk lines), a quoted binary change, and git's trailing-tab separator on `+++` lines. Near-miss guards pin that the spaced unquoted path, an ordinary modification, an added file and a whole-file deletion are all byte-identical to before — a discriminator tested only where it fires is vacuous green. Positive control run rather than assumed: neutering the unquoting branch fails exactly the new quoted tests and leaves every near-miss guard green.
- `prompt.rs` — prompt execution, agent interaction, streaming event handling, auto-retry logic. A fatal `StopReason::Error` (yoagent 0.14.1+: dropped tool-call arguments, or `pause_turn` with no `error_message`) is recorded in `PromptEventState::fatal_error` and surfaces as `PromptResult::FatalError` → `PromptOutcome.last_api_error` — a caller-visible error that is **never auto-retried** (surface-and-stop, #646). `into_result` checks `fatal_error` *before* `retriable_error` so a fatal message that incidentally contains a retriable keyword still stops; overflow and known-benign "stream ended" keep their existing paths. File-change tracking lives in two places in `PromptEventState`, and the split is forced by the data: `write_file`/`edit_file` are recorded at **ToolExecutionStart** off their single `path` argument, while `rename_symbol` is recorded at **ToolExecutionEnd** by `record_rename_tool_writes`, reading the `files_written` details key and only when `is_error` is false (#783 — a multi-file rename's written set does not exist until the call returns). Day 174 (#808): `PromptOutcome` carries `text_since_last_tool` beside `text` — a parallel accumulator in `PromptEventState`, pushed at the same two sites as `collected_text` and **cleared at every ToolExecutionEnd** (errored tools clear too: the field answers "did the model say anything after the last thing it did"), mirrored in `handle_stream_json_events` with the same clearing rule so the two event paths cannot drift; the auto-retry wrappers carry the **last** attempt's value (overwrite, not append), and `should_auto_continue`'s silence branch reads this field while `text` keeps its whole-turn meaning for its four other consumers. Day 170 (#786): both halves are **free functions with two call sites each**, one per event path, so the display path and `--output-format json` cannot drift — the argument-based half is `record_tool_arg_writes(tool_name, args, changes)` (the `write_file` → `ChangeKind::Write` / `edit_file` → `ChangeKind::Edit` match, extracted from the inline match that used to live in `handle_tool_execution_start`), called from `handle_tool_execution_start` and from `handle_stream_json_events`'s `ToolExecutionStart` arm; the end-based half is `record_rename_tool_writes`, called from `handle_tool_execution_end` and from `handle_stream_json_events`'s `ToolExecutionEnd` arm, gated on `!is_error` at both. `handle_stream_json_events` and its two public callers `run_prompt_stream_json` / `run_prompt_stream_json_with_content` therefore take a `changes: &SessionChanges`, threaded from the **caller's own** tracker in `main.rs` (`run_single_prompt`, `run_piped_mode` — the single `SessionChanges::new()` binding each already had, moved above the stream-json early return; a throwaway tracker there would be #678's defect again). Day 172 (#794 half (a)): both `ToolExecutionStart` sites also call `changes.record_tool_call()` immediately beside `record_tool_arg_writes` — **both or neither**, the sweep #786 had to come back and repair. It is counted at **Start**, not End, deliberately: a tool that errors or never returns still means the turn did something. That counter is what the auto-continue gate reads (see the `session.rs` bullet); pinned at the emission point by `display_path_counts_a_read_only_tool_call` and `stream_json_counts_a_read_only_tool_call`, which drive a `read_file` start through each path and assert `tool_call_count() == 1` while `edit_count() == 0` — the whole defect in one assertion. Day 177: `apply_effort_hint` was split into a one-line wrapper that reads the process-global `cli_config::effort_level()` and the pure `apply_effort_hint_with(level, input)` holding the whole decision — production behaviour is **byte-identical**, the wrapper is the only global read, and the point is the tests: the three `test_apply_effort_hint_*` tests each wrote `EFFORT_LEVEL` (an `AtomicU8`) and raced each other on libtest's parallel threads, so `cargo test --bin yoyo apply_effort_hint` failed **6 of 12** runs while `--test-threads=1` failed 0 of 6 — a flaky red, which in `scripts/evolve.sh` means `git reset --hard` on whatever correct work was sitting beside it (CI run `32748888895` on `f3719406`). They now pass an explicit `EffortLevel` and touch no global, and **no `#[serial]` was added** — it only serialises against other `#[serial]` tests and would leave the class alive for the next test to trip on, plus a false record of which tests are dangerous (the #780 reason). The wrapper's global read is pinned by `test_apply_effort_hint_wrapper_reads_the_global_level`, a deliberately **weak** source-level check (needle built at runtime so it cannot match itself) — it proves the call is present, not that its result is used — verified against a broken wrapper before landing rather than only in the passing direction. **Deliberately not unified:** `run_prompt_with_content_and_changes` re-reads the global and builds its own `[Effort: {hint}]` as a *separate* `Content::Text` block, which is provably **not** the same string as the wrapper's `[Effort: {hint}]\n\n{input}`, so routing it through the new helper would change what reaches the model; the duplication stands and the second path stays untested.
- `repl.rs` — interactive REPL loop, tab-completion, multi-line input, `!` shell passthrough (`parse_bang_command` — run a shell command directly with zero tokens, output streamed live; output is also teed into an in-memory "last bang result" — `BangResult`, `tail_for_capture`, char-boundary-safe ~8KB/200-line tail — and `!?` (`parse_bang_query`, checked before the generic `!` parse) feeds it into the conversation via `build_bang_followup_prompt`, with a dim hint printed after non-zero exits), auto-continue for incomplete responses (`should_auto_continue` = yoagent 0.9 `follow_up_queue_len()` as an authoritative pending-work signal, falling back to the `looks_incomplete` text heuristic when the queue is empty; product-safe — no-op for providers that don't populate the queue, up to 5 follow-ups per user turn; when auto-continue is about to fire it also surfaces a dim one-line hint of the next queued follow-up via `follow_up_queue_snapshot()` (`format_followup_hint` — char-boundary-safe truncation, silent when the queue is empty/unsupported); Day 150 added an **opt-in** fourth input, `should_auto_continue(text, queue_pending, ran_tools, continue_on_silence)` (the parameter was named `used_tools` until Day 172) — with `--continue-on-silence` (default OFF, `cli::set_continue_on_silence`/`is_continue_on_silence`, read at the call site so the helper stays pure) a turn that **used tools**, ended **without an error**, has an **empty queue**, and produced **under `MIN_SUMMARY_CHARS` (20)** trimmed chars of final text also auto-continues — the issue #631 abstention case made an explicit third value instead of being absorbed into "finished"; a no-tools silent turn never loops, and off-mode is pinned byte-identical to the old two-argument logic by `should_auto_continue_default_off_is_byte_identical_to_legacy`. Off by default because yoyo cannot distinguish "stopped mid-work" from "finished quietly"; the `get_max_auto_continues` budget bounds the worst case at 5 wasted turns per prompt, not an infinite loop. Day 174 (#808): the silence branch judges a **fifth input**, `text_since_last_tool` — text emitted *after* the turn's last tool result — instead of the whole-turn `text`, which still feeds `looks_incomplete` unchanged. The whole-turn measure made the branch structurally unable to fire: agents narrate before every tool batch ("Let me trace the mechanism."), so `text` was always over `MIN_SUMMARY_CHARS` even when the model said nothing after its last tool — measured 0 firings over 11 opted-in sessions against 16 A1/A2 abstentions, all with that shape. Pinned by `announced_intent_then_closing_silence_fires_the_gate_808`, whose precondition asserts the tail is NOT caught by `looks_incomplete`'s phrase list (deliberately unbroadened: a generic "let me " pattern would false-positive on "Let me know if…" for non-opted-in product users). Day 171 (#794 half (b)): `should_auto_continue` and `get_max_auto_continues` (both already `pub(crate)`) have a **second caller** — `main.rs`'s `run_piped_mode`, which had no auto-continue of any kind, so `is_continue_on_silence()` was read at exactly one site that the evolve loop's piped agents never reach. Only the **decision** is shared: the REPL's loop body was deliberately *not* hoisted, because it is entangled with `turn_count`, `handle_post_prompt`, `TurnSnapshot`, `spawn_tracker` and `repl_file_config`, none of which exist in piped mode; the piped side duplicates ~20 lines of sequencing while calling this predicate and this bound, and both call sites carry a comment saying that is the intended trade. Nothing in this file changed and the REPL's behaviour is byte-identical — in particular the piped loop is gated *as a whole* on `is_continue_on_silence()` (default OFF; since Day 172 that reads the `--continue-on-silence` flag **or** the `continue_on_silence` config key) rather than reaching REPL parity, so no existing `yoyo -p` user gets an extra turn; see the `main.rs` bullet for why (#448)), a turn-end marker after the auto-continue loop (`classify_turn_end`/`format_turn_end` — pure helpers returning `TurnEnd::{Done,Paused,BudgetSpent}` and one dim stderr line stating *why yoyo believes the turn stopped*; informational only, never changes auto-continue behavior, gated on the turn having changed ≥1 file, silent under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`; under `cli::is_verbose()` an observation-shaped line is printed *above* the verdict — `format_turn_end_debug`, the raw classifier inputs plus the verdict they produced, pure ASCII so one string serves both output modes, and printed on every ended turn including the ones the verdict declines to speak about), contextual command hints after prompt turns
- `watch.rs` — watch mode: set/get/clear watch command(s), run watch command with streaming output, multi-phase watch (lint → fix → test → fix), auto-fix loop after prompts with command-type-aware fix prompts and structured Rust compiler error parsing (`CompilerError`, `parse_rust_errors`, category-specific hints) (extracted from `prompt.rs`), `/watch` command handler and project-type detection for auto-watch; the post-prompt watch cycle is skipped entirely when the prompt changed zero files (`should_run_watch_after_prompt` gate). Day 175 (#818, found by blind round 71): that gate takes a **per-turn baseline** — `should_run_watch_after_prompt(changes, edits_before)` is `changes.edit_count() > edits_before`, and `run_watch_after_prompt` threads an `edits_before: usize` from each caller. It used to be `!changes.snapshot().is_empty()`, i.e. the **session-wide** deduped file list, which `SessionChanges::clear()` only empties on `/clear` — so once anything had been edited in the session it returned `true` forever and the predicate could not answer the per-turn question its own doc comment claims ("no write/edit tool calls **this turn**"). **The unit that matters here is call sites, not the function**, and that is why the defect survived: three of the four callers were safe for three *different* reasons — `repl.rs` gates the call on its own outer per-turn delta (`edit_count() > ctx.edits_before`, from #774), and both `main.rs` sites are per-process, where session-wide *is* per-turn (they pass a literal `0`, so their behaviour is byte-identical). The fourth, `conversations.rs`'s `/extended`, had **no** outer guard, so the broken predicate was the only gate: after any earlier edit in the session, a zero-change `/extended` turn ran the full lint→fix→test cycle and, on failure, `build_watch_fix_prompt` told the agent *"Your changes caused failures"* about a pre-existing failure the turn did not cause — precisely the misattribution the gate exists to prevent, costing a wasted watch run plus up to `MAX_WATCH_FIX_ATTEMPTS = 3` API fix turns aimed at a lie. That call site samples its baseline **before** the prompt runs (a baseline read afterwards would already contain the turn's own writes — the Day-165 shape, where a guard that reads the world after its own action is blinded by that action). The **counter**, not the vec length, is the right input: a turn that re-edits an already-touched file grows `edit_count()` while leaving `snapshot().len()` unchanged. **Why no test caught it:** both pre-existing gate tests drive a *fresh* `SessionChanges`, where session-wide and per-turn state are identical, so neither could distinguish the two readings — vacuous green in exactly the direction that mattered. Both are kept verbatim (baseline `0` preserves their exact meaning) and a third drives a **non-fresh** tracker, asserting the skip in the direction the old code got wrong *and* the near-miss that must still pass through (re-editing the same file is work this turn), with an explicit precondition that the deduped set did not grow. Verified to fail against the old body before landing, so it is a real regression guard rather than a fixture agreeing with the fix.
- `prompt_budget.rs` — session wall-clock budget + audit log helpers (extracted from `prompt.rs`)
- `prompt_retry.rs` — error diagnosis and retry logic: retry prompt construction, exponential backoff, error classification, API error diagnosis (extracted from `prompt.rs`). Day 174 (blind round 65, the darkest non-risk room at 145 snapshots unobserved — **all three registered hypotheses lost** (0 hit / 1 partial / 2 miss); this was an **unpredicted find while grading h1**, and the ledger says so): `diagnose_api_error` decided four of its five branches with **bare substring matches on three-digit HTTP status codes** (`lower.contains("401")`, `"402"`, `"429"`, `"403"`) — the most collidable shape there is inside an error string that routinely carries other numbers. A context-overflow error reading `prompt is too long: 402134 tokens > 200000 maximum` contains `402`, so it was diagnosed as **exhausted credits**, telling the user "This is not a transient error — retrying won't help" when the real remedy is `/compact`. A confident *wrong* diagnosis in error-recovery code is worse than none, because the user acts on it. All four sites now go through the pure `contains_status_code(haystack, code)`, which requires a **digit** boundary on both sides — deliberately **not** a word boundary, so `HTTP 401`, `status=401`, `(401)` and `401:` all still match while `402134`, `4290ms` and `14012` no longer do; it never slices on a byte index that could fall inside a char (matches start on ASCII digits by construction, and the boundary checks read bytes rather than slicing). Pinned at the **emission point** — the string `diagnose_api_error` returns, not the helper one layer below: a token-count error must not read as billing *or* auth, a request-id's digits must not read as a 401, and the **near-miss guard** that a real standalone `401` still reaches the auth branch (a discriminator tested only on the side that blocks is vacuous green). **Deliberately left alone:** `tool_recovery_hint`'s hand-written tool-name table has drifted behind `agent_builder::BUILTIN_TOOL_NAMES` — there is no arm for `shared_state`, `todo` or `ask_user`, so they fall to the `_` catch-all (round 65's h2, graded *partial*: `sub_agent`, the other instance named, does have arms in both branches). Not fixed, and not for lack of clock — the catch-all returns an honest generic hint, so the fallback is degraded-but-true rather than wrong, and adding arms would be cargo-culting coverage where the absence costs nothing observable. Also confirmed **already correct** and worth not re-litigating: the provider named in an auth diagnosis comes from `cli::configured_provider()` → `cli::provider_api_key_env` (#590), and both retry-prompt builders cap the interpolated error via `safe_truncate` (200 / 300 chars).
- `prompt_retry_limits.rs` — rate-limit aware retry (Day 177): the pure trio `retry_after_hint` / `retry_wait_decision` / `rate_limit_giveup_message`, split out of `prompt_retry.rs` in the same task because adding them pushed that file 156 lines past the fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` (registering a brand-new 156-line overshoot would have laundered exactly the design event branch 1 exists to catch). **The measured defect:** `retry_delay` caps at `CAP_SECS = 60` plus ±50% jitter, so ~90s is the longest wait it can ever produce, while `is_retriable_error` classifies a rate limit as retriable and *nothing anywhere parsed a reset time* — so a provider answering `error: Rate limited, retry after Some(14454000)ms` (~4h) was met with five ≤90s sleeps against the same refusal, then a final error that never said when the door reopens. Not hypothetical: **2 of the 4 gradeable post-#808 sessions died this way**, which Day 175 handled by excluding them from a meter (`scripts/measure_abstentions.py`'s `EXCLUDED_PROVIDER_ERROR` bucket) — that treated a capacity loss as noise. `retry_after_hint` reads only the shapes that occur (`retry after Some(N)ms`, `retry after Nms`, `retry_after: N ms`, and the HTTP header `retry-after: N` whose unit is **seconds**), requires an explicit `ms` suffix for the millisecond forms, and returns **`None` for everything else** — including a marker followed by a unit it refuses to guess about (`retry after 5 minutes`), a `u64` overflow, and any number with no marker in front of it, so a token count or request id can never be mistaken for a reset (the Day-174 `contains_status_code` lesson: the whole digit run is consumed, so `144` cannot be read out of `14454000`). `retry_wait_decision(attempt, error_msg, budget_remaining)` returns `Wait` or `GiveUp`, and rule 1 is the load-bearing one: **no hint → `Wait(retry_delay(attempt))`, byte-identical to the pre-existing behaviour**, which is every user whose provider does not name a reset and was the regression risk. A hint longer than the session can wait gives up; a shorter one is honoured (the provider's own number beats an exponential guess), floored at the shared `prompt_retry::MIN_RETRY_DELAY_MS` so `retry after 1ms` cannot spin. `MAX_INLINE_RETRY_AFTER = 120s` is a **judgment threshold, not a measurement** — nothing measured says 120s is right; it is the point past which sleeping is worse than reporting. `budget_remaining` can only make that ceiling *smaller*, never larger (a deliberate deviation from a naive "prefer the budget": a session with three hours left still should not sleep an hour inside one retry). **Both** retry loops consult it — `prompt.rs`'s text path and its image/content path, which were the only two `retry_delay` callers and are now zero, so this is not the "two doors, one works" shape (#745, #767, #769, #816). Pinned at the emission point in both directions: the verbatim observed string, the near-miss guards that must return `None`, the no-hint `Wait` range that *is* the byte-identity assertion, and the `GiveUp` message asserted as the literal string a caller receives (it names the reset in human units via the existing `format::format_duration`, says the stop was **deliberate**, and says a retry will not fix it). Verified by positive control — mutating the `ms` unit and the give-up comparison each kill tests. **What it deliberately does not do, stated plainly: it does not reschedule or resume the session after the limit resets.** That lives in `scripts/evolve.sh`, which is protected and which yoyo may not edit; this only stops wasting the remaining attempts and reports when the door reopens. **Day 178 — the opt-in `--wait-for-reset`, and a superseded claim recorded rather than erased.** The sentence above used to end there, and its stated reason (the resume lives in a protected file) was doing double duty as a *product* design decision it does not license: that reason is true about my evolve loop and irrelevant to a user sitting at a terminal, where there is no `evolve.sh` and the process that must wait is the one already running. It is still true that **nothing here reschedules or resumes a session** — this lets an already-running process *wait*, it restarts nothing. What changed is that a user who would happily leave the terminal open for four hours now has a way to say so. `retry_wait_decision` keeps its exact 3-argument signature (so `prompt.rs`'s two retry loops needed no edit) and becomes a one-line wrapper reading `cli::is_wait_for_reset()` over the pure, table-tested `retry_wait_decision_with(attempt, error_msg, budget_remaining, wait_for_reset)` — the `apply_effort_hint`/`apply_effort_hint_with` split, so a test never writes a process global. **`wait_for_reset == false` is byte-identical to the Day-177 rule on every input**, which is every existing user and was the regression risk, and it is pinned by an explicit equality test that drives a 9-message × 6-budget × 3-attempt table through *both* the new function and a verbatim transcription of the old body (a second statement of the old behaviour, so the new code cannot pass by agreeing with itself). Opted in, the ceiling becomes `min(MAX_RESET_WAIT, budget_remaining)` — the budget still only ever *shrinks* a ceiling, the `MIN_RETRY_DELAY_MS` floor is unchanged, and the no-hint path is untouched. `MAX_RESET_WAIT = 6h` is a **judgment threshold, not a measurement** — nothing measured says 6h is right; it is the point past which a "wait" is really "come back tomorrow", and it exists so that opting in cannot mean an *unbounded* sleep (a 24h reset still gives up, pinned by test). `reset_wait_ceiling` is a separate `fn` beside `inline_retry_ceiling` rather than one parameterised clamp, deliberately: cargo-mutants cannot substitute one method call for another, so a clamp only enters the mutant population when it owns a function boundary (blind round 80). **An invisible multi-hour sleep is a bug even when it is the right sleep** (the #794 `⚡ auto-continuing` rule), so the pure `rate_limit_wait_notice(delay, plain)` returns `None` at or below `MAX_INLINE_RETRY_AFTER` — short waits stay silent, so nothing new prints on the common path — and above it names the wait in human units, says it was **deliberate and opted into**, and names the flag that turns it off; glyph-free under `is_plain_output()` (bullets *and* em dashes), silent under `is_quiet()`, emitted from the impure wrapper so `prompt.rs` stays untouched. Pinned at the emission point in **both** directions, including the inclusive boundary (`120s` silent, `121s` speaks) — a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. Verified by positive control, twice: flipping the flag argument in one assertion row kills a test, and neutering `MAX_RESET_WAIT` to equal `MAX_INLINE_RETRY_AFTER` kills two — a new parameter that changes nothing under test is the vacuous-green shape. **The default is OFF and that is a deliberate divergence from a rival, not an oversight:** Claude Code v2.1.234 (~Aug 20) ships session-continuation-on-reset **default-ON with an opt-out in `/config`**; a process that can silently sleep for hours is not a product-safe default (#448), so yoyo ships the same capability opt-in. The flag is in `KNOWN_FLAGS`, takes no value (so deliberately *not* in the value-taking list `check_flag_values` scans), has one `set_wait_for_reset()` call site in `parse_args`, and is documented in `cli_help_text()` — an undocumented flag is the discoverability defect already fixed three times (#745, #767, #769). It is **deliberately not part of the project-config trust boundary** (`gate_mcp_sources` / `gate_project_permissions` / `gate_project_hooks`) and a comment at the call site says so, because it grants no privilege — the worst case is a process that sleeps — the same reasoning already written down for `continue_on_silence`. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, second task):** this bullet used to end *"**Deliberately not done, named rather than implied: there is no config key.** `wait_for_reset = true` in `.yoyo.toml` (the `config.rs` + `SETTABLE_KEYS` + `validate_config_value` trio) is real follow-up work; the flag is the product door and the config key is separate."* That was true for exactly one day. The config key **shipped**, in the same three-part shape `continue_on_silence` established on Day 172, and the reason it had to is a product one: the whole point of the flag is a person willing to leave the terminal open for four hours, and that person wants to say so **once** rather than retype a flag on every invocation — a capability reachable only by retyping a flag is the shape that trains people to alias it away. `config::parse_wait_for_reset_from_config` joins the `config_flag`-shaped boolean readers (`auto_watch`, `quiet`, `no_bell`, `continue_on_silence`), **default `false`, opt-in**; `"wait_for_reset"` was added to `SETTABLE_KEYS` with its matching `validate_config_value` arm **in the same diff**, so `/config set wait_for_reset true` validates and `/config get wait_for_reset` reads it back — a key nothing can set through the normal door is half a feature. `set_wait_for_reset()` still has **exactly one** call site in `parse_args`, which now has **two sources OR'd together**: the `--wait-for-reset` flag, **or** the config key. There is deliberately **no disable flag** — an OR is the whole rule and a fourth precedence value was not needed. **The shipped default is unchanged: off unless the flag is passed or the key is written**, which is every existing user's path and was the regression risk here; pinned by the three-state reader test (absent → `false`, `= true` → `true`, `= false` → `false`, the last being the near-miss guard so the discriminator is not covered only on the side that fires) and by both positive controls (flipping the reader's default kills a test; renaming the `validate_config_value` arm kills another). The key is **deliberately not** part of the project-config trust boundary (`gate_mcp_sources` / `gate_project_permissions` / `gate_project_hooks`) and a comment at the call site says so: it grants no privilege — a repo's `.yoyo.toml` setting this can cost you wall-clock, never access — unlike an MCP command, a `permissions.allow` entry or a shell hook, whose entire content is executable code. **One thing deliberately left undone and named rather than implied:** `cli_help_text()`'s `--wait-for-reset` line was **not** extended with "(or `wait_for_reset = true` in your config)", because that text lives in `src/help.rs` — a fourth file, past this task's stated three-file scope. The config door is not undocumented, though: its `SETTABLE_KEYS` description ships in the same diff and is what `/config set` lists, so each door is documented where its own audience looks; the cross-reference between them is the missing half.
- `prompt_utils.rs` — message search, highlighting, summarization, output file writing, tool result preview, fenced code block extraction (`extract_code_blocks` — line-based, multi-byte-safe, triple-backtick only) (extracted from `prompt.rs`)
- `session.rs` — session tracking types: SessionChanges, TurnSnapshot, TurnHistory, format_changes (extracted from `prompt.rs`). Day 169 (#774): `SessionChanges` carries a **monotonic edit counter** (`edits: Arc<AtomicUsize>`, read via `edit_count()`, bumped unconditionally by `record` on *both* the dedup and push branches, zeroed by `clear()`) alongside the path-deduped vec, which keeps its exact meaning — `snapshot().len()` still counts **distinct files** and both dedup tests (`test_session_changes_deduplicates_same_path`, `test_to_json_summary_deduplicates_paths`) are untouched. Three **per-turn** checks in `repl.rs` now read the counter instead of that length: the per-turn change summary (`files_modified`), the tool-usage argument to `should_auto_continue` (the `--continue-on-silence` gate — since Day 172 that one reads `tool_call_count()` instead, see below), and `did_work` (the turn-end marker). Why: the deduped length answers a *session-wide* question ("did the session grow its distinct-file set"), and `clear()` only fires from `/clear`, so turns 2..N editing the **same** file grew nothing and all three checks silently read as "no work" — the most common editing shape there is. Day 172 (#794 half (a)): a **second, independent** monotonic counter sits beside it — `tool_calls: Arc<AtomicUsize>`, read via `tool_call_count()`, bumped by `record_tool_call()` and zeroed by the same `clear()`. It counts **every tool call the turn started**, write-class or not (`bash`, `read_file`, `search`, `web_search`, `sub_agent` included), which `edit_count()` structurally cannot: `edits` is bumped only by `record`, i.e. only by `write_file`/`edit_file`/`rename_symbol` writes. The two are pinned as independent by test (`test_tool_call_count_and_edit_count_are_independent` — a `record` does not bump `tool_call_count` and a `record_tool_call` does not bump `edit_count`), because collapsing them *is* the defect #794 half (a) fixed. `edit_count()` and `snapshot().len()` keep their exact prior meanings and every existing test is untouched; the tool-usage argument to `should_auto_continue` (in both `repl.rs` and `main.rs`) is the one thing that moved from `edit_count()` to `tool_call_count()` — the per-turn change summary and the turn-end `did_work` still read the edit counter, correctly, since those are questions about *edits*.
- `commands_project.rs` — `/context`, `/init`, `/docs`, project-type detection, `auto_context_for_prompt` (automatic file injection for prompts — scores repo files against query keywords, returns top matches with content), `format_auto_context`. Day 175 (#817): `auto_context_for_prompt(prompt, recent_context, restrictions: &DirectoryRestrictions)` takes the **caller's** directory restrictions and filters candidate paths through `restrictions.check_path` **before** the read loop — so a denied file is never opened, never consumes one of the `AUTO_CONTEXT_MAX_FILES` slots, and never reaches the signature block. It had no restriction check of any kind (`grep -n 'dir_restrictions\|check_path' src/commands_project.rs` returned zero), while every *model-facing* file tool is wrapped by `maybe_guard`/`maybe_guard_arc` in `src/tools.rs` — so the same file was **refused when the model asked for it** and **silently pasted into the same API request** when auto-context picked it, under a green `📎 Auto-attached: <path>` note. Fourth instance of "same rule, two doors, one deaf" (#745, #767, #769, #816). Threading rather than a process-global: there is exactly **one** non-test call site (`src/repl.rs`, inside `run_repl`, which already holds `agent_config`), so it passes `&agent_config.dir_restrictions`; `commands.rs`'s entry is a name re-export and needed no edit. **When `restrictions.is_empty()` the returned vec is byte-identical to before** — `check_path` short-circuits to `Ok` — which is the case for almost every product user and was the regression risk. **Deliberately no refusal block is printed**: a denied file is simply not attached, because auto-context is a silent convenience the user did not ask for, unlike #748/#761 where the user *asked* for the thing being refused. **What it does not cover, stated plainly:** `permissions.deny` (the bash-command patterns) is a different mechanism and is untouched here; this is the directory fence only. Pinned at the **emission point** — the vec a caller receives — in both directions in one test (`test_auto_context_respects_directory_restrictions`): the denied path is absent when denied, and present with empty restrictions, since a discriminator tested only on the side that blocks is vacuous green.
- `commands_dev.rs` — `/doctor`, `/health`, `/fix` handlers. `run_doctor_checks` builds the structured `DoctorCheck` list, including a **skill context-cost audit** (`skill_context_cost_status`/`skill_bytes_to_tokens`): sums `SKILL.md` bytes across **every directory skills can be loaded from on this run** — the three auto-discovery dirs (`.yoyo/skills/`, `~/.yoyo/skills/`, and `/skill install`'s destination `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/yoyo/skills` or `~/.config/yoyo/skills` — added Day 164, #728) **and** any `--skills <dir>` passed on the command line (`cli::skill_flag_dirs`, recorded at parse time) — then estimates recurring context spend (~bytes/4) and warns over ~8k tokens. Day 151 (blind-spot experiment): the `--skills` half was previously missing, so a repo that loads all of its skills that way (this one loads `./skills`) was audited at ~0 tokens and told "no skills loaded" — a silent under-report dressed as a pass. Enumeration and summing are split into pure/testable halves (`skill_source_dirs` — no I/O; `skill_bytes_in_dirs` — dedups by canonicalized path so a dir named twice, e.g. `--skills .yoyo/skills`, is counted once). Reports cost honestly — does not claim to detect *unused* skills (no usage telemetry in a product context)
- `commands_info.rs` — the read-only status handlers: `/version`, `/status`, `/tokens`, `/cost`, `/model`, `/provider`, `/think`, `/profile`, `/changelog`, `/evolution`, `/tips`. Day 179 (#832): `handle_evolution` is a **thin wrapper** — it injects the two subprocess resolvers (`count_tests_via_cargo`, and `|| fetch_ci_runs(10)`) into the pure-ish `handle_evolution_with`, which holds the whole report and shells nothing; the resolvers are called at the same points in the same order, so `yoyo evolution` output is **byte-identical**. Same injected-resolver discipline as `added_ts` in `never_forecast_files` and the resolved title in `revisit_add_at`. **The measured defect, recorded rather than implied:** `test_handle_evolution_no_panic` called `handle_evolution` twice, and `handle_evolution` shelled `cargo test -- --list` — a **nested, feature-less cargo** that rebuilt the bin without `gasp` and uplifted it over the shared path `target/debug/yoyo`, clobbering the featured copy. Integration tests run after unit tests and reach the binary through `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_yoyo")`, which bakes in that **shared, feature-suffix-less** path — so `tests/gasp_cli_run_ordering.rs` got the plain binary and its honest refusal (`this build has no GASP recorder`) reddened `main` for three sessions (`a586be84`, `329a9bb8`, `524c50e4`). It read as flakiness because the victim passes in isolation (`--test gasp_cli_run_ordering` runs no unit tests, so nothing clobbers) and only the full `cargo test --features gasp` fails — deterministically, locally and in CI. That test is the **victim, not the cause**, and was not touched. **The standing rule that falls out of it: no `#[test]` under `src/` may spawn `cargo`** — not even with the right features, because the uplift path is shared across feature sets, so whichever build ran last decides what every later `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*` consumer receives; the nesting also cost ~12s for one test. Pinned by a deliberately **weak** source-level guard (`test_evolution_report_path_never_spawns_cargo`): exactly one `Command::new("cargo")` in the module, inside the resolver, and none in `handle_evolution_with` — it proves the spawn is *absent from that body*, never that no callee reaches one. **The sweep found a second hit and it is named rather than absorbed:** `security_audit_command` (`src/commands_lint.rs:612`) runs `cargo audit --version` from three `#[test]`s — a *subcommand probe* that builds nothing and so cannot uplift a binary, i.e. **not** the clobbering defect, but still a test whose answer depends on the machine's toolchain; filed as **#834**, together with the class gate (nothing enumerates the rest of the tree — this rule's only enforcer today is that one module's own test).
- `commands_file.rs` — `/add`, `/apply`, `/copy`, `/web`, `@file` mentions. Day 174 (#780): `suggest_related_files_in(root, path, already_added)` is the dir-taking seam — every filesystem probe resolves against `root`, while returned paths keep their relative form — and `suggest_related_files` is the `Path::new(".")` wrapper, so all production call sites are byte-identical. Its five tests now pass a tempdir instead of calling `std::env::set_current_dir`, which is **process-global** and so corrupts any sibling test resolving a relative path concurrently (`#[serial]` does not help: it only serialises against other `#[serial]` tests, leaving unmarked CWD-*readers* exposed — the observed victims were in `setup.rs`). The `#[serial]` attributes on those five were removed with the chdir, since leaving them keeps a false record of which tests are dangerous. Day 175 (#780, second cluster): `apply_patch_in(root, path, check_only)` is the same seam one function over — the existence probe resolves against `root` and **every** git invocation goes through the local `git_output_in`, with `apply_patch` kept as the `Path::new(".")` wrapper so the production `/apply` path is byte-identical. The redirection flag is `git -C <dir> apply`: `-C` is a git **global** and must sit before the subcommand, because `git apply -C<n>` is an entirely different flag (context lines that must match) — the strategy ladder's own `-C1` relaxation still sits after `apply`, so both `-C` forms coexist in one argv and neither is the other. That removed the last 7 `set_current_dir` lines in this file (5 tests: the four `setup_git_repo` users, which now get a `(TempDir, file_path)` pair with no cwd move, plus `test_apply_patch_from_string_valid_in_git_repo`), and the five `#[serial]` attributes went with them — leaving the file with none, so `use serial_test::serial;` was dropped too. **Still moving the CWD:** 2 test-side lines in `commands_git.rs` and the production `/cd` site in `dispatch.rs` — **3** calls of the original 22, down from 5. (Count from `grep -rn "set_current_dir" src/ --include=*.rs` run Day 175: 20 matching lines, of which 17 are comments documenting these seams and 3 are calls. Reading the matched lines rather than the raw count matters — a Day-175 assessment read "21 matches" as 21 chdirs and indicted this bullet as fictional bookkeeping when it was accurate.) `dispatch_sub.rs` left the list on Day 175 (#780, third cluster), and by a different route than its two siblings: its one offender was `goal_set_routing_stores_multi_word_goal_without_quotes`, which drives the whole `yoyo goal set …` route end-to-end (`try_dispatch_subcommand` → `handle_goal` → `save_goal` → `goal_path` → `goal_base`), so a dir-taking seam would have meant threading a root through the 36-arm dispatcher. `commands_goal.rs` already owned the right seam — the `#[cfg(test)]` thread-local `GOAL_BASE_DIR` every goal path resolves through — so it was made reachable from a sibling module's tests as `pub(crate) fn with_goal_base_dir(dir, f)` (restores the **previous** value rather than clearing, so nesting is safe, and restores on panic); `commands_goal.rs`'s own private `with_temp_dir` now delegates to it, so the rule has one statement rather than two copies. A thread-local is strictly better than a chdir here and that is the reason to prefer it: each test thread gets its own, so it cannot leak the way the process CWD does. The `#[serial]` went with the chdir; the file's two remaining `#[serial]` attributes are on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env-var tests — a different process-global, still legitimately serial — so `use serial_test::serial;` stays. The test gained an assertion at the **emission point** (`tmp.path().join(".yoyo/goal.md").exists()`), because every assertion it already had could be satisfied by a regression that wrote the *real* repo's goal file, which is the exact damage #780 names.
- `commands_ast_grep.rs` — `/ast` structural search (wraps ast-grep's `sg` binary): `is_ast_grep_available`, `run_ast_grep_search`, `parse_ast_grep_args`, `handle_ast_grep`. Day 168 (blind round 52): `sg`'s stdout is bounded by `cap_ast_output` at `AST_MAX_OUTPUT_LINES = 200` — a judgment threshold for readability, applied at **both** stdout-returning arms of `run_ast_grep_search` so every caller inherits the bound, with the cut marked in band (`… N more line(s) elided …`) because a silent elision is the bug. Under budget the output is byte-identical to before. `/ast` was the only one of the three search commands with no bound at all — `/grep` has `GREP_MAX_MATCHES = 50` and `/find` a `take(20)` — and it prints through a bare `println!`, never through the `TruncatingTool` decorator (that wraps agent *tools*, not slash commands), so nothing downstream would have capped it. Day 168 (#767) closed the sibling defect that bullet used to carry as known-and-unfixed: the completion hint advertised `/ast <pattern> [path]`, but the parser honours a path only via `--in`, and its catch-all arm folded a bare second positional into the *pattern* string — so `/ast $X.unwrap() src/` searched for the literal pattern `"$X.unwrap() src/"` and reported `No matches found.`. The hint was corrected first; per the Day-164 lesson (a doc-side repair of a doc/code mismatch deletes the only detector while leaving everything demonstrably honest), the parser half is now fixed too. `parse_ast_grep_args` **refuses** that shape rather than guessing: the pure `bare_path_argument(pattern_parts, exists)` recognises only the unambiguous case — **≥2** pattern tokens (so a single-token pattern that happens to name a file, `/ast mod.rs`, keeps its escape hatch), the **last** token only (a path argument trails), that token exists on disk per an injected resolver (the I/O stays at the call site so the decision stays pure and table-tested), and no `--in` was given (an explicit path disambiguates). The error names the offending token and the escape hatch verbatim (`Did you mean: /ast <pattern> --in src/`). It deliberately does **not** treat the token as the path — guessing would silently mis-scope a legitimate pattern, trading one silent wrong-op for another; refusing turns it into an actionable error. The pre-existing characterization test that pinned the folding behaviour was rewritten to assert the fix in **both** directions (a real path is refused; a trailing non-path token like `$Y` still joins the pattern), rather than left as a fixture asserting a known-wrong output. Day 169: `/ast` has a **CLI door** — `yoyo ast '$X.unwrap()' --in src/` routes through `try_dispatch_subcommand` to the same `handle_ast_grep`, args forwarded verbatim, so every correction the handler earned (#767's bare-path refusal, `cap_ast_output`'s 200-line bound) comes along rather than a second implementation starting at zero corrections. Before this the multi-token form slipped past the Day-165 near-miss guard (which only inspects the bare `yoyo <word>` shape) and started a billed LLM turn; bare `yoyo ast` now reaches the handler's own usage message instead.
- `commands_rename.rs` — rename symbol across project files, word-boundary matching, preview and apply. Day 170 (#778): `/rename` is a **recorded write path** — `handle_rename(input, changes: &SessionChanges)` records every file it actually wrote with `ChangeKind::Edit` (via `apply_rename_reporting`, which returns `(replacements, written_paths)`; `apply_rename` is a one-line `.0` wrapper so no existing test changed), and both doors pass `ctx.session_changes`: `dispatch.rs`'s `/rename` arm and `/refactor rename`, which forwards through `handle_refactor(input, changes)`. It records what was **written**, not what was matched — a file that fails to read or write is skipped by the writer and so is never recorded. Before this the command rewrote every matched file and recorded nothing, so the three per-turn checks in `repl.rs` (change summary, `should_auto_continue`'s `used_tools`, the turn-end `did_work`), `--output-format json`'s `session` summary, and the post-prompt watch gate all read a multi-file rename as "nothing happened". Day 170 (#783) closed the sibling defect on the *agent-tool* path — the `rename_symbol` tool the **model** calls, which reaches `rename_in_files` via `rename_in_project_restricted` and so inherited nothing from #778. It could not be fixed by adding a name to the `write_file`/`edit_file` match in `prompt.rs`, because those record off a single `path` **argument** while a rename's written set is only known *after* the call: `rename_in_files` now calls `apply_rename_reporting` too and returns that set on `RenameResult.written`, `RenameSymbolTool` emits it as the `files_written` key of the tool result's `details`, and `prompt::record_rename_tool_writes` records those paths as `ChangeKind::Edit` in `handle_tool_execution_end` — **only when `is_error` is false**, so a failed rename records nothing. Same discipline as #778: what was **written**, never what was matched (a file the writer skipped — unreadable, unwritable, or denied by `dir_restrictions` — never reaches `written`). `apply_rename` is now `#[cfg(test)]`-only, since every production caller needs the written list; it is kept rather than deleted so its existing tests keep asserting the exact call shape they were written for. **No longer true as of Day 170 (#786) — the third door of this family is closed:** the `--output-format json` event path (`prompt::handle_stream_json_events`, reached from `run_prompt_stream_json` / `run_prompt_stream_json_with_content`) now takes a `changes: &SessionChanges` and records through the **same two free functions** the display path uses — `record_tool_arg_writes` at ToolExecutionStart for `write_file`/`edit_file`, and `record_rename_tool_writes` at ToolExecutionEnd for `rename_symbol`, still only when `is_error` is false. It was handed no tracker at all before that, so it recorded neither renames **nor** ordinary edits, and it is the path whose entire purpose is machine-readable reporting: its own `session` summary reported zero edits after a run that edited files, and the per-turn summary, `should_auto_continue`'s `used_tools`, the turn-end `did_work` and the `should_run_watch_after_prompt` gate all read the same zero. The tracker threaded in is the **caller's own** — the single `SessionChanges::new()` binding already present in `run_single_prompt` / `run_piped_mode`, moved above the stream-json early return rather than a second one constructed there (#678's defect). Pinned by three tests driving synthetic `AgentEvent`s through `handle_stream_json_events` and asserting on `changes.snapshot()` / `edit_count()` — the tracker a caller receives — not on the emitted JSON.
- `commands_refactor.rs` — `/refactor` (umbrella: `rename`/`extract`/`move`, with a catch-all arm that names an unknown verb and lists the available ones) and `/extract` (`parse_extract_args`, `find_symbol_block`, `extract_symbol`, `handle_extract`, `handle_refactor`). These operate on **source text, not ASTs** — which the `/refactor` overview says out loud (`commands_refactor.rs:358`) and which is the root of #770 below. Day 168 (blind round 55, a never-forecast file): `extract_symbol` creates the target's missing parent directories (`create_dir_all`, skipped when the parent path is empty — a bare filename — or already exists), and the guard is placed **before the source write**, which is the half that mattered: the two writes are ordered source-then-target, so `/extract MAX src/config.rs newdir/constants.rs` used to delete the symbol from the source and then fail writing the target, leaving the code **nowhere** — after the preview and after the user had already confirmed. `/extract`'s help (`help_data.rs:262`) promised "Creates the target file if it doesn't exist" and the write path called `fs::write` with no `mkdir -p`; the doc claim was broader than the code, so the code was moved up to it rather than the promise trimmed. Pinned at the **emission point** — both tests call `extract_symbol` and assert the `Result` a caller actually receives: `extract_symbol_creates_missing_target_parent_dir` (two missing levels: `Ok`, target content present, **and** the source not mutilated) and `extract_symbol_bare_target_filename_still_works` (a target whose parent already exists still returns `Ok` and writes — the pass-through half, so the guard cannot regress the common case). Stated plainly: the third branch — an **empty** parent path, i.e. a bare relative filename — is guarded in code but **not** covered by a test, because exercising it would make `extract_symbol` write into the test process's real cwd. Day 169 (#770): `find_symbol_block`'s forward brace scan no longer counts braces that are not structural. It used to be a bare `for ch in line.chars()` with `depth += 1` / `depth -= 1` and no string/char-literal or comment state, so a body containing `println!("}")` or a trailing `// }` ended the block **too early** and a `println!("{")` ended it **too late** — and because `extract_symbol` deletes exactly that line range from the source and appends the captured text to the target, both files were left wrong, with the preview showing the same wrong block the write would use (confirming the prompt did not protect the user). The scan now iterates the pure `significant_braces(line, &mut BraceScanState) -> Vec<char>`, which returns the structurally significant `{` / `}` **in order** (not an open/close count — a line like `} fn other() {` both closes a block and opens one, and the caller's depth machine must see those in sequence); the call site's logic is otherwise byte-identical. **Named classes covered** (this is not a claim to handle Rust syntax): `"…"` strings with `\` escapes (so `"\""` does not end the string), raw strings `r"…"` and `r#"…"#` at any hash count plus the `b` byte prefix (`raw_string_open`, which uses `is_ident_char` to refuse an `r` that is merely the tail of an identifier), `'x'` / `'\n'` / `'\u{7d}'` char literals **without** mistaking a lifetime for one (`char_literal_len` returns `None` for the lone `'` in `&'a str`, so it is treated as an ordinary character — the same trap as open issue #759, deliberately not repeated), `//` line comments, and `/* … */` block comments same-line, multi-line **and nested** — the state the caller carries is a `&mut usize` **depth**, not a bool (Day 171, #771 item 1): an inner `/*` while already inside a comment increments it, a `*/` decrements, and only depth 0 resumes scanning code, so `/* /* */ */` no longer ends the comment at the first `*/`; a stray `*/` at depth 0 is ignored rather than underflowing. It walks a `Vec<char>` and never indexes a `&str` by byte, so multi-byte input cannot panic. Day 171 (#771 item 2) added the second carried fact beside that depth: **string literals that span lines**. The two facts now live in one `BraceScanState` (`block_comment_depth: usize` + `open_string: Option<StringDelim>`, `Default`-derived) rather than as two out-parameters, so the next carried fact does not churn every call site again, and **both delimiter classes are carried**: `StringDelim::Normal` (a plain `"…"`, closed by a `"` that is not backslash-escaped — a trailing `\` escaping the newline just means it is still open) and `StringDelim::Raw(n)` (`r"…"` / `r#"…"#` at any hash count, closed by a `"` followed by exactly `n` `#`, with no escapes, so a `"#` inside an `r##"…"##` does not close it). On entry the shared `close_open_string` helper looks for that closer **first**, emits nothing before it, and resumes ordinary scanning on the same line just past it; when the closer is not on the line, an empty `Vec` is returned because every brace on it is string content. **What is still not handled, stated rather than widened:** this is a brace scanner, not a Rust lexer — `#[cfg]`-disabled code, macro token trees with deliberately unbalanced braces, and nested `{}` inside a format string's `{…}` argument capture all still count as ordinary code. That issue's **item 3** — the sibling data-corruption class in `src/commands_move.rs` — **was fixed the same day** (see that bullet), which is why `significant_braces` is now `pub(crate)` with **two consumers**: `find_symbol_block` here and `find_impl_blocks` / `find_method_in_impl` in `commands_move.rs`. One scanner, one set of table tests — the alternative was a second brace counter drifting from this one. Day 169 (#759) made `char_literal_len` itself `pub(crate)` for the same reason and with the same discipline: `src/format/highlight.rs` had a **private copy of this exact discrimination with the lifetime bug still in it**, so it now calls this helper (`is_rust_char_literal`) rather than keeping a third quote scanner — the consumer landed in the same diff as the visibility change. `char_literal_len` therefore has two cross-module consumers (`significant_braces` here, the Rust arm of `highlight_code_line`) and one table test. Day 173 (#806) extended that same sharing to the other two literal helpers rather than letting a copy grow back: `raw_string_open` and `close_open_string` are now `pub(crate)` as well, with `format::highlight` as their second consumer (its own private single-purpose `close_open_string` was deleted in that diff), and `StringDelim` gained a third variant `Backtick { escapes }` — JS/TS template literals honour `` \` ``, Go raw strings do not — which only the highlighter ever *opens*, since Rust has no backtick literal and `significant_braces` therefore never constructs one. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased:** this bullet used to say the highlighter is "line-based with no block-comment state at all, strictly more limited than `significant_braces`". That was true when written and has been false since Day 173 — the highlighter now carries block-comment depth and all three string delimiters in its own `HighlightState`. The two are still not one scanner (they answer different questions — which braces are structural, vs. which spans get string colour), but the literal-skipping rules underneath are shared code with one table, not two copies drifting. #771 is now **fully closed**: item 3 (the `/move` sibling) Day 169, item 1 (nested block comments) and item 2 (multi-line strings) Day 171. Proven in **two layers**: the `significant_braces_table` unit table (brace in a string, in a trailing `//`, inside a same-line `/* */`, a block comment spanning three lines, a nested `/* /* */ */` both same-line and spanning three lines, an unbalanced `*/` at depth 0, a `/*` inside a string literal, `"\""`, `fn f<'a>(x: &'a str) -> &'a str {`, `r#"}"#`, and a plain `if x { y } else {` that must still return `['{','}','{']`), and — the layer that matters, because the user receives two files — the emission-point tempdir tests `extract_symbol_moves_whole_symbol_with_brace_in_string` (target holds the whole fn including its closing brace; source no longer holds it, still holds its neighbour, and has equal `{` and `}` counts) and `extract_symbol_open_brace_in_string_leaves_following_fn_in_source` (the over-long direction: a following `fn after() {}` stays in the source and does not appear in the target), plus three `find_symbol_block` cases pinned against the exact wrong ranges #770 recorded. Item 2 got the same two layers rather than a unit-only pass: a sibling `significant_braces_multiline_string_table` driving **sequences** of lines through one carried state (plain multi-line, `r#"` and `r##"` multi-line, an escaped `\"` on a continuation line, a trailing `\` line-continuation, a same-line close as an explicit regression guard, and a `}` after the closer on the closing line as the resume guard — because the defect only exists *across* lines, a single-line table cannot see it), and the emission-point pair `extract_symbol_moves_whole_symbol_with_brace_in_multiline_string` / `extract_symbol_open_brace_in_multiline_string_leaves_following_fn_in_source`. No existing test was deleted or weakened — the original single-line table kept every row verbatim and only its driver changed.
- `commands_search.rs` — `/find`, `/grep`, `/index`, `/outline`, `/def` (`handle_def` — find symbol definition: reuses `symbols::detect_language`/`extract_symbols` to locate where a symbol is defined, prints `path:line` + source line; a small go-to-definition gesture, no LSP; forgives messy pasted input via `normalize_symbol_query` — `foo()`, `&foo`, `mod::foo`, backticks, trailing punctuation — pinned by a fixture-table test). Day 169: `/def` has a **CLI door** — `yoyo def <symbol>` routes through `try_dispatch_subcommand` to the same `handle_def`, forwarding its args verbatim; before this, `yoyo def handle_goal` was three tokens, so it slipped past the Day-165 bare-word near-miss guard (which only inspects `yoyo <word>`) and fell through to the single-prompt path — a paid LLM turn with write-capable tools, answering a search question from priors. `find`/`grep`/`index`/`outline` already had doors; this is the "two doors, one works" shape of #745 and #767 again.
- `commands_revisit.rs` — `/revisit` command: scan closed/shelved GitHub issues, check feasibility, track revisit candidates in `.yoyo/revisit.json`. Day 165 (#740): the file's three states are kept distinct instead of collapsing into `Vec::new()` — `parse_revisit_file` (pure, `&str` → `Result`) and `load_revisit_list_from(path)` return `Ok(vec![])` only for a **missing** file (and for a zero-byte/whitespace-only one, which has no surviving entries to lose), and `Err(msg)` naming the path for an unreadable or unparseable one. The old `unwrap_or_default()` made a truncated file read as "empty", so `/revisit list` printed `No revisit candidates` and — the reason this mattered — the next `/revisit add` loaded `[]`, pushed one entry, and `fs::write` **destroyed every surviving candidate** under a green `✓ Added #N` line. The write now refuses: `revisit_add_at` / `revisit_remove_at` (path-taking, so tests never touch the real `.yoyo/revisit.json`) return `corrupt_file_message` — what broke, where the file is, and that nothing was changed — **before** reaching `save_revisit_list_to`. Read paths (`scan`, `check`) never load the file. Day 170 (#741) closed the sibling `/revisit add` defect that bullet used to carry as known-and-unfixed: every hand-added entry stored the permanent placeholder `(issue #N)` as its **title**, so `/revisit list` rendered `#100 (issue #100)` — the number repeated in the one column meant to say *which* issue it is — and because `add` made no `gh` call at all, `/revisit add #999999 whatever` printed the plain green `✓ Added` line for an issue that does not exist. `add` now resolves the real title: `fetch_issue_title(number)` shells `gh issue view <n> --json title` in the **same shape** as the pre-existing `fetch_issue_detail` (same `Command::new("gh")`, same `Failed to run gh` / `gh issue view failed: {stderr}` errors — not a second JSON convention), and the decision half is the pure, table-tested `parse_issue_title(json) -> Result<String, String>`, where an empty, blank, absent, wrong-typed or `null` title is an **`Err`, never `Ok("")`** (absence gets its own name). The I/O stays at the one caller: `revisit_add_at(path, number, title: Option<&str>, reason, day)` takes the **resolved title as an argument** — the same injected-resolver discipline `never_forecast_files` uses for `git_added_ts` — so every existing test drives it with no network and the `gh` call happens only in `handle_revisit`'s `add` arm. **Failure degrades honestly rather than silently**: the command stays usable offline, so a missing `gh` / failed call / unknown issue **still adds the entry** with the `(issue #N)` placeholder stored unchanged, but the message is *not* the plain green success text — it names the stored placeholder verbatim and states that the title could not be fetched and the issue was **not verified to exist**, because the old text asserted more than it knew. Pinned at the **emission point** (the two strings a caller actually receives — resolved-title line carries the title and neither the placeholder nor the unverified claim; degraded line carries both admissions) plus the `parse_issue_title` table. **Deliberately not done:** entries already in `.yoyo/revisit.json` are **not** back-filled — rewriting stored history is a separate decision — and `/revisit scan` / `/revisit check` are untouched, so they still fetch titles for display without writing them into the stored list. The #740 refusal path is byte-identical: the corrupt-file check still runs **before** anything is written, so a damaged file refuses the write whether or not a title resolved.
- `commands_risk.rs` — /risk command: file risk scoring (`compute_file_risk_scores`, `learn_weights_from_history`, `detect_emerging_risks`), history, predict, co-change coupling, `top_risk_files` helper for cross-module risk queries. Day 174: the **risk-score universe is filtered to paths that exist on disk** — `compute_file_risk_scores` assembles its `file_set` from four sources (two git-churn windows, two `read_dir` walks) and passes it through the pure `scorable_paths(candidates, exists)` before scoring, with the I/O injected at the single call site (`&|p| Path::new(p).exists()`) exactly as `never_forecast_files` injects `added_ts` and `revisit_add_at` injects the resolved title. **The reason, which is the only thing that will stop someone simplifying the filter away later: a file's own deletion commit is churn.** The two disk walks are existence-checked by construction; the two churn windows are not, so deleting a file hands it a fresh 30-day *and* 7-day change count — i.e. a fresh risk score — while simultaneously guaranteeing it has never appeared in any prediction column, which sorts it to the top of the *never forecast* dark set. That set is not cosmetic: `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` feeds those rows to the planner as "point the self-driven slot here", so a deleted file becomes the most attractive target in the one output that steers task selection. Live instance: `src/commands_risk_families.rs`, deleted Day 173 by the orphan-module gate (#804), led the Day-174 dark set and had already eaten one session (receipt **#807**, an empty-diff revert on "finish the aborted extraction — compile `commands_risk_families.rs`"). Measured before/after on the real command: `never forecast` went from 6 files with the deleted one in the list to 5 real files, and `src/sync_util.rs` — previously hidden behind the `... (+1 more)` sample cap — became visible. **Note the polarity, which is the opposite of the Day-166 too-new split**: that one stopped calling genuinely-*new* files dark; this one drops a file that can **never** be studied from a list of rooms worth entering. Deliberately narrow: nothing else is filtered — past ledger lines in `.yoyo/risk_snapshots.jsonl` and `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl` are never rewritten or back-filled, because a snapshot that once predicted a now-deleted file is a true record of what yoyo once claimed; this changes what gets **scored today**, nothing about history. Row shape and section headers are byte-identical, so `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` / `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE` still match (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes). `classify_broke_files` filters its returned set to `src/` paths (#708), so all four grading call sites share the risk model's actual prediction universe — a `tests/` or `Cargo.toml` path was a guaranteed miss that dragged recall down with noise. Day 163 added **two-tier corroboration** to the same helper: a *revert* message is breakage evidence on its own (tier 1), but a *fix-family* message counts a `src/` file only if that file also appears in at least one **other** commit in the window (tier 2) — because yoyo's own delivered task commits are titled `Day 163 (01:56): Fix #710 — …`, so an ordinary green feature-delivery session was grading as a failure day and feeding every file it touched into the meter as breakage (the Day-148 lesson verbatim: the intake filter *was* yoyo's commit-message convention). Day 163 (second layer) narrowed tier 2 further: the "other commit" must not be harness bookkeeping (`is_mechanical_commit` — `cargo fmt`, `session wrap-up`, `journal entry`, `update learnings`, `assessment (draft)`, `docs(assessment)`, `session plan`, `bump skill-evolve counter`, `social session`), because `scripts/evolve.sh` commits `cargo fmt` separately after **every** task, green or red, touching exactly the files the task touched — so corroboration was being manufactured on every task and carried zero bits (9 of 15 validation events since 2026-08-08 were green sessions wearing failure-day labels). `has_uncorroborated_repair_evidence` routes through the same predicate so the two halves can't disagree about what a second opinion is. The filter is legitimate only because those subjects come from yoyo's own harness: a human commit literally titled "cargo fmt" would be misclassified, and a wording change in `evolve.sh` would silently stop covering it — `test_is_mechanical_commit_covers_harness_vocabulary` pins the list so that drift fails a test instead of polluting recall. Day 165 added the **fix-loop retry family** to that same harness vocabulary: `scripts/evolve.sh` commits fix attempts with a `(Task N, eval-fix M)` suffix, and `message_claims_repair` tokenizes on non-alphanumerics, so `eval-fix` → `["eval", "fix"]` made **every** retry commit a repair claim — while the sibling retry commits in the same window touched the same files and so corroborated *each other* under tier 2, the harness again supplying both the claim and its second opinion. The match is on the retry **suffix marker**, not a bare substring: `has_retry_suffix` accepts `(task N,` (a comma after the number is the retry marker) and deliberately **not** `(task N)`, which is an ordinary delivered task commit, so a human commit that merely contains the word "task" is not swallowed. Routing is unchanged — `is_mechanical_commit` is still the single predicate `has_uncorroborated_repair_evidence` shares. Three Day-165 events (2026-08-12 08:26, 15:04, 18:21 in `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl`) were booked as failure days through this hole — the 08:26 and 15:04 sessions were 2/2 green, and the 15:04 window is five `Blind round 38 … eval-fix N` commits all touching `src/setup.rs` — and they were **not** rewritten (past events are never rewritten; the recovery is forward-only), so `/risk accuracy` and the 23% reactive / 0% emerging numbers it feeds are recovering, not clean. Day 163 (#717) added the **third value** on top of that: `has_uncorroborated_repair_evidence` detects a window where a non-revert commit claims a repair of a `src/` file but corroboration dropped every candidate, and the CLI validate path (`handle_risk_validate`) then records **nothing** — an ungraded window with one honest stderr line — instead of falling through to the GREEN branch and booking a genuine failure day as crying-wolf evidence (tier 1 is structurally dead in this repo: `scripts/evolve.sh` reverts with `git reset --hard`, never a revert commit; the watch-failure path `auto_validate_after_failure` still has the same hole, see follow-up). Past events in `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl` are never rewritten, so `/risk accuracy` still blends the already-polluted untagged events with future honest ones — that number is recovering, not clean.
