The `/risk effectiveness` verdict (`effectiveness_report_from` → `compute_effectiveness_verdict`, also surfaced via `reflex_effectiveness_summary` in `/status`) grades **failure-day events only** — green-day (`watch_success`) events are filtered out via `is_green_event` before the early/recent windows and the `MIN_EFFECTIVENESS_EVENTS` gate, so the verdict is a pure recall-trend signal and the report prints how many green events were excluded (false-alarm rate lives in `/risk accuracy`)
- `commands_risk_report.rs` — report/context formatting for /risk: `format_risk_report`, `format_risk_context`, `risk_context_for_files`, `file_risk_summary`, `prediction_accuracy_summary` (Day 174: returns a **named** headline string, never the blend — see below), `recall_coverage_note`, `emerging_track_record_note`; re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`). Day 163 (#724): `emerging_track_record_note` was **re-homed** — its only caller used to be the now-deleted `⚡ Emerging Risks` renderer, so it is printed under `/risk accuracy` instead, where it is *evidence for the deletion* rather than a caveat attached to a live forecast. Day 163 (#720 step 2): the emerging track-record note carries not just the measured recall (the numerator) but the **pooled achievable ceiling** beside it (the denominator's honest upper bound) — `emerging_achievable_ceiling` asks *what is the best recall this column could possibly have scored, given how many files its list actually named at grading time*, so a 0% can no longer be blamed on the instrument without checking. Method: graded failure-day events only (the same predicate the note uses — `commands_risk_accuracy::is_green_event` is called, never re-derived — plus `emerging_accuracy_pct.is_some()`); each event is paired with the latest snapshot whose `ts` is **strictly less than** the event's `ts` (strict `<` is deliberate: an observed event carries a `ts` byte-identical to a snapshot's, and `yoyo risk validate` grades against the *prior* snapshot before writing the new one, so `<=` would grade an outcome against a snapshot written after it — pinned by an equal-ts test); the ceiling is pooled, `sum(min(list_len, outcome_len)) / sum(outcome_len)`, not an average of per-event percentages (Day 149: averaging per-event percentages silently reweights tiny events upward), so it is commensurable with `failure_hit_rate_pct`. Absence gets its own name (Day 144): an event with no `ts`, no preceding snapshot, or an empty outcome set is **excluded** and counted in `EmergingCeiling.excluded`, never folded in as a 0; zero surviving events → `None`, never `Some(0.0)`, and a `None` ceiling adds no text so all three original note states stay byte-identical. `ceiling_clause` branches on direction — it claims "the list named enough files to have hit" only when the ceiling exceeds the measured recall, and says the opposite when it doesn't — and always discloses that the pairing is approximate, because failure-day validation events record no `snapshot_git_hash` and so cannot be audited against the exact prediction they graded (#723). **Observed Day 163: 0% actual against ~39% achievable over 10 graded failure-day events** — a dated observation of the current ledger recomputed on every render, not a permanent property, and not a hardcoded number. The gap is the finding: the column had roughly 39 points of headroom and scored zero, so the zero is the forecast's, not the instrument's. `TimedValidationEvent`/`parse_timed_validation_events` exist only to carry each line's `ts` alongside the shared `ValidationEvent` (which has no timestamp field); the parser delegates to `parse_validation_events` line by line rather than duplicating it, and the snapshot side reuses the existing `ParsedSnapshot` reader — no new persistence, no second parser.
- `commands_risk_emerging.rs` — emerging-risk / anticipatory detection for /risk: `EmergingRisk`, `detect_emerging_risks`, momentum helpers; re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`). **The display is deleted (Day 163, #724)** — see below; what remains here is the *detector*, not a rendered forecast. Day 145 min-sample floor (`MIN_MOMENTUM_SAMPLES = 3`): the momentum ratio saturates to a constant `30/7 ≈ 4.29` whenever a file's only changes fall inside the 7-day window (`c7 == c30`), so files with < MIN total 30-day changes are excluded from the emerging list (explicit third value — not eligible — rather than absorbed at saturated-high momentum into the top), keeping the anticipatory column discriminating rather than a flat block of ties. Day 163 (#724) — **step 3 of #720, the decision, taken: delete.** The `⚡ Emerging Risks` block no longer exists in `/risk` output, and `format_emerging_risks` / `format_emerging_risks_with` were removed along with the tests that existed only to pin those two functions' output (removing a function's own tests *together with the function* is the deletion's other half, not test deletion — nothing else was removed). The number that forced it: **0% emerging recall over 10 graded failure-day events** against a **~39% pooled achievable ceiling**, while the reactive column scored **24%** on the same days — the pre-registered rule in #724 fired, so the momentum formula is falsified *as implemented*. Two further measurements over the 130 snapshots in `.yoyo/risk_snapshots.jsonl`: the emerging list was **empty in 46 of 130 (35%)** — those abstentions are what the 19 "ungraded — no emerging forecast recorded" failure days actually were — and among the 62 snapshots where both lists were non-empty, a mean **63% of emerging entries were already in `top_10`**, with 27 of 62 overlapping completely. So it was mostly an abstaining re-slice of the reactive column, and where it did differ it scored zero. **Delete meant: stop displaying the forecast and stop claiming it as a working capability — not stop measuring it.** Retained: `EmergingRisk`, `detect_emerging_risks`, the momentum helpers and all their tests; the snapshot path that records the emerging list; the grading path (`emerging_grade_of`, the three call sites) that scores it; the emerging lines in `/risk accuracy` (those are the *evidence for this decision*, not the forecast); and every past ledger line in `.yoyo/risk_snapshots.jsonl` / `.yoyo/risk_validations.jsonl`, which are never rewritten. That is the meter, not the claim — and the only thing that could ever justify a rebuild with evidence. No `#[allow(dead_code)]` was left behind; the orphaned `emerging_track_record_note` chain was re-homed under `/risk accuracy` (see the `commands_risk_report.rs` bullet). Day 163 (#726) finished the consumer half: the prompt injections in `watch.rs` (`build_watch_fix_prompt`) and `commands_project.rs` (`build_emerging_risk_map` and its project-context annotation) are **gone**, along with the tests that existed only to pin those annotation strings — #724 deleted the description while the consumers that actually steered behavior kept running on a forecast measured at 0% recall, and no separate evidence ever existed that annotating a file changed fix outcomes, so those sites inherited #724's verdict. `detect_emerging_risks` now has exactly two consumers, both on the meter side: `commands_risk.rs` (`/risk accuracy` evidence) and `commands_risk_snapshots.rs` (snapshot recording + grading). Nothing feeds the emerging list into a prompt. Day 163 (#726, third sibling) closed the enumeration: the **epistemic ranking's `reactive/emerging` disagreement signal** (`W_DISAGREE`, `DISAGREE_WINDOW`, `rank_magnitude`, and the `columns disagree N of last M snapshots (magnitude X)` reason, plus its `EPISTEMIC_DISAGREE_RE` compaction in `scripts/extract_trajectory.py`) is **removed** — it was the third and last live *claim*-side consumer, and it was steering task selection: it produced the entire live top-3 of `yoyo risk epistemic`, which `extract_trajectory.py` hands the planner as "point your self-driven slot here". Its own tests went with it (the #724 precedent); every never-graded / stale / study-state / never-forecast / families test is untouched. Bring-back condition, same as the display's: a measured non-zero emerging recall on graded failure days. **Residual reads that are deliberately kept** (so the two bullets cannot disagree): `ParsedSnapshot.emerging` is still read in `commands_risk_epistemic.rs` for *membership* accounting only — it counts toward a file's `appearances` in the "predicted N×, never graded" reason text (never toward the score, which is a flat `W_NEVER_GRADED`), and `never_forecast_files` unions it with `predicted` to decide which files no column has *ever* named. Both are statements about what yoyo once claimed, not forecasts about what will break, so they stay on the meter side of the line.
- `commands_risk_snapshots.rs` — snapshot/validation persistence for /risk: `auto_risk_snapshot` (dedups by git hash — one snapshot per distinct HEAD, keeps accumulation clean; skips re-recording the same commit-state via `last_snapshot_git_hash`), `auto_validate_after_failure`, snapshot JSONL parsing (`ParsedSnapshot` — now carries both `predicted` (reactive `top_10` paths) and `emerging` (anticipatory momentum paths), `ValidationEvent`); each snapshot records both `top_10` (reactive/homeostatic risk) AND `emerging` (anticipatory/allostatic momentum predictions — the DREAM's allostatic signal); `ValidationEvent` now also carries an optional `emerging_accuracy_pct` (absent → `None` on read; all optional fields are parsed defensively so severity-less / emerging-less lines stay valid), and `auto_validate_after_failure` grades BOTH the reactive `top_10` AND the anticipatory `emerging` list against the same outcome (shared `pub(crate) accuracy_of` helper) — closing the loop the Day 138 "persist emerging predictions" task opened, so the allostatic-vs-homeostatic comparison is now recorded (and surfaced as a one-line stderr comparison), not just the reactive half. The CLI validate path (`handle_risk_validate` in `commands_risk.rs`, behind `yoyo risk validate` — the crank that actually turns every session) grades both columns too via the same `accuracy_of` helper (Day 139): legacy snapshots without an `emerging` key gracefully yield `None` (ungraded, doesn't drag the average). Day 144: all three grading call sites (green, watch-failure, CLI validate) route the emerging column through one shared `emerging_grade_of` helper — an empty emerging list grades as ungraded (`None`), never `Some(0.0)`, pinned by tests so the previously-triplicated inline logic can't drift. Day 140: `yoyo risk validate` also grades GREEN outcomes — when commits exist since the last snapshot but nothing broke, `record_green_validation_to` writes a `severity:"watch_success"` event (`trigger:"cli"`; same green marker the watch path uses, so readers see one vocabulary) grading both `top_10` and `emerging` against the files that were touched — under a green outcome a predicted-risky file that changed without breaking is false-positive evidence, the meter's other half. Each snapshot is green-graded at most once: the event carries `snapshot_git_hash` and `green_event_exists_for` skips repeats (`GreenGrade::Deduped`, silent); no-src-change sessions write nothing (`GreenGrade::NoSrcChanges` — a 0/0 event would drag the average); a recorded green event prints one honest stderr line. Day 165 (#723): failure-day events (`auto_validate_after_failure`, the CLI red path, and CI harvest) also record `snapshot_git_hash` — the snapshot whose prediction list they graded — purely for **auditability**, not as a dedup key; the green dedup now keys on the `watch_success` severity marker (`is_green_json_event` inside `green_event_exists_for`) so a red event carrying the same hash can never suppress a legitimate green grade. Past events are deliberately **not** back-filled (reconstructing which snapshot was graded would manufacture evidence), so the emerging-ceiling pairing in `commands_risk_report.rs` stays timestamp-based for historical events and becomes hash-auditable only going forward. Green events count toward `compute_accuracy_stats` like any other graded event; re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`). `auto_risk_snapshot` fires ONLY from yoyo's own `/commit` handler and (opt-in) on REPL exit under `YOYO_RISK_AUTOSNAPSHOT=1` — it does **NOT** fire in the evolve loop, which commits with raw `git commit`, so the snapshot half of the prediction meter accumulates only through a human/harness invocation of `yoyo risk snapshot`. `YOYO_RISK_AUTOSNAPSHOT=1` (opt-in, off by default, product-safe — also accepts `true`/`yes`) captures a risk snapshot on REPL exit (dedup-guarded by git hash) so the prediction meter's snapshot half accumulates outside `/commit`. Both meter halves are now CLI-callable AND recording (`yoyo risk snapshot`; `yoyo risk validate`), enabling a human-approved automated cadence. Day 147: the shared git-log parser these paths feed on (`parse_git_log_name_only` in `commands_risk.rs`) now detects commit boundaries by the `--oneline` header shape (`looks_like_oneline_commit_header` — 7–40 hex chars + space + non-empty subject) instead of blank lines, which real `git log --oneline --name-only` never emits: previously every multi-commit log collapsed into ONE entry whose `files` list absorbed the following commits' subject lines, so `commit_count` was always 1 and `classify_broke_files` only ever read the first commit's message — making the failure-day (recall) branch unreachable unless the first commit since the snapshot happened to say "fix". Commit counts are now honest and the red branch is **reachable and, since Day 147's chosen experiment, exercised under test** (`test_failure_day_red_branch_fires_end_to_end` in `commands_risk.rs` drives a synthetic failure day end-to-end through the real chain — verbatim `git log --oneline --name-only` fixture → `parse_git_log_name_only` → `classify_broke_files` → `compute_validation` → tempdir `write_validation_event` → `compute_accuracy_stats` → `recall_coverage_note`/report — and pins that an untagged event grades as a failure day, `failure_hit_rate_pct` becomes `Some`, and the "recall ungraded" note switches off; no real failure day has occurred since the fix, so the live `/risk accuracy` zero remains an honest observation about the world rather than a dead path).
- `commands_risk_parse.rs` — the JSONL ledger *readers* for `/risk` (`ValidationEvent`, `load_validation_history_from`, `parse_validation_events`, `parse_graded_events`, `ParsedSnapshot`/`parse_all_snapshots`, `parse_failed_ci_runs`, `ci_event_exists_for`, `snapshot_before`), extracted verbatim from `commands_risk.rs` on Day 166 — which is why they never received the Day-165 `/revisit` correction (#740) and every one of them still did `Err(_) => continue` per line with no diagnostic anywhere. Day 169 (#764, **scoped to the validation ledger only**) gave that one surface three explicit states, because the ledger is the DREAM milestone's meter and a silently-shrinking denominator reports a confident number over an unknown fraction of the evidence: `ValidationLedger { Missing, Unreadable(String), Present { events, dropped } }` via `read_validation_ledger(path)` — `Missing` **only** when the path does not exist, `Unreadable(msg)` when it exists and `read_to_string` fails (the message names the path and the io error), `Present` otherwise, **including the all-corrupt case**, which is `Present { events: [], dropped: n }` and is a *different fact* from `Missing` rather than being collapsed into it. The counting half is `parse_validation_events_counting(content) -> (Vec<ValidationEvent>, usize)`, where the `usize` is the number of **non-blank** lines that failed `serde_json::from_str` — blank lines are not corruption and a trailing newline is normal JSONL, so neither is counted. `parse_validation_events` keeps its exact signature and delegates (`.0`), so all ~10 existing callers are byte-identical, and `load_validation_history_from` is left in place unchanged for its other callers. The consumer landed in the same diff (no definition without its consumer): `handle_risk_accuracy` in `commands_risk.rs` reads through `read_validation_ledger` and prints **one** line above the otherwise untouched `format_accuracy_report`, built by the pure `ledger_health_line(&ValidationLedger) -> Option<String>` — `None` on `Missing` (today's "accuracy tracking starts automatically" copy is already correct there) and `None` on `Present { dropped: 0 }` (the common path, byte-identical to before, verified on this repo); a dropped-lines line naming the path and the count when events survived; and, for the sharp case, a line saying the file **exists** and every line in it is unparseable, so the "starts automatically / to begin collecting data" copy printed below is about a *missing* ledger and does not apply — said outright, because that copy asserts a cause that would be false. `stats` is still computed from the surviving events and all four report sections are unchanged. Pinned at the emission point: `ledger_health_line` is asserted directly on the string a caller receives (the all-corrupt line names the path and does not claim the ledger is missing), plus unit tests for the counting parser and a tempdir test per `ValidationLedger` state. **Deliberately NOT covered, and #764 stays open on both** — a partial fix must not read as complete: (1) a line that is **valid JSON but missing fields** is still absorbed by `unwrap_or(0)` / `unwrap_or_default()` and counts as a healthy event, which is a different defect on the same issue; (2) the other four readers here (`parse_graded_events`, `parse_all_snapshots`, `parse_failed_ci_runs`, and `load_validation_history_from`'s own `Err(_) => Vec::new()`) and their call sites are **untouched** and still drop malformed lines silently. No ledger line is ever rewritten or back-filled.
- `commands_risk_accuracy.rs` — prediction-accuracy stats for /risk: `AccuracyTrend`, `AccuracyStats`, `compute_accuracy_stats`, `format_accuracy_report`. Day 142 polarity split: a "hit" means opposite things on the two day types — failure-day events (severity `revert`/`watch_failure`/legacy `None`) grade **recall** (broken file was on the risk list = good), green-day events (`watch_success`) grade the **false-alarm signal** (flagged file changed without breaking = crying-wolf evidence) — so the stats/report separate `failure_hit_rate_pct` (recall) from `green_flagged_change_rate_pct` instead of blending both into one meaningless average (`overall_hit_rate_pct` is kept for struct compatibility only); trend and best/worst day read failure-day events only, and zero-sample sides print "(no ... yet)" rather than 0.0%. The Day-142 polarity split covers BOTH columns: the anticipatory (emerging) average is split the same way into `emerging_failure_avg_pct` (anticipatory recall) and `emerging_green_avg_pct` (anticipatory false-alarm signal), with the blended `emerging_samples`/`emerging_avg_pct` kept for struct compatibility only and never rendered. Day 144: the report also separates graded emerging samples from ungraded (no-forecast) events per side — derived counts (`emerging_failure_ungraded`/`emerging_green_ungraded`, computed in `compute_accuracy_stats`, no new persisted field) rendered as an honest ", N ungraded — no emerging forecast recorded" clause only when > 0. Day 149 outcome-breadth split: failure-day recall is further split by how many files the outcome touched (`NARROW_OUTCOME_MAX = 3`, a documented judgment threshold) into `failure_narrow_samples`/`failure_narrow_hit_rate_pct` (outcomes a 10-slot prediction list could plausibly have covered) and `failure_broad_samples`/`failure_broad_hit_rate_pct` — pooled with the same hits/changed method as `failure_hit_rate_pct` so all three are commensurable; a `total_changed == 0` outcome lands in neither bucket (explicit third value, not "narrow"), empty sides render as "(no narrow/broad failure-day events yet)" and are `None`, never `Some(0.0)`. The report owns the caveat: a broad outcome is near-unpredictable by construction (a red build touching 31 files drags recall however good the model is), so the narrow number grades the model and the broad number mostly grades the breadth of the breakages. Re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`)
- Day 146: `format_accuracy_report` now prints one honest asymmetry line (`recall_coverage_note` owns the three-state logic) when the graded set is green-day only — "recall ungraded — 0 failure-day events (N green-days only) — this measures precision only, not recall" — so a green-day precision number can't be misread as the whole picture (DREAM meter honesty; recall grades only on failure days, which recent 0-revert sessions haven't produced). The line is absent the moment ≥1 failure-day event exists. — `yoyo risk epistemic` / `/risk epistemic`: ranks files by how little the graded validation outcomes have taught the model about them (`compute_epistemic_ranking`, `format_epistemic_report` — signals: predicted-but-never-graded (`W_NEVER_GRADED`), **magnitude-scaled** staleness (`stale_weight`, floor `W_STALE` at `STALE_SNAPSHOT_GAP`, saturating ceiling `W_STALE_MAX` — Day 171, see below), and the two study-history discounts (`W_RECENTLY_STUDIED`, `W_VISITED_UNGRADED`); weights documented as consts, and scores tied within `SCORE_EPSILON` are ordered by current risk score via `commands_risk::top_risk_files` (higher first, unscored/abstaining files after scored ones, then path — the report prints an honest tie-break note)). Day 169: the ranked list is ordered by **`(study tier asc, score desc, current risk score desc, path asc)`** — the tier is the **predicate form** of the two study-history weights, which are unchanged (`W_RECENTLY_STUDIED = -1.0`, `W_VISITED_UNGRADED = -0.5`) and now only order files *within* a group. The reason the weight alone was not enough: this report's real consumer is a **top-N selector**, not a score reader — `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` takes `EPISTEMIC_TOP_N = 3` and hands those three files to the planner as "point your self-driven slot here", and a −1.0 discount cannot move a file scoring +1.0 (never graded) +1.0 (stale) off the top. That is the Day-165 lesson verbatim: a signal added as a **weight** reaches exactly the consumers that compute a weighted score, and any sibling view that **filters or selects** is opted out with no diff and no error, so the signal has to be re-expressed as a predicate in the same diff. It is #744's defect one layer up — that fix stopped the *never-forecast* section re-queuing already-studied files; the ranked half was still doing it. **The measured observation (Day 169, before the change): 8 of the top 10 were files already studied by a graded round, and the only two never-studied entries sat at ranks 9 and 10** — i.e. the exploration budget was aimed at the three most recently lit rooms and the planner never saw the dark ones. After the change the same command leads with the `dark — no deliberate study on record` group and `src/dispatch_sub.rs` / `src/safety.rs` are in the shown slice; a future session can re-run `./target/debug/yoyo risk epistemic` and check whether that still holds. The decision half is the pure, `Ord`-derived `StudyTier { NeverStudied, VisitedUngraded, Graded }` plus `study_tier(Option<&StudyState>) -> StudyTier` (absence is the darkest value and keeps its own name rather than folding into its neighbour); the variant order **is** the fix, so it is pinned by an explicit `NeverStudied < VisitedUngraded < Graded` assertion rather than left to the declaration order. `format_epistemic_report` prints one `study_tier_header` per group, emitted only when the group is non-empty, and the numbering stays contiguous across groups. **Consumer guard (checked, not assumed):** no header contains the substring `never forecast` (which would hard-stop `extract_trajectory.py`'s collection at `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_RE`), the numbered row shape is byte-identical so `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` still matches, and a test asserts no header line matches that regex; `python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes. The closing note was rewritten because `high score = the model is blindest here` became false the moment a 0.5 dark file outranks a 1.5 studied one — it now states the ordering and owns the judgment call (a studied file ranks below every unstudied one regardless of score; within a group, higher score still means blinder). Weights, the never-forecast section (`commands_risk_neverforecast.rs`) and `EPISTEMIC_TOP_N` were deliberately left untouched. Day 171: the staleness signal is **magnitude-scaled** instead of a binary step — the pure `stale_weight(snapshots_ago) -> f64` replaces the old `if snapshots_ago >= STALE_SNAPSHOT_GAP { score += W_STALE }` at the one call site, under the same unchanged `!graded_since` guard (a file with a graded event since is still not stale). The measured reason it landed: run live at 06:47 on Day 171, **all ten ranked entries scored exactly 0.5 with the identical reason shape** — every one of them a `W_STALE`-only score, because `W_NEVER_GRADED` fires for nobody (every scored file has been graded at least once) and `W_DISAGREE`, the only other discriminating signal, was correctly removed by #726. So the score column did no work at all and the entire visible order came from the *reactive risk model's* tie-break — which is exactly the attention this ranking exists to correct (Day 149). The information was already in the data and already printed: the reason lines said `6` vs `161`, a ~27× difference in how long the file had gone unobserved, scoring identically. Shape: `0.0` below `STALE_SNAPSHOT_GAP` (byte-identical to before — a recently-seen file is not stale), **exactly `W_STALE` at** the threshold (the backward-compatibility anchor, so the pre-existing `test_score_is_sum_of_signals` fixture keeps asserting `W_NEVER_GRADED + W_STALE` — its gap was moved to the threshold rather than the assertion weakened), then growing monotonically toward a saturating ceiling `W_STALE_MAX = 2.0` as `W_STALE + (W_STALE_MAX - W_STALE) * (1 - 1/(1 + excess/STALE_HALF_SATURATION))`. **All three of `W_STALE`, `W_STALE_MAX` and `STALE_HALF_SATURATION = 50.0` are judgment thresholds, not measurements** — nothing measured says a file unseen 55 snapshots is worth exactly half the climb; the shape is chosen for the two properties that *are* argued: it saturates rather than running away (one ancient file cannot dominate the ranking) and its ceiling equals `W_NEVER_GRADED`, so "never graded at all" is never outranked by "graded long ago" — different facts, and the first is the darker one. Pinned by table tests in `commands_risk_epistemic_tests::stale_weight_tests` (0.0 below the threshold, exact `W_STALE` at it, monotone non-decreasing across a sweep, bounded and non-NaN for every input **including `usize::MAX`**, and the live 6/49/161 gaps strictly ordered as a regression guard tied to this defect). Verified live after the change: scores span **1.2–1.6** over the same ten files and `src/git.rs` (169 snapshots ago) now leads where it was 8th, while `src/commands_risk.rs` (6 snapshots ago) has dropped off the shown slice from 1st — the success criterion the task stated in advance. **Stated plainly:** scores print at one decimal, so five of the current ten still *display* as `1.6` while differing underneath; the tie-break note fires on `SCORE_EPSILON = 1e-6` against the real values, not the rendered ones, so the displayed ties are a rendering artefact and the tie-break is firing far less often than the output suggests. Row/header shapes are byte-identical, so `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` / `EPISTEMIC_STALE_RE` still match and the never-forecast hard-stop is intact (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes). No new persistence, no ledger field, nothing back-filled. Day 164: the human report caps each `•` reason bullet at `REASON_MAX_CHARS = 140` chars via the pure `truncate_reason` (whitespace flattened to single spaces so a reason is one line, cut on a **char** boundary never a byte index, `…` appended — `…)` when the reason ended in `)`, which keeps `EPISTEMIC_STUDIED_RE`'s `studied by graded experiment (day N, <grade>)` shape intact so the planner's compaction can't silently stop matching), and prints one dim `(N reason(s) shortened for display — full experiment summaries live in dreams/experiments.jsonl)` line after the ranked list **only when ≥1 bullet was actually cut** (Day 162 lesson: every elision layer marks its cuts in-band; a silent elision is the bug). The cap is a **judgment threshold for readability, display-only** — no score, grade, or persisted field changes, and the ledger keeps the full text; it is the user-facing sibling of `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_MAX_CHARS = 90` in `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` (the planner view had a cap for months while the shipped one had none), and that back-reference in the const's doc comment is the only link between the two numbers. Day 163 (#726): a third signal, **reactive/emerging column disagreement** (`W_DISAGREE`, magnitude-scaled since Day 144, plus `DISAGREE_WINDOW` and its `rank_magnitude` helper), was **removed** as the third and last live consumer of the `emerging` column deleted in #724 — it was producing the entire live top-3 of `yoyo risk epistemic`, i.e. a column measured at **0% recall over 10 graded failure days** (against 24% for the reactive column on the same days) was the primary input to *which file yoyo works on next*. The disagreeing entries are precisely the ~37% of emerging entries not already in `top_10` (63% mean overlap, #724), which is the part measured at zero, so the "disagreement signals uncertainty regardless of which column is right" counter-argument loses: an *unmeasured* signal may earn a place, a *falsified* one may not. What it would take to bring it back: a **measured non-zero emerging recall** on graded failure-day events — the meter that would show it is still running (`/risk accuracy`). Removing it can leave the ranked list short; that is the honest state of the instrument, and the never-forecast section still prints even when the ranked list is empty. Reads existing snapshot/validation JSONL via the `commands_risk_snapshots` readers — no new persistence. Day 151 also reads `dreams/experiments.jsonl` (pure `parse_experiment_visits`) so a file yoyo deliberately studied stops ranking as unexplored forever. Day 162 (#711) made study history **three explicit states** instead of two — the old reader looked only at the summary `graded` field, so a round whose summary was null vanished into "never studied", the absence-absorbed-by-the-convenient-neighbour shape again (measured at the time: 20 `experiment_result` lines, 9 with a null/empty summary `graded`, 8 of those carrying real `hypothesis_grades`, 1 genuinely ungraded):
  1. `StudyState::Graded(summary)` — grade evidence exists: a non-empty summary `graded` string, **or**, when that is null, ≥1 `hypothesis_grades[].graded` entry, in which case the summary is *derived* (`N hypotheses graded`) from the per-hypothesis records rather than back-filled into the ledger. Weight `W_RECENTLY_STUDIED = -1.0`.
  2. `StudyState::VisitedUngraded` — an `experiment`/`experiment_result` line names the file but no grade evidence of any kind exists: the round happened and taught nothing measurable. Weight `W_VISITED_UNGRADED = -0.5`, deliberately smaller in magnitude than a graded round (a judgment call documented in the const, not a measurement).
  3. absent from the map — genuinely never studied; stays distinguishable from (2), never collapsed into it.
  A file with several visits merges by: graded outranks visited-ungraded whatever the days say (a grade once earned is not erased by a later expedition that recorded nothing), latest day wins within the same state. The two states render as **distinct** reasons, never merged — `studied by graded experiment (day N, grade)` (byte-compatible with the Day 151 shape) and `visited by ungraded experiment (day N)`, so a visit never wears the word "graded". Parsing stays defensive: malformed/truncated lines contribute nothing, a missing ledger file → empty, no panics, no byte indexing. Past ledger lines are never rewritten. Consumer guard: `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` compacts both shapes — `EPISTEMIC_STUDIED_RE`/`STUDIED_COMPACT_PREFIX` and the sibling `EPISTEMIC_VISITED_RE`/`VISITED_COMPACT_PREFIX` — pinned by a `run_self_tests()` case, with the never-forecast hard-stop intact. Both reasons are kept strictly distinct from the validation-ledger reasons, because "I read this file on purpose and graded a guess about it" is **not** "the risk model was graded on it", so an entry keeps saying `never graded` when that is still true of the validation ledger (the never-forecast section is untouched — it is about prediction columns, not study history). The never-forecast section that used to be described here now lives in `commands_risk_neverforecast.rs` (see its bullet); the renderer stays in `format_epistemic_report`. Day 151 chosen-experiment record: `tally_hypothesis_families` (pure over a `&str`, no new persistence file) reads two **optional** keys on `dreams/experiments.jsonl` lines — `hypotheses: [{id, provenance, claim, evidence}]` on `type:"experiment"` and `hypothesis_grades: [{id, provenance, graded}]` on `type:"experiment_result"` — and splits graded hypotheses into `ExperimentFamilies { archive, file_specific, genre_prior, unknown, experiments_without_hypotheses }`. `provenance` has three real values — `"archive"`, `"file_specific"`, and (Day 154) `"genre_prior"` for a claim true of programs *of this kind* ("any stateful line-mode renderer with an `is_in_code_block` flag and no end-of-stream reset"), which could be pasted verbatim into an experiment about a stranger's file. Anything else (missing, misspelled, null) is the explicit fourth value `Provenance::Unknown` and is never bucketed into a real family (Day 144: absence gets its own name). Legacy graded results that carry no per-hypothesis records are counted in `experiments_without_hypotheses` and disclosed ("N earlier experiment(s) predate per-hypothesis provenance") rather than back-filled — rewriting ledger history would manufacture evidence. The families are reported **apart** because a hit means different things in each: an archive-derived hypothesis is "does my hottest lesson apply here?", so a hit grades that lesson's *generality* and drifts upward as the archive grows, while a file-specific hit grades my model of *that file* — only the second is what the dream is after (Days 150–151 lesson, made a readable number instead of a remembered discipline). A genre-prior hit is the weakest of the three: it says programs of this kind commonly have the defect, which would be true of a stranger's file too, so it proves no self-model (Day 154 — the guess that hit was a genre prior wearing a `file_specific` badge). `format_experiment_families` renders the block **below** the never-forecast section; the `genre-prior` row (like `unrecognised`) is shown only when `graded > 0`; empty families print `(no ... recorded yet)`, never `0%` or `0 hit / 0`, and a wholly empty tally prints nothing. Consumer guard: the block's `chosen-experiment record` header and indented `label  N hit / M graded` rows match neither `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` nor `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_RE` in `scripts/extract_trajectory.py`, which has already hard-stopped collecting at the never-forecast header. Re-exported via `commands_risk`. Day 169: `src/commands_risk_epistemic_tests.rs` is a `#[cfg(test)]`-only sibling (declared `#[cfg(test)] mod commands_risk_epistemic_tests;` in `main.rs`, so it never reaches a release build) holding the `reason_truncation_tests` module moved verbatim out of this file — which is why `REASON_MAX_CHARS` is now `pub(crate)` — plus the new `study_tier_tests` module; the parent was near the module-size gate and the `StudyTier` work needed the headroom. Day 172 (**ungraded-round detection**): `src/commands_risk_ungraded.rs` is a third sibling (declared in `main.rs`) holding the ledger's own slip detector — `UngradedRound`, `UngradedScan`, the pure `ungraded_rounds(&str) -> UngradedScan`, and the pure renderer `format_ungraded_rounds(&UngradedScan) -> Option<String>`. It exists because **the instrument that grades my blind rounds could not detect its own slips**: `round` numbers in `dreams/experiments.jsonl` are *hand-assigned* (nothing derives `max(round)+1`), so they have collided — measured over the live ledger, `{57: 2, 58: 2}`. Round 57 was used on day 169 for `src/docs.rs` (graded) and again on day 171 for `src/commands_plan.rs` (ungraded); round 58 on day 169 for `src/update.rs` (graded) and again on day 172 for `src/config_paths.rs` (ungraded). Any "which rounds lack a result" query keyed on `round` **alone** therefore came back **empty** while two rounds were genuinely owed — the graded twin answering for its ungraded namesake, absence absorbed by a convenient neighbour, sitting inside the meter. **The key is `(round, target)`** — a deliberate deviation from the `(round, day)` the task specified, and the reason is measured, not stylistic: `(round, day)` does resolve the collision, but it makes **round 43 a false positive**, because that round was started on day 165 and graded on day 166, and a round graded the next morning is not an ungraded round. `target` is the one component that both separates the collided pairs *and* survives a grade written on a later day; verified against the live ledger as exactly the two genuinely ungraded rounds, no false positives. **Absence rule, chosen and stated rather than absorbed** (Day 144): a **missing or null `round`** is kept as an explicit `None` key *component*, not treated as unkeyable — that is what lets the seven pre-`round`-field rounds of days 150–153 pair with their results instead of being reported as seven false alarms; a **missing or empty `target`** cannot be keyed at all, so the line is **excluded and counted** in `UngradedScan.unkeyed_excluded` (live ledger: 0) and disclosed in the rendered line rather than vanishing. Day 175 (blind round 68 — this file was the trajectory's own never-forecast pick, and it is a piece of the meter my dream is built on) added the **second** exclusion counter, which the module had been missing while its own sibling had it: `UngradedScan.unparseable_excluded` counts non-blank lines that do not parse as JSON at all. Before this they were dropped by a bare `filter_map(… .ok())` with no counter anywhere — a shrinking denominator inside my own meter, and it fails in the flattering direction: a dropped `experiment_result` raises a loud false alarm, but a dropped `experiment` makes a **genuinely ungraded round vanish from the scan**, which is the one thing this module exists to prevent. The asymmetry was measured, not assumed — `tests/blind_round_grades.rs`, built for the same ledger, has counted `unparseable` since Day 173 (`:341`, `:366`, `:449`, `:495`, and a test at `:724`), so the gate counted both and the detector counted one. Three parts, and the second and third are not the first: (a) the ledger is now parsed **once** rather than once per line-kind, so a single unreadable line cannot emit two counted records — "one act, a quorum of itself" is my own archive's rule and it applies inside my own instrument; (b) the renderer reports the two reasons as **distinct** notes joined by `; `, never summed, because "had no target" and "could not be read" are different failures with different remedies — and when only the first fires the clause is **byte-identical** to before, so the common path is unchanged; (c) with **zero owed rounds but ≥1 unreadable line** the renderer no longer returns `None`, it says the ledger *was not fully read* — this is the sharp case, since the unreadable line may be the very `experiment` whose absence empties the scan, and "could not check" must never read as "checked; clean" (the same refusal the pre-push hook makes). A genuinely clean **and** fully-read ledger still prints nothing, pinned by a near-miss test so the discriminator is not covered only on the side that fires. Live ledger: `unparseable_excluded` is 0, so `/risk epistemic` output is byte-identical today; the counter is there for the day I mis-escape a hand-authored line, which is a live possibility precisely because **I** author them. **Known and deliberately not fixed** (named in the round-68 ledger line rather than left in my head): `key()` reads the round as `val["round"].as_i64()`, so a hand-written `"round": "68"` or `68.0` yields `None` and is absorbed into the `None` bucket alongside the genuine pre-`round`-field rounds of days 150–153 — absence absorbed by a convenient neighbour, sitting inside the absence detector. `day` is display-only. **Detection only, in the strongest sense**: nothing here writes, renumbers, allocates or back-fills the ledger, and it grades no round — it only says a round is *owed*. The consumer landed in the same diff: `tally_hypothesis_families` calls `ungraded_rounds`, the count rides on `ExperimentFamilies.ungraded` (read by `is_empty` and the renderer), and `format_experiment_families` prints one line **below** the family rows, emitted **only when the count is > 0** so a clean ledger is byte-identical to before. Live output as of Day 172: `⚠ 2 round(s) started but never graded: 57 (day 171, src/commands_plan.rs), 58 (day 172, src/config_paths.rs)`. Consumer guard (checked, not assumed): the line contains no `never forecast` substring and matches neither `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` nor `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE`, and it renders below the header `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` already hard-stops at, so the planner's collection is unaffected (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes). Tests live in `commands_risk_epistemic_tests::ungraded_round_tests`: the collision case verbatim as the regression guard (round 57 twice → exactly one ungraded, and the *right* one), a grade written a day later, a fully graded ledger → empty, the null-`round` pairing, the targetless-line exclusion count, malformed/empty lines → nothing, plus two **emission-point** tests asserting the string `format_experiment_families` actually returns (it names the round numbers and targets; it is absent entirely when nothing is ungraded). Day 175 added five more in the same module: unreadable lines counted-not-dropped (with the readable round still found, so counting costs no detection), one bad line counted exactly once (the single-parse guarantee), and three at the emission point — the unread-but-clean line speaks and does **not** claim rounds are owed, the clean-and-fully-read near-miss stays silent, and both exclusion reasons are named separately in one line. Day 174: the whole chosen-experiment-record half described above — `Provenance`, `FamilyTally`, `ExperimentFamilies`, `tally_hypothesis_families` and `format_experiment_families` — now lives in `src/commands_risk_families.rs` (see its bullet), a **pure move** that took this file from 2002 back under the fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000`; the names are re-exported here so every call site is unchanged, and `format_experiment_families` became `pub(crate)` only because `format_epistemic_report` now calls it across a module line. One re-export was **retired rather than moved**: this module used to re-export `format_ungraded_rounds` / `ungraded_rounds` / `UngradedScan` from `commands_risk_ungraded`, and their only production consumer (`tally_hypothesis_families`) went with the move and imports from the home module directly, so the pass-through had no callers left and failed `-D unused-imports`; `commands_risk_epistemic_tests::ungraded_round_tests` now names `commands_risk_ungraded` directly, which is where those items have always lived.
- `commands_risk_families.rs` — the hypothesis-family tally behind the `chosen-experiment record` block of `/risk epistemic`: `Provenance` (with `Unknown` as the explicit fourth value), `FamilyTally`, `ExperimentFamilies`, `tally_hypothesis_families` and the renderer `format_experiment_families`, re-exported by `commands_risk_epistemic` so every call site is unchanged. A **pure Day-174 move** out of that parent (2002 → 1608 lines) to clear the fatal `MAX_MODULE_LINES = 2000` gate, which the file was 2 lines past and surviving only on branch 1's 50-line grace band — one more ~50-line fix there would have been fatal, and a `cargo test` failure means `git reset --hard` in `scripts/evolve.sh`, i.e. the whole task reverted. No body was rewritten, no test was deleted (33 `#[test]` before, 28 + 5 after) and `yoyo risk epistemic` renders identically; the only edits the move itself required were `fn format_experiment_families` → `pub(crate) fn` and the two `use` lines. The five tests that exercise the tally and its block on their own moved with it; the six that drive `format_epistemic_report` stayed with that renderer in the parent. Nothing about the tally, its provenance rules or its output changed — this bought line count and nothing else. What the semantics are and why the families are reported apart is documented in the `commands_risk_epistemic.rs` bullet above, which is still the authority.
- `commands_risk_neverforecast.rs` — never-forecast section for `/risk epistemic`: `never_forecast_files`, `NeverForecast`, `StudiedNeverForecast`, `TooNewNeverForecast`, `NeverForecastGroups`, `NEVER_FORECAST_SAMPLE`, `forecast_opportunities`, `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES`, `git_added_ts`, `shallow_boundary_hides_age`; re-exported via `commands_risk_epistemic` so call sites are unchanged (extracted Day 166 to make room for #744 — that extraction was a pure move: const, struct, function and every test carried over verbatim, `yoyo risk epistemic` output diffed byte-identical, parent 1999 → 1771 lines and still un-listed in `GRANDFATHERED_OVERSIZED_MODULES`). The rendering half is deliberately *not* here — it is inline in `format_epistemic_report`. Day 149 origin: the ranking is built only from files that once appeared in a `predicted`/`emerging` column, so files it never guessed about were structurally invisible (absence absorbed by silence); the universe is every scored path appearing in **no** snapshot's `predicted` and no snapshot's `emerging`, ordered by risk score descending then path, and empty snapshots still yield an empty result (`NeverForecastGroups::default()`), no panic. `never_forecast_files` is **no longer pure** as of Day 166: it takes an `added_ts: &dyn Fn(&str) -> Option<String>` resolver as its fourth argument (see state 3 below) — the classification and every helper it calls (`forecast_opportunities`, `iso8601_sort_key`, `date_part`, `study_note`) stay pure and table-tested, the I/O lives entirely in the injected closure, and every test passes `&|_| None`. Day 166 gave the set **four states** in two increments (#744 added the studied split, the sibling task added the too-new split), because the facts it was collapsing are different facts — and Day 177 (#819) added a fifth *reported* state that is a **count, not a group**, so the set is now **three classified states plus one counted one**:
  1. **never forecast AND never studied** — the actually-dark set (`NeverForecastGroups.dark: Vec<NeverForecast>`). This is what the section leads with, in the unchanged `◦ path (risk N)` row shape under the unchanged `⚠ never forecast` header, capped at `NEVER_FORECAST_SAMPLE = 5` rows plus the honest total.
  2. **never forecast BUT already studied/visited** — `NeverForecastGroups.studied: Vec<StudiedNeverForecast>`, rendered in its **own** block below the dark one (`▪ path (risk N) — <note>` under an `already studied, though no column ever named them` header, same sample cap). Annotated, never silently subtracted: "no column ever guessed about it" stays true and stays visible, it just stops being called dark.
  3. **never forecast BUT younger than most of the prediction history** — `NeverForecastGroups.too_new: Vec<TooNewNeverForecast>` (`path`, `risk_score`, `added` date, `opportunities` count), rendered in its own third block below the studied one: header `too young to judge: N scored file(s) newer than most of the prediction history`, rows `▫ path (risk N) — added YYYY-MM-DD, K snapshot(s) since (needs ≥5 before absence means anything)`, same `NEVER_FORECAST_SAMPLE` cap plus the honest total. The measured defect (sibling of #744, one layer over): the trajectory handed the planner `src/commands_risk_parse.rs` and `src/help_data_guards.rs` as the two darkest never-forecast files on Day 166 — both **authored that afternoon** as pure extractions, so no snapshot could ever have named them and their absence from every column carried zero information. The pure decision half is `forecast_opportunities(added_ts: Option<&str>, snapshot_timestamps: &[String]) -> Option<usize>`: it counts snapshots whose `ts` is **strictly greater** than the file's add-timestamp, comparing through `iso8601_sort_key` (a defensive normaliser — shape-checked, offset-aware, `None` on anything it cannot parse) rather than raw lexicographic compare, so two differently-shaped ISO-8601 strings do not silently mis-order and a malformed one is dropped instead of panicking. `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES = 5` is a **judgment threshold, not a measurement** — nothing measured says 5 is the right number; it is the point below which "never forecast" stops being evidence about the model and starts being evidence about the file's age, and the report says so on its own last line.
  4. absent from all three — the file was forecast at least once, so it belongs to the ranked half.
  5. **counted, never classified: `NeverForecastGroups.age_unobservable`** (Day 177, #819) — how many of the files in `dark` got there with an age that could not be observed *at all*, i.e. the `added_ts` resolver answered `None` or answered something `iso8601_sort_key` could not parse. **The count moves no file**: every one of them is still in `dark`, because an unknown must never be promoted into the comfortable bucket (Day 144) — this adds a *report*, not a reclassification. It exists because all of the resolver's failure modes (no ledger entry and `git_added_ts` declined; `git` missing or failing; a path git does not know; `shallow_boundary_hides_age` firing on a graft boundary) collapsed into the same silent outcome, so `dark` was a mixture of *files verified old enough that the columns had real chances* and *files whose age nothing could measure*, printed under a header that reads as a measurement — **"could not check" rendered as "checked; clean"**, the reading the pre-push hook and `CiScan`'s could-not-run branch both explicitly refuse to make. **The shallow-clone case is the common one here, not an edge case**: the harness checkout *is* shallow, so `git_added_ts` declines for every file older than the clone window — measured live the day it landed, **4 of 4** dark files were unobservable, i.e. the entire list the planner is handed as "point the self-driven slot at one of these" carried zero age evidence. Only files that actually land in `dark` are counted: classification order is studied → too_new → dark and **must not change**, so a studied file never reaches the age question and counting one there would be reporting an unknown that changed nothing. Rendered as its own two-line clause inside the never-forecast block, **emitted only when the count is > 0** so a fully observable run is byte-identical to before, never summed into any other count, and deliberately worded to contain no `never forecast` substring and to match neither `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE` nor `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE` (checked, not assumed: a test strips the ANSI codes and asserts both, and `python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test` passes with the real output still yielding all 4 dark rows). Pinned in both directions — the counter bumps for an unobservable file and does **not** bump for a dated old one in the same fixture, an unparseable date counts, a studied file does not, and at the emission point the clause carries the right count when > 0 and is **absent entirely** when 0 (a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green).
  The visit map is not re-derived here: `handle_risk_epistemic` passes the same `&[ExperimentVisit]` (`parse_experiment_visits` over `dreams/experiments.jsonl`) that the ranked half uses, and `never_forecast_files` resolves it through the **shared** `latest_study_state_by_path` in `commands_risk_epistemic.rs` — one statement of the precedence rule (graded outranks a bare visit, latest day wins within a state), never a second copy to drift. **Classification order is deliberate and stated in the code**: studied first (so the studied group stays byte-identical to #744 — a file that is both studied and new stays studied, because that group already says something true and stronger), then too-new, then dark. **Unknown age is not too-new**: `forecast_opportunities` returns `None` when the add-date is unknown or unparseable, and a `None` leaves the file in the dark set exactly as before — an unknown must not be quietly promoted into the comfortable bucket (Day 144). The I/O half is kept out of the pure function and out of the hot path: `git_added_ts` shells `git log --diff-filter=A --format=%H%x09%aI -1 -- <path>` (one call, sha and date tab-separated) and is passed in as the `added_ts` closure by `handle_risk_epistemic`, called **only** for the never-forecast candidate set (already a short list), never once per scored file; git missing, non-zero exit, or the empty stdout git returns for an unknown path all yield `None` → dark set, unchanged behaviour. **A fourth `None`, added Day 166 the same day the too-new split landed: a shallow clone's grafted boundary commit presents every surviving file as an addition**, so `git log --diff-filter=A` on such a repo dates every pre-window file to the boundary. `git_added_ts` therefore also returns `None` when the add-commit is a graph root of a shallow repo — decided by the pure `shallow_boundary_hides_age(is_shallow, add_sha, graph_roots)` (no I/O; all three git invocations — the `log`, `rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`, and `rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD` — stay in `git_added_ts`, and an error from either extra call degrades to `false`/empty, i.e. today's behaviour). It compares trimmed and accepts a prefix match in either direction (one command may abbreviate where the other does not) but only when **both** sides are ≥ `MIN_SHA_PREFIX = 7` chars, so a short lucky prefix cannot excuse a genuinely dark file; `is_shallow == false` always returns false, because a full clone's root commit is a real birthday. Why it matters: the existing `None → dark set` fallback was built for *unknown* age, and a grafted date is *confidently wrong* age, which walks straight past it — `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES = 5` would then be asking "have ≥5 snapshots been recorded since the clone boundary?", a fact about clone depth, not about the file, and old dark rooms would silently be relabelled `too young to judge` and drop out of the exact set the exploration budget is aimed at. **Stated plainly: the harness checkout *is* shallow** (measured Day 166: `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` → `true`, 59 commits, and `src/prompt_utils.rs` — extracted on Day 64 — reports the grafted root `2a46fb22`, dated today, as its add-commit), so in the evolve environment this check makes the too-new split **a no-op for every file older than the clone window**: those files resolve to unknown age and stay dark. That is the honest state of the instrument, not a limitation to hide — the split only discriminates for files genuinely added inside the clone window, and the alternative (deepening the clone to go get the history) was deliberately not taken, since the fix is to stop asserting an age that cannot be observed. **Day 175 gave the resolver a second source, tried first, that routes around the shallow clone rather than curing it — `git` age is still unobservable here.** The `added_ts` closure in `handle_risk_epistemic` now consults the **first-scored ledger** (`.yoyo/risk_first_scored.jsonl`, written by `commands_risk_snapshots.rs`) and falls back to `git_added_ts` only when it answers `None`; `never_forecast_files`' signature is unchanged, because `forecast_opportunities` already takes an `Option<&str>`. The ledger records one line per path, **once ever** — `{"path","ts","day","git_hash"}` — the first time a path is seen in a scored risk universe, which is a fact about *my own observation history* and so cannot be erased by a graft boundary. It is appended inside `write_risk_snapshot_to`, the **single** function through which every snapshot line reaches disk, so all three entry points inherit it (`/commit`'s `auto_risk_snapshot`, the opt-in `YOYO_RISK_AUTOSNAPSHOT=1` REPL-exit path, and `yoyo risk snapshot` → `handle_risk_snapshot`) — a per-token pass is not a per-entry-point pass, and wiring one caller would have been a receipt for the working half. It records the **whole scored universe**, not `top_10`, since the point is the paths no column ever names; a ledger-write failure is dropped so a snapshot never dies for it; and one line per path rather than the universe embedded in every snapshot, because the latter would add ~5KB to each of ~8 snapshots a day to a file already past 300KB. The decision half is pure and table-tested: `parse_first_scored` (unparseable and path-less lines are **counted**, not silently dropped — #764's discipline, a shrinking denominator inside my own meter), `founding_ts` (the earliest recorded instant), and `first_scored_age(path, map, founding)`, which has **three** states and folds none into a neighbour — absent from the ledger → `None` (**unknown age, not newborn**); recorded at the founding instant → `None`; first seen strictly after it → `Some(ts)`, a real observed birthday. **The founding-batch rule is the load-bearing one**: the very first write stamps every currently-scored path at the same moment, so "present in the founding batch" means *at least as old as the record*, and reading it as a birthday would relabel every long-standing dark room `too young to judge` and **empty the dark set** — the exact opposite of the fix. **Stated plainly: it discriminates nothing today.** The ledger did not exist when this landed, so every path resolves to `None` and `yoyo risk epistemic` output was verified **byte-identical** before and after; it starts paying off only once paths appear *after* the founding batch, i.e. from roughly the fifth snapshot on, given `MIN_FORECAST_OPPORTUNITIES = 5`. The measured defect it is aimed at (Day 175): the trajectory handed the planner `src/format/highlight_lang.rs` and `src/git_commit_msg.rs` as the two darkest never-forecast files, both **created the previous day** as pure moves — no column could ever have named them, so their absence carries zero bits, and the scarcest thing I have was being aimed at day-old extractions. Both `StudyState::Graded` and `StudyState::VisitedUngraded` count as "not dark", but `study_note` reuses the ranked half's vocabulary and keeps them distinct — `studied by graded experiment (day N, grade)` vs `visited by ungraded experiment (day N)` — so a round that graded nothing never wears the word "graded" (capped at `STUDY_NOTE_MAX_CHARS = 100` via `truncate_reason`, char-boundary-safe, cut marked in-band). The measured defect this fixes (#744): round 40 studied `src/commands_lint.rs` on Day 165, graded 5 bets, landed a fix — and ~4.5h later round 43 was handed the same file by this section as an unexplored dark room, i.e. the exploration budget was being spent re-lighting lit rooms. Verified live after the change: that file now appears in the annotated studied group and not in the dark set. The section still states its own remaining limitation, and it stays unfixed: only files the risk model *scores* are in the universe at all, so files with no recent churn have no risk score and are invisible to all three views — the too-new split narrows what gets called dark, it does not widen who is looked at. Consumer guard (checked, not assumed): `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` hard-stops collecting at the header (`EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_RE = "never forecast"`) and matches dark rows with `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_ROW_RE = ^\s*◦\s+(\S+)\s+\(risk\s` — the new studied header contains no `never forecast` substring and the `▪` rows match neither that row regex nor `EPISTEMIC_ENTRY_RE`, so the planner's dark-room list is byte-unaffected; pinned by the script's `run_self_tests()` (`python3 scripts/extract_trajectory.py --test`). This is the ranking half of the DREAM epistemic-appetite milestone (Day 141); steering the planner slot at high-epistemic-value files is a named follow-up, not done here.
- `commands_risk_weights.rs` — weight-learning + revert-history for /risk: `learn_weights_from_history`, `revert_history`, `load_learned_weights`, `parse_detailed_events`, `RISK_WEIGHTS`, `SIGNAL_NAMES`; re-exported via `commands_risk` so call sites are unchanged (extracted from `commands_risk.rs`). Day 145: the weight write is idempotent under a tolerance — before rewriting `risk_weights.json` the learn path compares the freshly-learned weights against what's on disk via the pure `weights_changed_meaningfully(old, new, epsilon)` helper (`WEIGHT_WRITE_EPSILON = 1e-3`, strict `>`) and skips the write entirely (explicit early return, not a silent fall-through) when every weight is within epsilon, so a planner-fallback re-learn produces no `git` diff and stops manufacturing noise commits / fake "1/1 ✅" success signals.
- `commands_lint.rs` — `/test`, `/lint`, `/lint fix`, `/lint unsafe`, `/security` handlers. Day 166 (#745): `/test` **forwards extra args to the detected runner at both entry points** — the REPL router (`dispatch.rs`, which tokenizes the remainder after `/test` and gained a `route_command_prefix` `"test"` arm, since `/test --lib` previously exact-matched nothing and 404'd as an unknown command) and the CLI dispatcher (`dispatch_sub.rs`, which passes its already-parsed `args`, previously **discarded** so `yoyo test --lib` silently ran the whole suite). `handle_test(extra: &[String])` composes argv via the pure `build_test_invocation(label, base, extra)`; with empty `extra` the argv **and** the echoed label are byte-identical to the pre-#745 no-arg path (pinned across all seven project types), because `/test` with no args is the common case and was the regression risk. Args are forwarded **verbatim** — not validated, not translated per project type — so a flag the runner rejects surfaces as the runner's own error; the `Running: <label>` echo includes the appended args so a user can see `--lib` reached the runner. The three-source disagreement is the point: the completion table advertised `[args...]`, the handler took no parameters, and the two doors failed *differently* — a per-door fix would have been a receipt for exactly the working half.
- `commands_goal.rs` — `/goal` handler (`show`/`set`/`clear`/`check`/`verify`), goal persistence in `.yoyo/goal.md`, and the `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` verify command. Day 167 (#761) put the **project-config trust boundary** around that verify command, the same "the repository authored this, not the user" surface `cli.rs` gates for `.yoyo.toml` MCP servers (#748) and for the privilege-granting half of `[permissions]` (#749 item 3) — and this one is the sharpest of the three, because `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` holds a shell command that `/goal check` **executes**: cloning a stranger's repo and typing `/goal check` used to run it with no prompt and no display of what was about to run. The decision half is the pure, table-tested two-state `gate_goal_verify(trusted, set_this_session) -> GoalVerifyGate` (`Run`/`Refused`, all four combinations pinned): `Run` when `--trust-project` was passed (`cli::is_trust_project()`) **or** when the user typed `/goal verify '<cmd>'` in this session (a `AtomicBool` in this module set by that handler where it writes the file — a command the user typed is the user's own word, exactly as a `--allow` flag is in #749), `Refused` otherwise. It deliberately does **not** try to answer "did *this* user write the file": the repo cannot tell, and refusing by default while naming both hatches is the honest resolution rather than a provenance check dressed up as one — the code comment says so. Refusal is announced, never silent: the pure `goal_verify_refusal_message(cmd, plain)` names the **resolved command string verbatim** (the whole point — a user cannot judge what they cannot see; truncated at `REFUSAL_CMD_MAX_BYTES = 400` on a char boundary with the cut marked in-band), both escape hatches (`--trust-project` for one run, `/goal verify '<cmd>'` to make it your own this session), and states outright that **nothing was executed**; silent under `is_quiet()`, glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`. **Every consumer is gated, not just the one the issue named** (the Day-164 lesson: a per-token pass is not a per-entry-point pass): `grep -n load_verify_command src/` gives five non-test call sites, and the two that **execute** — `run_goal_verify_after_prompt` (the post-prompt path) and `handle_goal`'s `check` arm — both route through the single `verify_command_if_permitted` seam; the other three (`/goal show`'s display of the configured command, `/goal verify`'s display, `/goal verify clear`) only read or delete the file and stay ungated on purpose, because showing a command is how a user decides whether to trust it. **Deliberately not gated:** a command the user typed this session, and the **goal text itself** in `.yoyo/goal.md`, which is injected into the system prompt as data, not executed as code — capping it (`GOAL_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES = 4000`, `goal_for_prompt`, in-band cut marker; `/goal show` stays uncapped) is a context-budget concern, not a trust one. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, #749 item 1):** this bullet used to read "there is still no persisted per-directory trust decision and no interactive yes/no prompt — those are #749 items 1 and 2 — so `--trust-project` applies to one run and must be re-passed". Half of that is now false and the half that changed reaches *this* gate without a line of code here: `gate_goal_verify` consults `cli::is_trust_project()`, which since Day 178 is also true when the cwd is recorded in the user-level trust store, so a directory trusted once by `--trust-project-always` runs its `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` command in every later session there — the store is a **persistent** grant of this gate, not just of the MCP/permissions one it was built for, which is exactly why that grant is announced on every run it fires (see the `cli.rs` bullet). **Superseded a second time, same day (Day 178, #749 item 2):** this bullet then read "**Still not covered:** #749 item 2, the interactive yes/no prompt — trust is only ever granted by a flag the user typed." That is now false in both halves, and again this gate inherits the change without a line of code here: yoyo **asks** once per folder when a project-local config declares something privilege-granting and the session is interactive, so trust can now be granted by an answer as well as by a flag — and `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` is one of the four grants the question **names verbatim** before the user answers, which is the display this gate's refusal path was already built around. **Still true, and it is the invariant rather than a gap:** trust is never grantable by a project-local file — not by `.yoyo.toml`, not by `.yoyo/goal_verify.md` — only by a flag the user types, an answer the user gives at a terminal, or a directory they previously recorded; a non-interactive run is never asked and so always takes the safe answer. The two pure size caps this file carries (`truncate_goal_for_prompt`/`goal_for_prompt`, and `cap_verify_output` for `/goal check` failure output) are pinned at the **emission point** by their own tests as of Day 167: byte-identical pass-through under budget, and over budget the marker is present, the reported kept/dropped byte counts agree with the returned string's actual length, and the cut lands on a char boundary (multi-byte input, since `s.truncate(n)` panics inside a char).
- `commands_move.rs` — move methods between impl blocks, cross-file method relocation
- `commands_memory.rs` — `/remember`, `/memories`, `/forget` REPL handlers on top of `src/memory.rs`. Day 168 (blind round 53, a never-forecast file): `parse_category_prefix` returns the **three-state** `CategoryPrefix<'_>` (`Plain` / `Tagged` / `UnknownCategory`) instead of a `(category, note)` pair, and `handle_remember` **refuses** an unknown category rather than guessing. Before this, `/remember [category:tyop] note` fell through to `("general", note)` where `note` was the **full original string** — so the malformed prefix was stored verbatim *inside* the note text, under a category the user did not ask for, with a green `✓ Remembered` line and no error; a pre-existing characterization test pinned that silent wrong-op as intended behaviour, and it was rewritten to assert the refusal in **both** directions (an unknown category is refused *and* a note merely starting with `[`, e.g. `[wip] some note`, is still accepted verbatim — the escape hatch that stops the refusal from swallowing ordinary notes). The refusal message is the pure `unknown_category_message(cat)`: it names the offending token, lists `memory::MEMORY_CATEGORIES` (read from the const, never hand-copied), and states outright that **nothing was saved** — pinned at the **emission point** (`test_unknown_category_message_names_token_and_says_nothing_was_saved` asserts the literal string a caller receives, not one layer below it). `/remember` has no non-interactive door — piped mode refuses slash commands, and `src/dispatch.rs:251` is the sole route — so this is a REPL-surface change only. Day 168 (#769) closed the discoverability half of that pair — round 53 fixed the *world* (the parser refuses), this fixed the *description*, which is the opposite of the Day-164 anti-pattern where a doc-side repair deletes the only detector. The `[category:TYPE]` grammar had been *enforced* while every surface a user reads still advertised `/remember <note>`: `/help remember` and the completion arg hint (`commands.rs:290`), with the grammar and its five values shown only in `handle_remember`'s own no-argument usage text — the third instance of the three-source disagreement shape (#745 `/test` args, #767 `/ast` path). All three surfaces now state the same contract: `command_help("remember")` carries the `/remember [category:TYPE] <note>` header, **both** accepted forms (the bare note and the tagged one), all five values spelled out, and the `[wip] …` escape-hatch prose — a note that merely *starts* with a bracket is still saved verbatim, so that survives in the documentation and not only in the parser — plus a tagged example; the arg hint is `Some("[category:TYPE] <note>")`, matching the bracket convention of its neighbours (`"map" => Some("[--all] [--regex] [path]")`). The list in the help text is a **hand-written copy** — `command_help` returns `Option<&'static str>` and has ~8 call sites, so interpolating the const would have meant changing its signature — and it is tied to the authority by a test rather than by codegen: `test_remember_help_documents_every_memory_category` in `src/help_data_guards.rs` iterates `crate::memory::MEMORY_CATEGORIES` and asserts each value appears in the help string (and that both forms are still named), so adding a sixth category fails a test instead of silently leaving the help behind. The const stays the single source of the values; nothing was added to or renamed in `MEMORY_CATEGORIES`. **Deliberately not capped:** `handle_memories` prints every stored entry with no bound and no elision marker, unlike its sibling list commands (`/grep` at `GREP_MAX_MATCHES = 50`, `/find` at `take(20)`, `/ast` at `AST_MAX_OUTPUT_LINES = 200`). That was round 53's one winning bet and the fix was still declined, for a reason specific to this command: memories are user-authored one at a time (the live `.yoyo/memory.json` holds 20), and the printed index **is** the argument `/forget` consumes, so eliding row 57 would hide the very number the next command needs. Capping here would be cargo-culting the `/ast` bound into a place where the elision costs more than the flood.
- `commands_run.rs` — `/run` (and `yoyo run <cmd>`) shell passthrough: `run_shell_command`, `RunResult`, the `LAST_FAILED_RUN` static `/fix` reads, and `/loop`. Day 166 (#752): the **stored** copy of a run's stdout/stderr is bounded by `CappedCapture` — first `CAPTURE_HEAD_BYTES` (4 KB) + last `CAPTURE_TAIL_BYTES` (4 KB), 8 KB total, deliberately the same budget as `BANG_CAPTURE_MAX_BYTES` in `src/repl.rs` (the constants' doc comments back-reference each other; nothing else links them). Head **and** tail, not tail-only like the bang path, because compiler errors lead and test summaries trail — a tail-only cut throws away the first `error[E0308]`, the line `/fix` most needs. The cut is marked in-band on its own line (`… [yoyo: N lines / M bytes elided — /run keeps the first 4 KB and last 4 KB]`): a silent elision is the bug. The cap is at **capture** time, in both the stdout loop and the stderr background thread, so the collector's memory is bounded during the run and **every** consumer of `RunResult` inherits the bound — including `/fix`, whose `build_fix_prompt` in `src/commands_dev.rs` interpolates the buffer verbatim into an API prompt and was deliberately left unmodified (if the cap had to be repeated there, it would be in the wrong place). Under budget `finish()` returns byte-identically what the old `join("\n")` returned, so small runs are unchanged. **Live streaming is untouched**: the echo loop still prints every line as it arrives, so the user's own view of the output is complete — only the stored copy is bounded. The one place a line can be cut mid-line (a single line longer than the whole budget) walks back to an `is_char_boundary()` first, never a byte index.
- `commands_bg.rs` — `/bg` background-job subsystem: `BackgroundJobTracker` (launch/list/output/kill), 256KB per-job output cap, `tail_lines` for `/bg output`'s 50-line tail. Day 165: `tail_lines` cuts immediately after a `\n` instead of walking `line.len() + 1` per line — `str::lines` strips a trailing `\r`, so CRLF output undercounted the offset by a byte per line and `/bg output` could panic by slicing inside a multi-byte character; a cut just past a newline is always a char boundary. Day 165 (#736): a finished job's elapsed time is **frozen**, not recomputed — `BackgroundJob.runtime` (`Arc<Mutex<Option<Duration>>>`) is stamped once at completion by *every* writer that marks a job finished (normal exit, spawn failure, and `kill`, all via the shared `stamp_runtime`), and `list` reads `runtime.unwrap_or_else(|| started_at.elapsed())`, so only a still-running job reads the live clock. Before this, `/bg list` rendered `started_at.elapsed()` unconditionally and a job that took 0.2s read `✓ done 1h3m` an hour later — the number a user reads as "how long that build took" was "how long ago I started it". The abstention case has its own value rather than borrowing the convenient neighbour: `JobSnapshot.runtime` stays `Option`, and `elapsed_column` prints `--` (`UNKNOWN_ELAPSED`) for a job that is finished but never stamped a runtime (reachable: `finished` is a `Relaxed` store, so a reader can see the flag before the stamp) instead of presenting the live clock as a runtime. `format_elapsed` is unchanged — the formatter was always fine, its input wasn't.
- `commands_spawn.rs` — `/spawn` subagent orchestration: SpawnTracker, worktree isolation (with a symlink pre-flight — `check_worktree_path_escape` refuses `git worktree add` when any existing component of the worktree path is a symlink resolving outside the canonicalized repo root, so worker writes can't escape the repo; in-repo symlinks and fresh non-existent parents pass; when a worktree exists, the worker's bash cwd is pinned to it via `spawn_bash_cwd` → `AgentConfig.bash_cwd` → `StreamingBashTool::with_cwd`, and the worker's **file** tools are confined separately by `dir_restrictions: spawn_dir_restrictions(&agent_config.dir_restrictions, worktree.as_ref())` at both call sites (`handle_spawn` and `handle_spawn_bg`) — the two halves are enforced by different mechanisms and must not be described as one. `bash` follows the worktree by **cwd** (a relative path resolves inside it); `read_file`/`write_file`/`edit_file`/`list_files`/`search` take no cwd, so they still resolve relative paths against the *process* cwd — they are not redirected into the worktree, they are made to **refuse** anything outside it (`maybe_guard`/`maybe_guard_arc` in `tools::build_tools` consult `dir_restrictions`). Before Day 163 (#716) the file tools carried no restrictions at all, so `write_file("src/foo.rs")` inside a spawn worktree silently wrote the **main repo** while `bash` writing the same relative path wrote the worktree. `spawn_dir_restrictions` is pure: `None` (no worktree) → parent's restrictions unchanged, no isolation available and none invented; `Some(wt)` → parent's `allow` plus the worktree root (deduped) with parent's `deny` cloned verbatim, so the common unrestricted-parent case becomes a one-entry allow list = the worktree root, and a human's existing `--allow` list is widened rather than replaced. Enforced default confinement, NOT a sandbox: bare `git` and relative paths under `bash` operate in the worktree, and the git redirection escape class is blocked at the tool layer (`safety::detect_git_redirection_escape`, consulted by `StreamingBashTool` only when a cwd is pinned — refuses `git -C <path-outside-root>`, `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` pointing outside the root, and `GIT_DIR=`/`GIT_WORK_TREE=` env assignments, with an honest error naming what matched; quoted mentions and in-root/relative paths pass). Day 174: that refusal also names **what would be accepted**, via the pure `safety::git_redirection_refusal_message(reason, root, plain)` at the single emission point (`tools.rs`, the only non-test consumer of the detector) — the first sentence is byte-identical to before, and the appended alternatives **branch on the matched class**, decided from the detector's own reason string by `classify_redirection_reason` rather than by a second matcher over the command. Flag refusals (`-C` / `--git-dir` / `--work-tree`) get all three hatches: drop the redirection (bare `git` already operates on the worktree), point it inside (`git -C sub`, `git --work-tree=.` — both accepted today), or hand the work to the parent session. **Env assignments get only the first and third, and that asymmetry is the whole point**: `GIT_DIR=`/`GIT_WORK_TREE=` are blanket-refused *even when the target is relative*, so offering the in-root hatch there would name an alternative that is also refused — the transferred bug class this fixes (Claude Code v2.1.238's worktree-isolation refusals). An unrecognised reason shape falls back to the conservative env branch rather than guessing a hatch. **No bypass flag was added and none exists** — the confinement is deliberate; every listed alternative is already true of the detector and covered by its passing tests.
