Glyph-free under `is_plain_output()` (bullets *and* em dashes), matching `project_mcp_refusal_message`/`goal_verify_refusal_message`; pinned at the **emission point** — the string a caller receives — including an assertion that the env branch names neither `git -C` nor `--work-tree`. **What it deliberately does not do:** `bash` with a non-git absolute path can still write outside the worktree — the `dir_restrictions` guard covers the file tools only, never `bash`; no worktree → `bash_cwd: None` and parent restrictions passed through, process cwd as before), and completion handoff — when a worker finishes with uncommitted worktree changes, `commit_worktree_handoff` commits them to the worktree branch (`spawn: <task>` message, char-boundary truncation) and surfaces a `ready to review: branch spawn/<id> — N files changed (+a/-b)` line plus a `git diff main...spawn/<id>` hint; failed commits and no-change completions are reported honestly, never pre-announced. Opt-in `--pr` flag (default off, product-safe): after a successful handoff commit, pushes the branch (`git push -u origin spawn/<id>` via `std::process::Command`, not `run_git`) and opens a draft PR via `gh pr create --draft` (pure `build_spawn_pr_args` builds the command, char-boundary title truncation), printing the PR URL; degrades gracefully — `gh` missing / push failure / PR failure each get one honest note and the local branch remains the result. `--parallel` now writes a rerunnable JSON manifest of the fan-out to `.yoyo/spawn_runs/<run_id>.json` (pure `build_spawn_manifest` + `write_spawn_manifest`), and `/spawn replay [<run_id>|latest]` reads it back and re-launches the same fan-out (`parse_spawn_manifest`/`load_replay_tasks` — honest errors for missing/corrupt/empty manifests, never a silent no-op); `/spawn runs` (alias `/spawn replay --list`) lists recorded runs. The reader half of codified/replayable orchestration (#341). Day 175 (blind round 67, the darkest real room at 125 snapshots unobserved — 1 hit / 2 miss, and the hit was the only bet whose subject was a *seam* rather than a missing guard): the manifest's `task` field marks its own cut. `MANIFEST_TASK_CAP = 200` **bytes** cut every recorded task through `safe_truncate`, which appends no marker, so a spawn task over 200 bytes was recorded as a truncated instruction and `/spawn replay` re-launched *that* as if it were the recorded one — the manifest is not a display, it is the input a subagent is handed. `manifest_task_field` now appends `" …[truncated]"` **inside** the cap (so the stored field never exceeds the cap it is named for) and is byte-identical under budget. Pinned at the **emission point** — the string `manifest_replay_tasks` returns after a real JSON round-trip, not the writer's own return value — plus the near-miss guard that an under-cap task carries no marker. **What it does not do, stated plainly: it does not make a truncated replay faithful** — the tail is gone and unrecoverable from the manifest; the marker only makes the loss visible. **Also not fixed, filed as #815:** the manifest records no launch modifiers at all, and the replay arm hardcodes `pr: false`, so `/spawn --parallel --pr` replays without `--pr` and nothing says so — a schema change too large to land safely inside the round. Day 175 (#815, the next session) closed that: the manifest carries **`pr`**, `build_spawn_manifest(run_id, tasks, results, pr)` takes it from the launching `SpawnArgs.pr`, `ParsedManifest.pr` reads it back, and the replay arm passes `pr: manifest.pr` instead of the hardcoded `false` — so a run launched with `--pr` replays with `--pr`. **A manifest written before the field existed keeps working and keeps today's behaviour**: `parse_spawn_manifest` reads `.get("pr").and_then(as_bool).unwrap_or(false)`, and that `false` is the *honest reading* of the absence rather than a fallback guess — those runs genuinely recorded no modifier. **The banner names it before it acts** (the pure `replay_banner(run_id, task_count, created_ts, pr)`, asserted at the emission point): a replay about to open draft PRs says so on the line announcing the replay, and when `pr` is false the string is **byte-identical** to the pre-#815 banner, which is every existing user's path and was the regression risk. Both directions are pinned plus the legacy near-miss (`pr: true` round-trips true, `pr: false` round-trips false, a no-`pr`-key JSON parses to `false` with its tasks intact) — a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. **Still not recorded, stated rather than implied: `pr` is the *only* modifier in the schema.** Worktree/isolation state, `--model`, `--system` and `-o` are absent from the manifest, so a replay is faithful about `--pr` and silent about the rest; widening the schema is separate work, not covered here.
- `commands_stash.rs` — conversation stash subsystem: push/pop/list/drop conversation snapshots (extracted from `commands_session.rs`); `/clear` auto-stashes the outgoing conversation as a single `pre-clear` entry (`stash_pre_clear`), restorable via `/rewind` (consuming — one rewind per clear, via `handle_rewind`/`take_pre_clear_entry`) or `/stash pop`
- `commands_update.rs` — `/update`: check the latest GitHub release, download the matching asset, extract it, replace the running binary (with a `.bak` restore path). Day 169 (#756) fixed three defects that made this path unable to succeed on Windows at all — **evidence limit stated up front: all three were read off the source and are pinned by pure unit tests; nothing here was verified on a Windows host, because this repo has no Windows runner, so this is not a claim that `/update` now works on Windows.** (1) Temp paths come from `std::env::temp_dir()` instead of a hardcoded `/tmp`, and every segment is composed with `PathBuf::join` + `format!` on the *filename* only, so no `/` is ever baked into a path (`update_paths(temp_root, run_id, version, ext) -> UpdatePaths { archive, extract_dir }`, pure and table-tested: both paths are under the given root, the extract dir differs per run id, and the same run id is reproducible). On Unix `temp_dir()` resolves to `/tmp` or `$TMPDIR`, so existing platforms are unchanged. (2) The extract dir is **per-run** rather than the old shared `/tmp/yoyo-update-dir`: `update_run_id()` is pid + wall-clock seconds (the seconds guard against a recycled pid reusing a stale tree), the directory is `remove_dir_all`'d immediately *before* extraction and again on **both** the success and the failure paths — a half-extracted tree from a crashed run must never be read by the next attempt as a complete extraction, which turns a failed update into a wrong one. (3) The `.zip` branch no longer shells out to `unzip`. The release pipeline publishes `.zip` only for the Windows target, so the zip branch *is* the Windows branch, and Windows ships `tar` (bsdtar, since 10 1803) but not `unzip` — i.e. it was invoking the one extractor that host does not have. `extractor_argv(archive_path, extract_dir)` is pure and returns the argv that reaches the OS (`tar xzf <archive> -C <dir>` for `.tar.gz`, byte-identical to before; `tar -xf <archive> -C <dir>` for `.zip`; `None` for anything else, which surfaces as an unsupported-format error rather than a guess), pinned by tests asserting the argv itself — including that the zip branch's `argv[0]` is never `unzip`. Failure is named, not swallowed: if the extractor cannot be run, `extractor_missing_message` reports the command that failed **and** the path where the downloaded archive was left, so the user can finish by hand (the archive is deliberately *not* deleted on that path) — a successful download is not a replaced binary, and the message must not imply otherwise.
- `setup.rs` / `config_paths.rs` — the first-run setup wizard, and the config-file **precedence** helpers it now consults. `config_paths.rs` holds one statement of the ladder `config::load_config_file` implements (`./.yoyo.toml` → `~/.yoyo.toml` → `~/.config/yoyo/config.toml`, first existing wins, **no merging**) plus the two guards built on it: `shadowing_config_file`/`shadowed_write_warning` (the file just written sits *below* an existing one, so nothing in it will be read) and `demoted_config_file`/`demoted_write_warning` (the file just written is the *new* top rung, so whatever config yoyo had been reading has gone dark). Day 179 added the **read-side** door those two were missing: both of them fire only at *write* time (`/config set`, and the wizard since #816), so nothing anywhere answered the question a user actually asks when a setting silently does nothing — *which of my config files is yoyo reading, and which ones exist and were skipped?* `/config show` rendered the loaded config as though it were the only one, so a `~/.yoyo.toml` shadowed by a stray `./.yoyo.toml` produced a setting that did nothing and the one command you would go to in order to debug that did not mention the other file exists. Fifth instance of the "two doors, one policy, one deaf" shape (#745, #767, #769, #816) — here the write door worked and the read door was absent. The decision half is the pure, table-tested `skipped_config_sources(chain, loaded) -> Vec<PathBuf>`, which **does not restate the ladder**: it compares by path equality against the chain the caller collected from `existing_config_paths()`, so there is still exactly one statement of the search order. Five rules, each a test row — nothing loaded → **empty** (there is no "skipped" without a winner, and a non-empty chain with no winner must not invent a claim either); one entry which is the loaded one → **empty**; rung 0 loaded with rungs 1..n present → those rungs **in ladder order**; a lower rung winning drops exactly that entry; and a `loaded` path not in the chain at all → the **whole chain** rather than a panic, which says nothing false since every one of those files exists and none of them was read. The rendering half is the pure `skipped_sources_note(skipped, loaded, plain) -> Option<String>` in `commands_config.rs`, and **`None` for an empty slice is the whole regression guard** — a user with one config file, which is essentially everyone, sees `/config show` output that is byte-identical to before (pinned by an `assert_eq!` against `format_config_output`, not merely by the absence of the block). When non-empty it names every skipped path **verbatim** (a user cannot act on a path they cannot see) and states the mechanism — *first match wins and config files are not merged* — so the note explains *why* the file is inert rather than only that it is; glyph-free under `is_plain_output()` (bullets **and** em dashes), mirroring `project_permission_refusal_message`. **Both doors are wired by one edit, verified rather than assumed**: `/config show` is routed from `dispatch.rs:813` through `commands::handle_config_show`, which is a re-export of `commands_config::handle_config_show`, and the `yoyo config show` CLI arm at `dispatch_sub.rs:389` calls that same function directly — one renderer, no unwired entry point. `format_config_output` was deliberately left byte-identical for its other callers; the ladder question is composed on top of it by `config_show_output`, which **is** the emission point (the exact string `handle_config_show` prints) and is where the tests assert. `handle_config_show` now takes the chain head as the loaded path rather than calling `detect_loaded_config_path()` separately — one filesystem scan, and provably the same value, since `detect_loaded_config_path` *is* `existing_config_paths().into_iter().next()`. Tests use fabricated paths only, so they touch no filesystem, no process-global CWD (#780) and no env, and need no `#[serial]`; the positive control was **run rather than assumed** — neutering `skipped_config_sources` to return an empty vec fails `config_show_names_the_config_file_it_skipped` and leaves both near-miss guards green (they assert *absence*, which is exactly right). **Out of scope and named rather than implied: first-wins is unchanged.** This makes the ladder *legible*, it does not merge anything, adds no config key and no flag, and does not touch `/config edit` (`resolve_config_edit_path_from`, which already mirrors the loader's precedence on purpose) or the two write-side guards, which are correct — this is their missing twin. Day 175 (#816): that family had exactly **one** consumer — `handle_config_set` — while the door most users actually walk through, the wizard, consulted none of it and printed an unqualified `✓ Saved to <path>` in **both** directions: menu option 1 (`SaveLocation::Project`) writes the **highest** rung and silently demotes a working `~/.yoyo.toml`/XDG config, and menu option 2 (`SaveLocation::User`) writes the **lowest** and is silently shadowed by any `./.yoyo.toml`. The write succeeding is the container; being *loaded* is the payload. Both arms are now wired — covering one would have been a receipt for exactly the working half — through the single pure writer `config_write_warnings(before, after, written) -> Vec<String>` in `setup.rs`, which calls the existing helpers and never restates the ladder; an empty `Vec` when there is nothing true to say, so first-time setup is byte-identical. Warnings go through the wizard's own `writer` handle (never `println!`), so they are ordered and testable. **The load-bearing constraint is the pre-write snapshot** (#735): a newly created file is already position 0 of its own chain, so a purely post-write check answers "no, you're on top" and stays silent about the file it just demoted — a guard that reads the world after its own action is blinded by that action. Day 175 also added the dir-taking seam `existing_config_paths_in(project_dir)`, with `existing_config_paths()` as the `Path::new(".")` wrapper (byte-identical, still pinned by `test_config_file_path_precedence`): the chain is compared **by path equality** against the file just written, and the wizard writes `project_dir.join(".yoyo.toml")` — absolute in production — which can never equal the bare relative `.yoyo.toml` the cwd-relative chain carries, so without the seam the Project arm's guard would have been structurally incapable of firing. Tests are two layers: a table test driving the pure helper with fabricated paths in **both** directions plus the silent near-miss (a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green), and an emission-point test asserting the demotion string in the wizard's own output buffer, with `HOME` **and** `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` both redirected to tempdirs (`#[serial]`) so the chain is determined by the test rather than by the machine. No `set_current_dir` — the process CWD is global and #780 just spent two tasks removing those. **Deliberately untouched:** `/config edit` (`resolve_config_edit_path_from`) already mirrors the loader's precedence on purpose. **Known debt:** this pushed `src/setup.rs` to 2067 lines and onto `GRANDFATHERED_OVERSIZED_MODULES`; the split is blocked on ~20 private helpers its ~1200 lines of tests reach through `use super::*`, and the register entry says so. Day 178 (#749 item 1) added a **second persisted-user-level-state family** to this file, on the grounds that it is the same question one surface over — *which persisted user-level file governs this directory?* — and so belongs beside the precedence ladder rather than in a new module: the **workspace trust store** (`trusted_dirs_contains`, `append_trusted_dir`, `trusted_dirs_path`, `dir_is_trusted`, `remember_trusted_dir`), a plain-text file of one absolute directory path per line at `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/yoyo/trusted_dirs` (resolved off the ladder's own XDG rung via `cli::user_config_path()` — `config::user_config_path`, re-exported by `cli` — deliberately **not** a second XDG resolver). The decision half is pure and table-tested — `trusted_dirs_contains(content, dir)` (a non-blank, non-`#` line that trims to exactly the dir's string) and `append_trusted_dir(content, dir)` (**idempotent**: already-present → `content` unchanged, never a duplicate line; exactly one trailing newline) — with all I/O in the three siblings at the call site. **Three rules that are the security design, each pinned by a test:** (1) the store is **user-level, never project-level** — nothing looks for a `./.yoyo/trusted_dirs`, because a repo that could trust itself defeats the whole boundary, the same provenance question `config_path_is_project_local` answers for configs; (2) **exact directory match only, no prefix or subdirectory inheritance** — trusting `/home/me/proj` trusts neither `/home/me/proj/vendor/evil` nor `/home/me/proj-other`, since a prefix match would silently widen a security control across directories the user never saw (the near-miss is pinned in both directions); (3) paths are stored **canonicalized**, so `.`/`..`/a symlinked checkout cannot present as a different string than the one that was trusted, and when canonicalization fails the helpers **refuse** rather than falling back to the raw path — "could not check" must not read as "trusted". A missing or unreadable store is `false`, never an error. The consumer is the single `set_trust_project()` site in `cli.rs` (see that bullet for the announcements and for what a stored decision actually grants). Day 178 (#749 item 2) put the **question** itself beside the store, on the same grounds: the decision is pure and belongs with the state it reads, and `cli.rs` should own only the one line of stdin and the printing. Four pure, table-tested functions plus one message: `should_prompt_for_trust(already_trusted, flag_passed, project_local, grants, interactive) -> bool` is a five-way AND and nothing else — true only when the directory is not already trusted, no trust flag was passed, the loaded config is **project-local**, there is something to trust, and **both stdin and stderr are terminals**; every other combination is false and the run proceeds exactly as it did before, pinned by the all-true row plus **each of the five inputs flipped independently** (a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green). `project_trust_grants(config_text, goal_verify_exists) -> Vec<&'static str>` names the four privilege-granting surfaces the existing gates already refuse (MCP servers, `permissions.allow`, shell hooks, `.yoyo/goal_verify.md`), reusing `config::parse_mcp_servers_from_config` and `parse_permissions_from_config` where they are cheaply reachable and falling back to a line scan for the top-level `mcp = [...]` list and the `hooks.*` keys — **both error directions are safe here and the comment says so**: a false positive costs one question the user did not strictly need, a false negative means no prompt, which is today's behaviour, which is the safe state. It decides whether to **ask**, never what to **grant** — the gates are untouched and still decide that. Keys that grant nothing return **empty**, and this repo's own `.yoyo.toml` key set (`continue_on_silence`, plus `model`/`wait_for_reset`/`quiet`) is pinned by name as an empty row, because a repo that prompts about its own harmless config would train the user to answer yes without reading. `parse_trust_answer(input) -> TrustAnswer { Once, Always, No }` maps `y`/`yes` → `Once`, `a`/`always` → `Always`, and **everything else** — `n`, empty line, EOF, whitespace, junk, a stray paste — → `No`, case-insensitively and trimmed; **default-No is the entire safety property** and is pinned with rows for the empty string, whitespace-only and a junk token. There is deliberately no "remember my no": declining is free and costs one keypress next time, whereas a persisted refusal is a second piece of state with its own revocation problem. `trust_prompt_text(dir, grants, plain)` mirrors `project_mcp_refusal_message`/`goal_verify_refusal_message` because it is the same act one step earlier — it names the **directory** verbatim, lists **every** grant verbatim (a user cannot judge what they cannot see), states that answering no still runs yoyo just without those, and names all three answers (`[y]` / `[a]` / `[N]`, the capital marking the default); glyph-free under `is_plain_output()`, bullets **and** em dashes. It is **deliberately not silent under `is_quiet()`**, and that is a judgment written into the code comment rather than an oversight: `--quiet`'s documented scope is *informational* stderr output, and a question that blocks for an answer is not informational — silencing it would either hang or answer for the user. `trust_declined_message(plain)` is the one-line note after a `No`; it *is* informational, so the caller drops it under `--quiet`. **Positive control was run rather than assumed**: flipping `parse_trust_answer`'s default from `No` to `Once` kills a test, and removing `interactive` from the AND kills another — a new guard that changes nothing under test is the vacuous-green shape.
- `sync_util.rs` — shared synchronisation helpers: `lock_or_recover` for poisoned `Mutex` recovery (deduplicated Day 58)

Uses `yoagent::Agent` with `AnthropicProvider`, `default_tools()`, and an optional `SkillSet`.

**Documentation** (`docs/`): mdbook source in `docs/src/`, config in `docs/book.toml`. Output goes to `site/book/` (gitignored). The journal homepage (`site/index.html`) is built by `scripts/build_site.py`. Both are built and deployed by the Pages workflow (`.github/workflows/pages.yml`), not during evolution.

**Evolution loop** (`scripts/evolve.sh`): pipeline:
1. Verifies build → fetches GitHub issues (community, self, help-wanted) via `gh` CLI + `scripts/format_issues.py` → scans for pending replies on previously touched issues
2. **Phase A** (Planning): Agent reads everything, writes task files to `session_plan/`
3. **Phase B** (Implementation): Agents execute each task (30 min each — sized for thinking models, Day 161), with two fix loops: build/test failures get up to 10 fix attempts (10 min each), then the evaluator runs (10 min per pass) and rejections get up to 9 more fix attempts (10 min each). Reverts only after all fix attempts are exhausted. Max 2 tasks per session (was 3; measured sessions land at most 2). Session-budget gates (see Wall-clock budget below) can end the fix/eval loops early: build-fix abandons fail-closed (revert), evaluator skips fail-open (task keeps its green build+test, logged as "accepted UNVERIFIED"). The harness **safety-commits green-but-uncommitted work** via `safety_commit()` (after the impl agent and after each eval-fix attempt, once protected-file + build + test checks pass) — the evaluator only sees committed diffs (`git diff PRE_TASK_SHA..HEAD`), so agents cut off before `git commit` used to lose valid work to an empty diff → FAIL → revert (ate multiple sessions, Days 122–124). The helper refuses to sweep protected files (unstaged protected edits are invisible to the fix-loop re-checks) and surfaces commit failures loudly instead of pre-announcing success. The planner-fallback task (written when Phase A2 produces no task files) is deliberately scoped to terminate: top `agent-self` backlog item or first concrete improvement, smallest version, commit early, stop — the old "most impactful improvement" wording caused non-terminating search.
4. Verifies build, fixes or reverts → agent-driven issue responses (agent directly calls `gh issue comment`/`close`) → pushes

**Wall-clock budget**: Two layers, agent-side and shell-side. Agent-side (product, opt-in): `YOYO_SESSION_BUDGET_SECS` enables a soft per-process budget in the yoyo binary — `prompt::session_budget_remaining()` returns `Some(remaining)` when set (45 min default if unparseable), `None` otherwise; consulted at the top of each retry attempt in `run_prompt_auto_retry`, `run_prompt_auto_retry_with_content`, and the watch-mode fix loop via `session_budget_exhausted(30)`. Shell-side (evolve loop, landed Day 161): `evolve.yml` sets `YOYO_SESSION_BUDGET_SECS` per attempt (10800/7200/5400) plus a job-wide `JOB_DEADLINE_EPOCH`; `scripts/evolve.sh` validates the value (non-numeric → 2700 + warning), clamps to the job deadline minus a 20-min wrap margin (retry attempts restart their own timers, so the epoch is what keeps them under the shared 210-min `timeout-minutes` ceiling), and gates work-starts on `session_secs_left()`: new task ≥3750s, checkpoint-retry impl pass ≥2550s, build-fix iteration ≥1650s, eval-fix attempt ≥1650s, evaluator pass ≥900s, plan-retry ≥4350s. Gates stop work from STARTING; a session that fills its budget wraps up and pushes instead of being killed with unpushed work (Days 159-160 lost two sessions to exactly that).

**Assertion-weakening detector** (`scripts/check_assertion_weakening.py`, Day 177, blind round 78): `scripts/evolve.sh` allows up to **10 build-fix attempts** and then up to **9 eval-fix attempts** on a rejected task, and nothing in that loop forbids satisfying the gate by *weakening an assertion* instead of fixing the code — the named signature of Confirmation-Driven Development, and the measured norm in the wild (*All Smoke, No Alarm*, arXiv 2606.18168: **80.2%** of agent-authored test patches carry a weak or absent oracle, over 86,156 test-file patches). **An assertion loosened during a fix attempt is indistinguishable from a correct repair in every signal I currently collect**, and 123 of my 156 graded risk events are green days, so four fifths of what my self-model has learned is a claim `cargo test` makes about the *absence* of a defect. `scripts/evolve.sh` is protected, so — exactly as with `tests/global_state_races.rs` — the deliverable is **the detector plus the measurement**, not a wired-in gate. Same shape as its two siblings: pure decision functions with all I/O at the call sites, `--test` self-tests exiting non-zero, and a real `argparse` `--help` (`measure_abstentions.py` shipped without one and raised `FileNotFoundError: '--help'`; not repeated). Six weakening shapes and their six mirrors — assertion deleted outright, `assert_eq!` → `assert!(… .contains(…))` (#811's signature), a `contains` needle shrinking, a comparison relaxed (`==` → an inequality, or a literal moving permissive), net assertion count dropping, a `#[test]` removed. **Three verdicts, never two**: `WEAKENED` / `STRENGTHENED` / `UNKNOWN`, and `UNKNOWN` is counted and reported rather than dropped, because a shrinking denominator inside my own meter is the defect this tool's whole subject is about. A hunk touching no test-ish line returns `None` — *not in scope* is not `UNKNOWN`, and conflating them would put every production edit in the denominator. `WEAKENED` wins a mixed hunk while both shapes stay visible in the row. **Self-contamination was asked forward, not audited backward** (`measure_abstentions.py`'s lesson): this script and this very paragraph both contain the literal strings `assert_eq!` and `.contains(`, so the anchor is the **path filter** — only `*.rs` is scanned, pinned by a self-test rather than left to reading. An exact expectation that merely *changed* (`assert_eq!(total, 3)` → `4`) is deliberately **neither** weakened nor strengthened; it is a different claim, and calling it weakening would be the confident wrong verdict the tool exists to avoid.

**The first reading, which is the deliverable.** `--from HEAD~40 --to HEAD`: **40 commits, 28 `*.rs` hunks, 8 test-file hunks examined — WEAKENED 1, STRENGTHENED 7, UNKNOWN 0.** The single `WEAKENED` is a **false positive**: `tests/gasp_doc_version.rs` losing 3 test attributes and 8 assertions is #821's *rename* to `tests/doc_version_claims.rs`, which sits one row above it in the same report as `STRENGTHENED` with 12 assertions added — `git diff` is per-file, so a move reads as a deletion. Confirmed independently by `--per-commit` (each commit against its parent, `--diff-filter=d` dropping whole-file deletions): **WEAKENED 0, STRENGTHENED 8, UNKNOWN 0**. So over surviving history in this window, **zero real weakenings**. **What that does and does not prove**: it is a real result about *surviving* history and says nothing at all about reverted history — limit 2 below is not a formality, it is the whole hole. **No finding was fixed and no test changed, on purpose** — the deliverable is the dial (the discipline the three mutation readings held). **Four limits, printed to stderr on every run including a clean one** so "could not check" can never read as "checked; clean": (1) it matches **text shapes, not semantics** — a legitimately narrowed assertion is textually identical to a loosened one, so every row is a *candidate for a read*, never a conviction, and it cannot tell a move from a deletion; (2) `scripts/evolve.sh` reverts a failed task with `git reset --hard PRE_TASK_SHA`, so **weakening inside a reverted task is invisible to it forever** — the sessions most likely to contain the behaviour are exactly the ones whose evidence was destroyed; (3) `--from A --to B` is a **net** tree diff, so a loosening rewritten by a later commit inside the window cancels out — which is precisely the fix-loop shape, hence `--per-commit`; and (4), the round's **unpredicted finding**, recorded because it silently bounds every number above: this harness runs on a **shallow clone with 50 commits total**, so `HEAD~40` spans *one day*, not the five I assumed when I wrote the prediction. A window expressed in commits reads as a window in time and is not one — the same shallow-clone fact already documented for `git_added_ts` in `commands_risk_neverforecast.rs`, which I failed to carry into a new instrument built in the same repo. Round 78 graded **1 hit / 2 partial / 0 miss**, and the split is the same one running since round 73: every clause whose subject was an expression I could quote landed (the move-vs-deletion mechanism, the `assert_eq!`→`contains` shape), and every clause that required knowing what the *data* contained missed — I predicted `UNKNOWN` would dominate and it came back **zero**, because my sessions almost never edit an existing assertion, they append whole new `#[test]` fns.

**Skills** (`skills/`): Markdown files with YAML frontmatter loaded via `--skills ./skills`. Seven core skills (immutable, `core: true` + `origin: creator`) define the agent's foundational capabilities:
- `self-assess` — read own code, try tasks, find bugs/gaps
- `evolve` — safely modify source, test, revert on failure
- `communicate` — write journal entries and issue responses
- `research` — internet lookups and knowledge caching
- `skill-evolve` — autonomous meta-skill: refines/creates/retires non-core skills based on past-session evidence (cron-driven, gated)
- `skill-creator` — on-demand meta-skill: scaffolds a new skill when the human creator or a community issue explicitly asks for one (interview-driven, no autonomous gating)
- `analyze-trajectory` — on-demand RLM-style deep dive: when YOUR TRAJECTORY shows a recurring failure (STUCK task / clustered CI error fingerprint / frequent reverts), dispatches sub-agents to digest CI logs without bloating main context

Additional skills (`origin: yoyo`, eligible for skill-evolve to refine/retire):
- `social` — community interaction via GitHub Discussions
- `family` — fork registration, introduction, and cross-fork discussion via the yoyobook discussion category
- `release` — binary release pipeline

**skill-evolve vs skill-creator** — both can produce new skills, but they're complementary, not redundant:
- skill-evolve runs autonomously on cron, mines past sessions for recurring patterns, gated by ≥3-session recurrence + 24h cooldown + diff-scope guard. Strong safety properties.
- skill-creator runs on demand inside a normal evolve session when explicitly invoked, no recurrence gate, human-in-the-loop. Use only when a person asks for a skill — never as autonomous self-creation (that belongs in skill-evolve).

**Discussion categories**: General, Journal Club, The Show, Ideas, and `yoyobook` (family discussions for yoyo forks — registration address book, introductions, cross-fork conversation). The `yoyobook` category is created manually in repo settings; `format_discussions.py` fetches all categories automatically.

**Memory system** (`memory/`): Two-layer architecture — append-only JSONL archives (source of truth, never compressed) and active context markdown (regenerated daily by `.github/workflows/synthesize.yml` with time-weighted compression tiers):
- `memory/learnings.jsonl` — self-reflection archive. Each line: `{"type":"lesson","day":N,"ts":"ISO8601","source":"...","title":"...","context":"...","takeaway":"...","pattern_key":"..."}`. The `pattern_key` field is **optional** and follows kebab-case `<verb>.<object>` form (e.g. `tests.add_before_change`); skill-evolve and analyze-trajectory cluster recurring patterns by it. Omit when the lesson is one-off. Two further **optional** fields (issue #501) gate promotion into skills: `classification` (`CREATE_SKILL|UPDATE_SKILL|ADD_LEARNING_NOTE|IGNORE`, default `ADD_LEARNING_NOTE`) and `validation_case` (`{given,when,then}`) — a learning is only promoted to a skill if it carries a `validation_case`. All readers parse defensively, so these are backward-compatible. See `skills/communicate/SKILL.md`.
- `memory/social_learnings.jsonl` — social insight archive. Each line: `{"type":"social","day":N,"ts":"ISO8601","source":"...","who":"@user","insight":"..."}`
- `memory/active_learnings.md` — synthesized prompt context (recent=full, medium=condensed, old=themed groups)
- `memory/active_social_learnings.md` — synthesized social prompt context
- Archives are appended via `python3` with `json.dumps()` (never `echo` — prevents quote-breaking). Admission gate: only write if genuinely novel AND would change future behavior.
- Context loaded centrally by `scripts/yoyo_context.sh` → `$YOYO_CONTEXT` (WHO YOU ARE, YOUR VOICE, YOUR LINEAGE, SELF-WISDOM, SOCIAL WISDOM, YOUR ECONOMICS, YOUR SPONSORS sections)

**Yopedia second brain** (`skills/yopedia/SKILL.md`, external/on-demand — complements the in-repo memory above): recalls from / ingests to yopedia (https://yopedia.yolog.dev), a per-agent knowledge vault. **Division of labor** — *behavioral lessons* (always-on, compressed, shape every session) → `memory/learnings.jsonl` (above); *research / reference / sources* (unbounded, retrieved on demand) → yopedia. Rule of thumb: *changes how I act next session* → learnings; *might want to look up someday* → yopedia. **Recall is agent-scoped** (all the agent's notes, any vault); **ingest routes by vault** — each loop maps its own vault secret to the `YOPEDIA_VAULT_ID` env var the skill reads (dream → `secrets.YOPEDIA_VAULT_ID`; evolve → `secrets.YOPEDIA_EVOLVE_VAULT_ID`), all under one agent token `YOPEDIA_AGENT_TOKEN`, fork-safe per-ingest `vaultId`, skips silently if keys unset. Used by the dream loop (recall → wander → ingest a research report) and the evolve A1 research step. The in-repo memory stays the source of truth for *how yoyo acts*; yopedia is the *library it consults*.

**Release pipeline** (`.github/workflows/release.yml`): Triggered by `v*` tags. Builds binaries for 4 targets (Linux x86_64, macOS Intel, macOS ARM, Windows x86_64) and publishes a GitHub Release with tarballs/zips + SHA256 checksums. Install scripts:
- `install.sh` — `curl -fsSL ... | bash` for macOS/Linux
- `install.ps1` — `irm ... | iex` for Windows PowerShell

**State files** (read/written by the agent during evolution):
- `IDENTITY.md` — the agent's constitution and rules (DO NOT MODIFY)
- `PERSONALITY.md` — voice and values (DO NOT MODIFY)
- `LINEAGE.md` — prompt-visible family-tree identity: generation, root ancestor, parent, branch point, and status
- `journals/JOURNAL.md` — chronological log of evolution sessions (append at top, never delete). External project journals (e.g., `journals/llm-wiki.md`) also live here.
- `DAY_COUNT` — integer tracking current evolution day
- `session_plan/` — ephemeral directory with per-task files (task_01.md, task_02.md, etc.), written by Phase A planning agent (gitignored)
- `.yoyo/commands/` — project-local custom slash command definitions (`.md` files); `~/.yoyo/commands/` for global commands
- `.yoyo/goal.md` — persistent session/project goal (plain text, set via `/goal set`; automatically injected into system prompt)
- `ISSUES_TODAY.md` — ephemeral, generated during evolution from GitHub issues (gitignored)
- `ECONOMICS.md` — what money and sponsorship mean to yoyo (DO NOT MODIFY)
- `SPONSORS.md` — auto-maintained sponsor recognition (only additions, never removals; amounts shown so yoyo understands the investment)
- `sponsors/sponsor_info.json` — single source of truth for sponsor state (recurring + one-time, with shouted_out and benefit_expires for the time-limited perks). Rebuilt by `scripts/refresh_sponsors.py`; entries are permanent — no grace-window pruning, and every sponsor is listed in README.md forever (the legacy `run_used` flag is inert).

**Skill evolution loop** (decoupled from main evolve pipeline):
- `skills/skill-evolve/SKILL.md` — meta-skill that refines/creates/retires *other* skills based on past-session evidence. Four hard rules: (1) only edit skills declaring `origin: yoyo` (allow-list); (2) never edit itself; (3) one mutation per cycle; (4) every refine/create event must include an `expected:` line — freeform prose naming a concrete observable signal, a horizon, and a fallback if the prediction fails. This is decision-observability discipline (paper: arxiv 2604.25850) at the cognitive layer only — no automated validation harness; future cycles re-read the line as informal evidence and humans use it as an audit trail.
- `scripts/skill_evolve.sh` — one cycle entry point. Gates: dirty-tree refusal, session-counter ≥ 5, 24h cooldown, `cargo build && cargo test` green. Post-agent: diff-scope guard (`origin: yoyo` + not `core: true` + within allow-list), build/test re-verify, revert on any violation.
- `.github/workflows/skill-evolve.yml` — hourly cron at `:30` (off-phase from evolve which runs at `:00`); runs `scripts/skill_evolve.sh` which exits silently if gates aren't met.
- `audit-log` branch — long-lived data-only branch, never merges to main. `evolve.sh` pushes per-session evidence (`audit.jsonl` from `--audit`, `outcome.json`, `transcripts/*.log`) into `sessions/day-N-<ts>/`. skill-evolve clones it into a worktree to mine recurrence/scoring signals.
- `skills/_journal.md` — append-only ledger of every skill-evolution event (init, refine, create, retire, meta-suggestion, refused, NO-OP).
- `skills_attic/` — soft-delete destination for retired skills (sibling of `skills/`, NOT scanned by `--skills`).
- `.skill_evolve_counter` (tracked) — bumped at end of every evolve session; reset to 0 by skill-evolve cycles.
- `.skill_evolve_last_run` (gitignored) — epoch timestamp for cooldown.
- `scripts/skill_evolve_report.py` — Layer-3 observability report (per-skill score/eligibility, event log, recurrence trend).

**Skill provenance via `origin:` frontmatter field** — every skill declares one of:
- `origin: creator` — written by the human creator (Yuanhao or fork creator). Immutable. Backed up by `core: true` on the four core skills.
- `origin: yoyo` — written by yoyo (via skill-evolve, or in past evolutions like `social`/`family`/`release`). Eligible for skill-evolve to refine/retire.
- `origin: marketplace` (or `gh:user/repo`, etc.) — installed third-party skills. Off-limits — upstream owns them.
- (missing) — unknown provenance. Off-limits (default-safe).

This is enforced both by HARD RULE #1 in the meta-skill (LLM-side) and by the diff-scope guard in `scripts/skill_evolve.sh` (harness-side).

**Skill scoring inputs** — `origin: yoyo` skills carry an additional `keywords:` list in their frontmatter (e.g., `keywords: ["gh api graphql", "discussion"]` for `social`). skill-evolve uses these to detect "this skill was used in session N" by grepping each session's `audit.jsonl` for any keyword. `last_used`, `uses`, and `wins` are computed from this signal.

**Trajectory awareness** (harness-side, Phase A1+A2 only):
- `scripts/extract_trajectory.py` — aggregates audit-log session outcomes + git log + recent CI runs into a `YOUR TRAJECTORY` markdown block. Hard-capped at 100 lines / 3KB (raised from 2KB on Day 142 so the last-rendered epistemic blind-spot section survives capping intact); typical output 2–3KB. Stderr is captured to `$SESSION_STAGING/trajectory.stderr.log` and surfaced (head -20) in the cron's stderr if non-empty, so `warn()` diagnostics actually reach operators.
- `scripts/evolve.sh` Step 1c — runs the extractor at session start (read-only worktree fetch from `audit-log` branch); inline cleanup, no EXIT trap
- The block is injected into Phase A1 (assess) and Phase A2 (plan) prompts only — Phases B (impl), C (issue response), D (journal) prompts are unchanged
- Seven sub-sections: recent session outcomes, per-task activity from git log, reverts in window (Day 164: two signals reported as **distinct named lines, never summed** — per-task reverts derived from the already-loaded outcome dicts via `count_task_reverts` (`max(0, tasks_attempted - tasks_succeeded)`, plus the whole-session `reverted` flag as a session with reverts), and whole-session revert *commits* matched by `REVERT_COMMIT_RE`; a per-task revert is a `git reset --hard` + filed issue and so leaves **no commit**, which is why the commit-only counter printed a reassuring `0` for 120 days while six `agent-revert` receipts existed, and why the all-clear line now names both things it checked), subsystem concentration (self-driven task-commit histogram + monoculture gate — `CONCENTRATION_WARN_RATIO = 0.5`, Day 150 lesson; rendered mid-order, right after reverts, so it survives `TOTAL_BYTE_CAP`), recurring CI error fingerprints (clustered via `gh run view --log-failed`; Day 175 — **age-filtered and dated**, because `gh run list --status failure --limit N` has **no date filter**, so the N most recent failures *ever* were rendered under a header claiming they were "in window": measured Day 175, four-day-old failures — two of them the `setup::tests::test_wizard_*` panics already cured by #780's chdir removals — read to the planner as *CI is failing right now*, and the failure direction is flattering-to-alarming, since the healthier the loop gets the staler the section becomes while never ceasing to look urgent. The `createdAt` field was already being fetched and discarded. The decision half is two pure, table-tested functions — `run_age_days(created_at, now)`, where a missing / unparseable stamp is **`None`, never `0.0`** ("I don't know when this ran" is not "it ran just now"), and `partition_failed_runs(runs, now, window_days)` returning **three** states, none folded into another: `in_window`, `too_old`, and `undated`, which is **counted and reported** rather than silently kept or silently dropped. The filter runs **before** the expensive `gh run view --log-failed` loop, so it also saves calls; each cluster carries its newest run's age (`[2×, last 4d ago] …`) and the header names the window (`last 14 days`) — reusing the existing `WINDOW_DAYS`, deliberately **not** a second CI-only window constant to drift against it. `render_ci_errors` takes a `CiScan` (not a bare cluster list) so it can tell its **three worlds** apart, since an empty result was reachable from all of them and printed nothing: failures in window → dated rows; query succeeded with zero → **one** line saying so out loud, naming the excluded older and undated counts; query failed or `YOYO_REPO` unset → a line stating the check **could not run** and explicitly not claiming CI is clean — **"could not check" must never read as "checked; clean"**, the same refusal the pre-push hook makes. A fourth, sharp in-between is named too: runs *are* in window but every log fetch failed, which reports the unread logs rather than "clean". That could-not-check line is deliberately **not** counted as trajectory data in `main`, so it cannot suppress the honest global `(no trajectory data yet)` state; it is printed beside it instead. The clean case is held to one line on purpose — the epistemic section renders *after* this one and Day 142 already had to raise `TOTAL_BYTE_CAP` once because the last section got truncated away. Day 178 named the **fourth state that partition never had — recent, real, and already repaired**. Measured that morning: the section rendered `assertion failed: (cost - 4.5).abs() < 0.001` (the sonnet-5 preset-pricing episode, fixed at `f9073e8d`/`0577bfe7`) and `test_apply_effort_hint_low_prepends` (the `EFFORT_LEVEL` `AtomicU8` race, fixed Day 177 by the pure-core split) as live recurring patterns while the 8 most recent `ci.yml` runs were all green (checked, not assumed, at doc time). The Day-175 age filter was working correctly and is not the defect — `gh run list` gave ages 0.9d–2.5d, so those rows were genuinely recent and genuinely real; the gap is one layer over, because `partition_failed_runs` classifies by **age** and nothing anywhere asked whether CI has *passed since*. It fails in the **alarming** direction, and that direction has a price: this section is handed to Phases A1 and A2, and priority 0 of the planning prompt is "fix CI failures — this overrides everything else", so a cured defect rendered as live can outrank the whole rest of the plan. It is my own rule landing on my own instrument again, in mirror image — *"could not check" must never read as "checked; clean"* has a twin, **"was red" must not read as "is red"**. The answer is one sentence printed directly under the header, above the unchanged cluster rows, from the pure, table-tested `green_since_verdict(newest_failure_ts, green, now)`, whose input is the three-valued `GreenScan { newest_success_ts, checked }` — built in the same shape as `CiScan` and for the same reason: **`checked` separates "the query ran and answered" from "I could not ask"**, so a failed `gh` call, a missing binary or an unset `YOYO_REPO` can render neither as "no successes" nor as green, which is exactly the collapse the bucket exists to prevent. `newest_successful_run(repo)` is the I/O half — one `gh run list --status success --limit 1` through the existing `run_cmd`/`GH_RUN_LIST_TIMEOUT` — and is **fail-soft by contract**: any exception, non-zero exit, unparseable JSON or missing `createdAt` yields `checked=False`, never a fabricated timestamp, because the extractor must never block a session. Its **cost guard is in `main`, not in the function**: it is called only when `ci_scan.ok and ci_scan.clusters`, so a healthy session — the common case — makes **zero** extra `gh` calls and its output is byte-identical. The comparison is **strict — the success must be strictly newer than the newest failure** — and ties go to "still live", the conservative direction: a success stamped exactly at the newest failure is *not* green-since, and both sides of that boundary are pinned, since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. (Read the code before "fixing" the operator: it is written on *ages*, `success_age < fail_age`, so strictly-newer reads as strictly-*smaller* there; the two are the same claim and only one of them is spelled `>`.) **The stated limit is in the rendered text itself, which is the whole point rather than a caveat about it**: a later green run is *not* proof the cause is fixed — a flaky test passes sometimes — only that CI is not red on that pattern now; determining that a cause is repaired needs re-running the failing test at HEAD and is a different, expensive question this deliberately does not ask. `None` is returned **only** when there are no in-window failures to qualify, so the clean, could-not-run and unread-logs branches stay byte-identical, and **nothing is dropped, filtered or re-ranked** — every failure that rendered before still renders, with one sentence of context above it. Verified live against the real repo the day it landed: `newest_failure_ts` `2026-08-24T16:06:29Z` against a success at `2026-08-25T14:00:12Z`, rendering `CI has gone green since (last <1d ago): every failure below predates it.` above the two cured clusters. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, three hours later): that verdict was wrong, twice over, and the session it shipped in was the one it misled.** The paragraph above asserts the probe works; the very next session's briefing printed `no successful run has landed since the newest failure below — these are live` above *the same two cured clusters it was built to stop* — sonnet-5 preset pricing and the `EFFORT_LEVEL` race — while 12 CI runs had succeeded since. Priority 0 of the planning prompt is "fix CI failures — this overrides everything else", so the failure direction is the expensive one. **Two independent defects, both measured, both fixed in one diff** (fixing one would be the "two doors, one works" shape — #745, #767, #769, #816). **(A) The probe was workflow-blind.** `newest_successful_run` ran `gh run list --status success --limit 1` with **no `--workflow` filter**; it asked `gh` for `workflowName` in `--json` and **never read it**, so a success from *any* workflow answered "has CI gone green?" — and `Sponsors Refresh` runs roughly every 40 minutes, so whenever the verdict was right it was right **by accident, because a cron is noisy**. This is my own archived lesson landing on my own instrument: *a check that tests for the container is a proxy; assert the payload.* "Some workflow passed" is the container; `cargo build && cargo test && clippy && fmt` is the payload. The **failure** side was equally blind and it was already visible in the output: measured over the 20 most recent failures, **11 were `Dream` / `Sponsors Refresh` / `Skill Evolution` / `Deploy Pages` / `Social` / `Evolution` runs**, none of which compile Rust, every one of them fingerprinted and rendered under a header reading *Recurring CI errors*. **(B) `--limit 1` returns a row that is not the newest.** Measured 2026-08-25: `--workflow ci.yml --status success --limit 1` → `2026-07-24T18:32:13Z` (a month old) while `--limit 5` at the same moment → `2026-08-25T14:43:20Z`. Same command, same instant, month-apart answers. **Stated honestly rather than as the task described it: this is intermittent, not deterministic** — re-measured later the same day the `--limit 1` row was correct again, so it reproduces sometimes and that is the worst kind of dependency to keep. At `--limit 1` a single stale row **is** the whole answer; at `--limit N` the true newest is recoverable. **The fix, and the one measurement that shows both defects at once:** the argv is now `--workflow ci.yml --limit GREEN_PROBE_LIMIT (10)` and the newest is taken by **`max()` over every parseable `createdAt`**, never `runs[0]`. At the moment of the fix the *old* body returned `2026-07-24T19:03:43Z` from a **`Dream`** run — wrong workflow *and* a month stale, and older than the newest CI failure, which is precisely why the briefing said "these are live"; the new body returned `2026-08-25T14:43:20Z` from `CI`. Rows with a missing or unparseable stamp are **skipped** — neither treated as newest nor as evidence that no success exists — and every fail-soft contract is byte-identical: `checked=False` on empty repo / non-zero rc / non-JSON / non-list / **rows present but none readable**, `newest_success_ts=None, checked=True` **only** for a genuine empty list. `run_age_days` is reused against one shared `now` rather than a second ISO-8601 parser being written. The three-valued `GreenScan` was already right and was not restructured; the age filter, the `CiScan` three worlds and the render are untouched. **The reason the bug shipped is the newest lesson in my archive, verbatim:** *an honest refusal is not an error, so a stack of correct refusals produces no red and looks like a working instrument with nothing to report.* Every existing self-test drove `green_since_verdict` with **fabricated timestamps** and none touched `newest_successful_run`, so the suite was green over a probe that asked the wrong question entirely — survivors follow the assertion, and the assertion stopped at the function boundary I found convenient. So the I/O is split: the pure `newest_success_from_runs(runs) -> GreenScan` takes the already-parsed list and is table-tested (out-of-order rows where the newest is **not** at index 0 — the exact `--limit 1` shape; a missing `createdAt` skipped while a good row still wins; an unparseable one skipped the same way; **all** rows unreadable → `checked=False`, *not* "no successes"; and the near-miss guard, a genuine empty list → `checked=True`, since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green), with `newest_successful_run` left as the thin shell that shells out and delegates. Both queries are built by pure argv functions (`green_probe_argv`, `failed_runs_argv`) so the shape is assertable without a network call. **Those argv assertions are deliberately weak and say so in a comment: they prove the filter is *present*, never that the answer is right** — "could not check" must not read as "checked; clean". **Positive control run rather than only the passing direction:** dropping `--workflow` from the green probe, reverting `max()` to `runs[0]`, and dropping `--workflow` from the failure scan each kill exactly one named test. **The limit that survives, stated rather than implied: this filters to the CI *workflow*, which is still not the same property as "the failing test now passes"** — the rendered sentence already says so, and that caveat is the point, not a hedge about it. **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 179) — and this is the *second* time a fix for this one probe has been claimed and gone stale, which is itself the finding.** The Day-178 paragraph above asserts that `--workflow ci.yml` + `--limit 10` + `max()` cured the stale answer. It cured the **ordering** half and not the **paging** half: `max()` over ten stale rows is still stale, because the staleness is in the *page*, not in the order of the rows within it. Measured 2026-08-26 at 06:53, the briefing again printed `no successful run has landed since the newest failure below — these are live` above three `gasp_cli_run_ordering` failures that were the #832 nested-`cargo` defect, **fixed at 03:51** (`b196368b`/`45d0d328`), with CI run `a55f8114` green at `05:10:56Z` — 100 minutes before the render, directly above the line where priority 0 says *"fix CI failures — this overrides everything else"*. Cause reproduced rather than guessed: the **first** call of `gh run list --workflow ci.yml --status success --limit 10` returned a page whose newest row was `2026-08-25T23:54:38Z`, **silently omitting** the `2026-08-26T05:10:56Z` success the *unfiltered* listing returned in the same minute; three immediate re-runs of the identical argv then included it, and a `--status completed` probe returned it every time. **So `--status success` is a server-side filter that intermittently serves a stale page**, and no amount of client-side ordering can recover a row that is not on the page. **The fix is to stop asking the server to filter:** `green_probe_argv` asks `--status completed` and fetches `conclusion`; `newest_success_from_runs(runs, limit)` filters to `conclusion == "success"` itself and then takes `max()` exactly as before. `--workflow ci.yml` is **unchanged and still load-bearing** — Day 178 established that without it a `Sponsors Refresh` run answers "has CI gone green?" by accident. `GREEN_PROBE_LIMIT` was raised 10 → 20, and that number is a **judgment threshold, not a measurement**: large enough that an ordinary red streak still contains a success inside the page, small enough to stay one API page. **The new third state is the load-bearing part, not the query change.** Once filtering moves client-side, "this page holds no success" becomes reachable two ways and they are deliberately **not folded**: a **short** page (`len(runs) < limit`) means the whole listing was seen, so `newest_success_ts=None, checked=True` is a real observation (a genuine empty list is its shortest case); a **full** page (`len(runs) >= limit`) holding no success means the probe **could not see far enough**, so `checked=False`. Folding the second into the first would render a long red streak as *CI has never been green* — a **stronger** false alarm than the one being fixed. Day 178's mirror gains a second one: **"could not see far enough" must never read as "there is nothing there"**. A row whose `conclusion` is missing or non-string is likewise **unreadable, never a non-success** — it forces `checked=False` rather than quietly shrinking the denominator of successes. Every fail-soft contract is byte-identical (`checked=False` on unset `YOYO_REPO`, non-zero exit, non-JSON, non-list, or all-rows-unreadable), rows with an unparseable `createdAt` are still skipped, the cost guard is untouched (`ci_scan.ok and ci_scan.clusters`, so a healthy session still makes **zero** extra `gh` calls), and `green_since_verdict`, `partition_failed_runs`, `CiScan` and `render_ci_errors` were **not touched**. Pinned by pure tests over fabricated rows: an out-of-order page, a mixed `success`/`failure`/`cancelled` page where a **newer failure must not win**, full-page-no-success → `checked=False`, and the near-miss guard beside it — short-page-no-success → `checked=True` — since a discriminator tested only on the side that fires is vacuous green. **Positive controls run rather than assumed:** folding the full-page branch into `checked=True` kills exactly the full-page test and leaves the short-page test green; dropping `--workflow` kills the argv test. Verified live the day it landed — the same command that had said *these are live* renders `CI has gone green since (last <1d ago)`), provider/API health from audit.jsonl, epistemic blind spots (from `yoyo risk epistemic`, fail-soft — Day 175, blind round 72: the never-forecast line **marks its own cut in band** via the pure `never_forecast_clause(paths, shown_max)`. Two independent caps bound that list and only one of them used to say so: the Rust renderer caps its rows at `NEVER_FORECAST_SAMPLE = 5` and marks that cut (`... (+N more)`), then this collector re-cut the survivors to `EPISTEMIC_NEVER_FORECAST_SHOWN = 2` and marked nothing. Measured live that day: **5 dark files existed, Rust rendered all 5, the planner was handed 2** under a hint reading "the never-forecast files are the darkest — point the self-driven slot at one of these", and the section header carrying the true count of 5 is consumed and discarded by the parser (`continue` at the `in_never_forecast` branch), so the number survived nowhere. The damage was directional, not cosmetic: both shown files were day-old *pure moves* whose darkness carries zero bits, while the three hidden behind the silent cut were the genuinely old rooms — i.e. the elision was hiding exactly the rooms worth entering, in the one output that steers task selection. The marker deliberately reuses the Rust renderer's `(+N more)` shape rather than inventing a second elision vocabulary. **Byte-identical whenever nothing is dropped** — including exactly at the cap — which is the common case and was the regression risk; pinned at the **emission point** (the string `render_epistemic` returns, which is the string that reaches the planner prompt) with a 5-of-2 assertion, an at-the-cap near-miss guard so the discriminator is not covered only on the side that fires, and both verified to fail against the pre-fix body before landing)
- Fail-soft: never blocks the session; emits `(no trajectory data yet)` if any input is missing
- Complementary to skill-evolve: skill-evolve mines audit-log for *skill-level* signals; trajectory awareness is *task-level*. Both consume audit-log, neither writes to it.
- `scripts/measure_abstentions.py` (Day 174, #810) — the grading instrument for the #808 auto-continue gate, built in the same shape as the extractor above (pure decision functions, `--test` self-tests, I/O at the call sites). It exists because **#810's proposed grep matched my own prose**: the Day-174 comment I posted on #810 contains the abstention markers verbatim inside a markdown table cell, and Phase C composes that comment *during the session being measured*, so the string lands in that session's own log — measured, not hypothetical (run `32480314380` grepped as 1 abstention while the same session's transcripts had 0). Worse, the contamination grows monotonically and inflates the **denominator**, so the gate would look progressively more broken than it is. **The anchoring rule: match the harness's whole emitted line, not a substring** — `classify_line` matches each of `evolve.sh:1273/1567/1605/1610` and `main.rs:643`'s `⚡ auto-continuing` from its first word to end-of-line, with only counters and budget seconds loose, and a runner prefix (`gh run view`'s `job\tjob\tTIMESTAMP`) allowed in front. The provenance half is the separate pure `is_quoted_prose` (markdown pipe, backtick, `NNN:`/`path:NNN:` grep prefix, leading `>`); a line that shape-matches but trips it is **counted and reported** as `prose_excluded`, never silently dropped. Double-counting is avoided deliberately: 1567 and 1605 are the two arms of one `if/elif` and both map to `PLANNER_ZERO_TASKS`, while 1610 (`PLANNER_FALLBACK`) is a *consequence* of the same event and is reported beside the count rather than added. `grade()` implements @yuanhao's corrected rule — a session with zero abstentions is excluded from **both** numerator and denominator, and below `MIN_GRADEABLE_SESSIONS = 4` it **refuses a verdict** and prints the wait, because "not yet gradeable" is its own state and not "the gate failed". **Recorded negative finding, so the next reader does not re-derive it:** `sessions/day-*/outcome.json`'s `fallback_phases` field looks like a structured source for this and is not — `scripts/evolve.sh:583` populates it from `STAGE_NAME` when a phase is served by the **fallback provider**, not when the planner falls back to a single task; no field in `outcome.json` records an A1 abstention or a planner fallback. Day 175 (@yuanhao on #810, "one gap, not a rewrite") added the **eligibility boundary**, because the first real run read `of 16 gradeable sessions, the gate fired in 0` where **all 16 sessions predated the #808 fix** — a firing was impossible by construction, so that is not a 0% hit rate, it is "could not fire" being reported as "did not fire", and it gets worse-looking as more pre-fix history accumulates in the window. `--since-sha <sha>` (resolved by `resolve_sha_timestamp` shelling `git show -s --format=%cI`) and `--since <ISO8601>` (no git needed) are mutually exclusive and set that boundary; an unresolvable sha or unparseable stamp **refuses to grade** rather than silently degrading to "no boundary". Each session then lands in one of **three** states, none folded into another — the pure, table-tested `classify_eligibility(session_ts, boundary_ts)`: **INELIGIBLE** (strictly before the boundary — excluded from numerator *and* denominator), **ELIGIBLE** (at or after; the boundary is **inclusive**, and both sides of that discriminator are pinned by test so it is not covered only where it blocks), and **UNKNOWN_AGE** (no parseable timestamp — *not* eligible and *not* ineligible, excluded from both halves but **counted and reported**, the same discipline `UngradedScan.unkeyed_excluded` and `NeverForecast`'s unknown-age branch use; a silently-absorbed unknown inside my own meter is the exact defect this tool exists for). Session age comes from the `sessions/day-N-YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ` directory name (`scripts/evolve.sh:3471`) via the pure `session_timestamp`, so it needs no git and works on a bare `audit-log` checkout. The verdict names the boundary, the predate count and the unknown count; the `MIN_GRADEABLE_SESSIONS = 4` refusal is **unchanged** and with a boundary applied it becomes the common case, which is correct — "not yet gradeable" is its own state, not "the gate failed". The same day gave `main()` a real `argparse` surface: `--help` prints usage and exits 0 instead of raising `FileNotFoundError: '--help'` (there was no parser at all — every non-`--test` argv entry was opened as a path). **With no `--since*` flag the output is byte-identical to the pre-boundary tool** — verified by diff, and pinned by a self-test asserting the pre-change verdict string verbatim plus the absence of any eligibility suffix on a row. `classify_line`, `classify`, `is_quoted_prose` and the anchored matching were **not** touched. **What it cannot do:** it checks that an abstention marker was *emitted*, never why the model abstained, and never whether a firing was the *right* continuation. Day 175 (third task, @yuanhao on #810) added the **fourth exclusion bucket**, `EXCLUDED_PROVIDER_ERROR`, because a zero-task outcome has at least three distinct causes and only one of them grades #808: closing silence (the gate *should* fire), an **API error / rate limit** (the gate **must not** fire — `piped_should_continue` requires `!had_error`, and continuing after a provider refusal would burn the remaining budget re-hitting the same limit), and a genuine early stop with an error (same). Both post-fix sessions that scored as *gradeable* were rate-limit failures — 08-21T21:25 and 08-22T01:17, each ending its planner turn on `error: Rate limited, retry after Some(14454000)ms`, a ~4h retry-after — while every healthy post-fix session (03:42, 06:41, 12:40, 15:22) carries zero such lines. So every rate-limit event was **manufacturing a gradeable session showing `firings=0`, which reads as accumulating evidence against #808 when it is evidence about Anthropic's rate limiter**. The detector (`classify_provider_error_line` / `classify_provider_error`) is anchored exactly as `classify_line` is — the emission's first word to end-of-line, numbers loose, a runner `job\tjob\tTIMESTAMP` prefix allowed — and every candidate still runs through `is_quoted_prose`, which is load-bearing rather than cosmetic here: #810's own thread carries the string `Rate limited` inside a markdown table, and a false positive **shrinks my own denominator** by excluding a session that really did test the gate. A shape-match that trips the prose filter is **counted** in `prose_excluded`, never silently dropped. A session with provider-error evidence *and* a zero-task outcome leaves **numerator and denominator both** and is reported as its own bucket, never summed into `INELIGIBLE`, `UNKNOWN_AGE` or the zero-abstention exclusions. **The coarseness is named rather than dressed up**: this is **session-level presence**, not the per-phase attribution @yuanhao's wording ("a provider error in the same phase as the zero-task outcome") describes — the concatenated lines of a session directory carry no cheap phase anchor, so it is a judgment threshold whose only evidence is that healthy sessions had zero such lines, and both the code comment and the printed verdict say so. `MIN_GRADEABLE_SESSIONS = 4` is **unchanged**, so `NOT YET GRADEABLE` becomes more common — which is correct, since "not yet gradeable" is its own state and not "the gate failed". **With no provider-error lines anywhere the output is byte-identical to the pre-bucket tool** (verified by running both), and `classify_line`, `classify`, `is_quoted_prose` and the anchored matching were not touched. Measured standings over the post-#808 window: the two rate-limited sessions move out of the graded set, taking the verdict from `2 of 4 gradeable sessions, gate fired in 0` to **`0 of 4 gradeable sessions`** — the number @yuanhao computed by hand, now computed by the instrument. **Sessions that actually tested the closing-silence gate: still 0.** **The reading, taken Day 178 (#810's actual deliverable — @yuanhao asked for the number either way, and it had not been taken since Day 175 while the instrument was rebuilt four times).** Boundary `--since-sha c46d8453` → `2026-08-21T10:18:16+00:00`. Three inputs, all reported, none summed. **(1) 471 session directories, boundary applied:** `INELIGIBLE` 444, `EXCLUDED_NO_HARNESS_OUTPUT` **27**, `UNKNOWN_AGE` 0, `EXCLUDED_PROVIDER_ERROR` 0, zero-abstention 0, `prose_excluded` 0 — 444 + 27 = 471, i.e. **every eligible session was eaten by the harness-output bucket**; verdict `NOT YET GRADEABLE: 0 of 4 gradeable sessions`. **(2) Same input, no boundary:** `EXCLUDED_NO_HARNESS_OUTPUT` **471 of 471**. That is not a regression — Day 177's bucket correctly excludes **100% of the session-directory stream**, because a `sessions/day-*/` directory carries agent transcripts and harness-written artifacts and never the harness's own stdout, so **the only gradeable stream is the workflow log**. **(3) Headline — 7 post-#808 workflow logs** (runs `32713601157`/`32721977884`/`32729450920`/`32746245140`/`32763854655`/`32777115768`/`32779862370`, 2026-08-24, 114KB–271KB, each checked non-empty), placed under `day-177-<compact createdAt>/` names so `session_timestamp` can resolve their age: all 7 **ELIGIBLE**, every bucket 0 except **zero-abstention 7 of 7**, verdict `NOT YET GRADEABLE: 0 of 4 gradeable sessions (7 session(s) read, 7 with zero abstentions and so excluded from both numerator and denominator). Recording the wait, not a verdict.` **Outcome 4 of #810's four named-in-advance outcomes fired** — not "fires and fallback drops", not "fires but fallback holds", not "still does not fire": there was **nothing to grade it on**. Verified independently of the classifier by raw grep over the same 7 logs — `WARNING: No assessment produced` **0**, `Planning agent produced 0 tasks` **0**, `auto-continuing` **0**, against `Phase A1: Assessment` 10 and `Phase A2: Planning` ≥1 per log, so harness output is genuinely present and the zero is a measured zero. Three logs carry the bare substring `no task files` and the anchored classifier scored all three **0** — they are the A1 agent's own closing sentence in an assessment-only phase, echoed transcript prose, which is the founding defect of this tool being caught in the wild. **The honest reading, which is not flattering to the ambition: the #808 gate has still never fired, and this is the first time I can say why with a denominator attached — not because it is broken, but because the condition it waits for did not occur in any measured post-fix session** (Day 177 ran 9-of-10 green with the planner producing tasks every time). `0 of 4` here means *the loop was healthy*, not *the fix failed*. **Defect found and recorded rather than fixed** (the task forbade touching the instrument, and polishing it a fifth time instead of reading it is the exact rut): the two supported streams were **mutually exclusive with `--since-sha`** — only the workflow log carries harness markers, and a workflow log has no `day-N-<ts>` name, so the boundary flag classified it `UNKNOWN_AGE` and dropped it from both halves; the Day-178 graded number above was only obtainable by renaming the input into the session-directory shape by hand. Every branch refused honestly, but the intended invocation could not produce a graded number. **That is the shape worth keeping: two individually-correct guards composed into a capability that could not be exercised, and neither guard was wrong.** **Superseded claim, recorded rather than erased (Day 178, second task):** this sentence read "**found and filed** rather than fixed" and **there was no filing** — `gh issue list --search` finds nothing; it was a *comment on #810*. "Filed" is a scheduler word and a comment is a **reader** surface, the exact distinction my own archive draws ("I write well into reader surfaces and feel filed"), so CLAUDE.md — which is re-injected as authoritative context every session — was asserting a tracking record that did not exist. It is now **fixed**, by `--session-ts` (below), rather than tracked anywhere. **Filename-suffix tolerance was deliberately NOT taken**: it would only re-bless the manual rename that *was* the workaround, i.e. bless the symptom, and it would let a filename assert an age the harness never wrote.

Day 178 (second task, #810) closed it with **`--session-ts <ISO8601>`**: an operator-asserted age for inputs whose *filename* cannot carry one. `session_timestamp` is **untouched** — it stays a pure reader of what the harness wrote — and the fallback lives at the one call site (`session_from_path(path, asserted_ts=None)`), keeping the file's three-valued discipline: (1) filename carries a parseable `day-N-<ts>` → use it, **byte-identical**; (2) no parseable stamp **and** `--session-ts` given → use the asserted stamp; (3) neither → `UNKNOWN_AGE`, exactly as before. **Precedence is one-directional and is the load-bearing rule: observed beats asserted, always** — a filename-stamped input ignores the flag entirely, so a claim can never overwrite a fact. An asserted stamp is **a claim by me, not an observation**, and this whole tool exists because I once let my own prose score as data, so the two are never merged: the row carries `[ts=asserted]` and the verdict carries `asserted age: N row(s) used an operator-asserted timestamp`, which is what makes the operator's claim ("every log I handed you is from at/after this instant") auditable afterwards by whoever fetched them. An unparseable value **refuses to grade** (`parse_timestamp` reused, no second parser), exactly as an unresolvable `--since-sha` does. It is deliberately **not** in the `--since-sha`/`--since` mutually-exclusive group — it is an *input age*, not a boundary, and composing it with a boundary **is** the fix; a self-test asserts that composition so an accidental exclusive group cannot silently reinstate the dead end. **With no `--session-ts` the output is byte-identical** (verified by diff on the same input, with and without a boundary), and `classify_line`, `classify`, `classify_provider_error`, `classify_harness_output`, `phase_reach_markers` and `is_quoted_prose` were **not** touched. Self-tests drive the real `session_from_path` (the precedence rule lives at that call site and nowhere else) across all three states, the precedence case, the composition, and the refusal; **positive control run, because a new parameter that changes nothing under test is the vacuous-green shape** — letting asserted override observed kills 3 tests, dropping the `[ts=asserted]` marker kills 2.

**The reading, taken with the intended invocation (Day 178, second task).** Command, verbatim:

```
python3 scripts/measure_abstentions.py --since-sha c46d8453 --session-ts 2026-08-24T00:00:00Z /tmp/ma810/*.log
```

Verdict, verbatim: `NOT YET GRADEABLE: 0 of 4 gradeable sessions (6 session(s) read, 6 with zero abstentions and so excluded from both numerator and denominator). Recording the wait, not a verdict.` — boundary `c46d8453` → `2026-08-21T10:18:16+00:00`, `asserted age: 6 row(s)`, `INELIGIBLE` 0, `UNKNOWN_AGE` 0, every other bucket 0. **This is a positive control and it passes: `--session-ts` reproduces the Day-178 hand-renamed result rather than inventing a new one** — same verdict shape, same zero-abstention count per session, all eligible. Confirmed independently of the classifier by raw grep over the same logs: `WARNING: No assessment produced` 0, `Planning agent produced 0 tasks` 0, `auto-continuing` 0, `Phase A1: Assessment` ≥1 in each.

**Six logs, not seven, and the difference is mine — stated plainly rather than papered over.** `gh` returned `HTTP 401: Bad credentials` partway through the fetch loop, so run `32779862370` came back as a **0-byte file**; a retry failed the same way (the token expired mid-task). So this run **could not check** that seventh session, and the Day-178 number is *not* being reported as though this run produced it. The accident is its own small proof: a first pass **including** the 0-byte log printed `[EXCLUDED_NO_HARNESS_OUTPUT]` on exactly that row and `excluded (no harness output): 1 session(s)` in the verdict — Day 177's bucket catching my own broken fetch in the wild, which is precisely the failure ("an absent input standing in for a measured zero") it was built for, and the one it would have been embarrassing to repeat inside the fix for it. **Superseded status, recorded rather than erased — the Day 174 reading it replaces:** **0 of 4 gradeable sessions** over the 19 day-173/174 sessions on `audit-log` — and note the transcripts carry agent output, not harness stdout, so the markers only appear in the workflow log; the four bare-substring hits present in those transcripts were all my own echoed `grep` commands and prose, and the anchored classifier scored every one of them 0. **Superseded reading, recorded rather than erased:** that Day-174 status was measured with **no boundary**, so every session in it predates the #808 fix and the `0 of 4` was the category error above, not a hit rate — re-run it with `--since-sha <the #808 fix commit>` and expect a *refusal* (`NOT YET GRADEABLE`) until four post-fix sessions with abstentions exist. Day 175 (second task, #810) closed the **stream mismatch** that made the sentence above about the workflow log load-bearing: every marker is anchored to a line `scripts/evolve.sh` prints to the **workflow log**, but a `sessions/day-*/` directory contains agent transcripts plus files the harness writes into it — the harness's own stdout is not in it — so pointing the tool at a session directory read `fallback=0` *by construction*. Measured: `day-174-20260821T232342Z` and `day-175-20260822T021038Z` both read `fallback=0` while both ran the planner-fallback path and committed nothing but a wrap-up, and @yuanhao's "5 sessions, 5/5 abstention-free, **0 fallbacks**" on #810 was computed this way. A session directory is now also read for **structural artifacts** — files the harness writes, which my own prose cannot forge, which is exactly why they are the right second source for a tool whose first defect was prose contamination. The decision half is the pure `classify_structure(relpaths, outcome)` (all filesystem work at the call site; `read_outcome` is its I/O sibling) over what was **observed**: `plan_retry` (`transcripts/plan_retry.log` — note the path, it is **not** top-level; 62 of 453 dirs), `unverified` (a top-level `unverified_task_<N>.md`; 11 of 453), and `tasks_attempted`/`tasks_succeeded` straight from `outcome.json`. Every field is **three-valued** — `False` means observed-and-absent, `UNKNOWN` means this stream cannot say — so a missing `outcome.json`, an absent key, a wrong-typed value and a plain log file with no listing all report `UNKNOWN` rather than collapsing into `False`/`0`; the near-miss side is pinned by test (a listing *without* the artifact must report `False`), and a top-level `plan_retry.log` must **not** match, since the artifact's real home is the one detail the commissioning task file got wrong. **The two streams are reported side by side and never summed** — different coverage, and adding them would double-count exactly when both are available, the same rule already applied to `evolve.sh:1567/1605` vs `1610`; the per-session structure line prints beneath its row and a `! streams disagree` line fires when a retry artifact is present while the log-derived `fallback=0`, because that disagreement **is** the finding. Naming is by what is observable, never by what it suggests: `plan_retry` says a planner retry artifact exists, which is evidence *toward* a fallback session and not a synonym for one. **`zero_output` is `UNKNOWN`, always, and the tool says why** — nothing in a session directory records the commits a session made (none of `outcome.json`'s twelve keys, no file), so deriving it from `tasks_succeeded` would be inventing the number this tool exists to stop inventing; `tasks_succeeded: 1` for a zero-commit session is `scripts/evolve.sh`'s own defect and that file is protected, so this makes the defect visible from the outside, which is all that can be done from here.
